Part OneIntroduction, Scope and Method

1.1 What this document is

This is the complete build specification for TechStar Store, an online shop selling STEM kits, educational electronics and electronic components, parts, tools and instruments. It defines the product, the user experience, the visual system, the database, the API, the business rules, the administration console and the operational plan in enough detail that an engineering team can implement the platform without needing to invent behaviour.

The brief was explicit: build a store that matches bafredo.co.tz feature-for-feature and screen-for-screen, rebranded as TechStar Store, on React and MySQL, with an administration area that can create, read, update and delete every object in the system. This document is written to that brief.

Every statement about the reference platform in Part Two is derived from direct observation of the live site — its JavaScript bundles, its rendered DOM, its stylesheets and its public API responses — not from assumption. Where the reference platform's behaviour could not be observed because it sits behind an authenticated wall, this document says so explicitly and states the design decision TechStar will take instead.

Reading order Parts 1–3 are for everyone: they define what is being built and why. Parts 4–8 are for designers and frontend engineers. Parts 9–13 are for backend engineers. Part 14 is for whoever builds the admin console — it is the largest single chapter because the brief asks for total CRUD coverage. Parts 15–22 are cross-cutting. Part 23 is for whoever plans the work. The appendices are lookup tables you will return to during implementation.

1.2 What was built versus what was researched

No application code has been written. This document is the deliverable. What was produced alongside it is a research archive, stored next to this PDF, containing the raw evidence behind every claim in Part Two:

ArtefactContents
research/api/*.jsonLive responses captured from the reference platform's public API: category list, subcategory list, product samples, grouped-product samples, banners, services, partners, shipping methods, currency configuration, region and district lists, order statistics, and product counts.
research/screenshots/*.pngFull-page renders of the reference storefront at desktop (1440 px) and mobile (390 px) widths: home, catalogue, product detail, checkout, about, contact.
research/ui/source-ui-label-inventory.txtEvery user-facing string extracted from all 65 lazy-loaded JavaScript chunks — screen titles, field labels, button text, validation messages, empty-state copy, toast messages and confirmation prompts. This is the source for Appendices B and C.
research/ui/source-home-dom-outline.txtA structural outline of the fully rendered home page DOM (2,732 nodes), used to derive the exact composition of the header, mega-menu, product card, off-canvas panels and footer.

1.3 How the reference platform was analysed

The reference site is a client-rendered single-page application. Its served HTML is an empty shell, so ordinary page fetching reveals nothing. Five techniques were used in combination.

1.3.1 Bundle extraction

The application ships one entry bundle (app.js, 144 KB), one vendor bundle (2.0 MB) and 65 lazily loaded route chunks (2.4 MB total). The webpack chunk manifest inside the entry bundle maps numeric chunk IDs to content-hashed filenames; that manifest was parsed to enumerate and download all 65 chunks. Because production Vue builds preserve string literals verbatim, every route path, API path, form label, validation message and toast string survives minification intact.

1.3.2 Route and store recovery

Vue Router route records and Pinia store definitions were located by pattern in the entry bundle and extracted with brace-matched slicing, yielding 14 complete store definitions with their state shapes and action bodies. Because each action contains its own HTTP call, the store bodies double as an API contract.

1.3.3 API surface reconstruction

All calls flow through one helper of the form request(path, method, body). Matching that call signature across the entry bundle and all 65 chunks recovered 90 distinct endpoint/verb pairs, including path parameters and query strings, listed in full in §2.3.

1.3.4 Live API probing

The recovered public read endpoints were called directly. This produced real response payloads with real field names, types and nesting — the difference between guessing a schema and knowing one. Authenticated endpoints returned 401 and were not pursued; no credentials were used, no accounts were created, and no write operation was ever sent.

1.3.5 Headless rendering

Key pages were rendered in headless Chrome with a 20-second virtual-time budget, then captured both as full-page screenshots and as complete post-hydration DOM dumps. The DOM dumps give exact element nesting, utility classes, ARIA attributes and copy; the screenshots give proportion, rhythm and colour.

Scope of observation The reference platform's administration console at /dashboard/* is behind authentication and was never entered. Everything Part 14 says about administration screens is reconstructed from the shipped client code for those screens — table column headers, form fields, modal titles, validation messages, bulk-action confirmation prompts and empty states are all recovered strings. What could not be recovered is server-side admin behaviour: exact permission rules, audit trails, and any admin-only response fields. Those are specified fresh in this document as TechStar's own design, and are marked as such.

1.4 Assumptions and decisions register

Where the brief left a choice open, a decision was made rather than a question asked, so that implementation is not blocked. Each is recorded here with its rationale and the alternative that was rejected. Any of these can be reversed; the cost of reversal is noted.

#DecisionRationaleAlternative & reversal cost
D1Server runtime is Node.js 20 + Express 4 + TypeScriptThe brief fixed React and MySQL but not the server. One language across client and server halves the hiring surface, allows shared validation schemas and shared TypeScript types, and matches the reference platform's own Node/Mongo lineage so behaviour maps cleanly.PHP 8 + Laravel 11, which would suit the XAMPP install already present on the workstation. Reversal is moderate: the data model, API contract, business rules and all UI work in this document are runtime-neutral and carry over unchanged. Appendix H gives the Laravel mapping.
D2Data access via Prisma ORM over MySQL 8Declarative schema doubles as living documentation, migrations are generated and reviewable, and the generated client is fully typed. Raw SQL remains available for the reporting queries in Part 18 where ORM query builders are a poor fit.Knex query builder or raw mysql2. Low reversal cost — Appendix E gives the complete hand-written DDL, so the schema is not Prisma-dependent.
D3Build tool is Vite, not Next.jsThe reference product is a client-rendered SPA with an admin console behind auth; server-side rendering buys little for the admin half and adds hosting complexity. SEO for the catalogue is addressed by prerendering the public routes (§21.4) rather than by adopting a full SSR framework.Next.js App Router for SSR/ISR on catalogue pages. Moderate reversal cost, best decided before Phase 2. If organic search is the primary acquisition channel, revisit this early.
D4Styling is Bootstrap 5.3 + SCSS tokens, consumed through React BootstrapThe reference UI is built on Bootstrap 5.2 utilities. Keeping the same primitive vocabulary means the layouts in Part 7 translate one-to-one, and the grid, off-canvas, modal, toast, accordion and dropdown behaviours all come for free and already match.Tailwind CSS. High reversal cost mid-project, negligible if decided now. Tailwind would require every layout in Part 7 to be re-expressed.
D5Client state is Zustand + TanStack QueryZustand is the closest React analogue to the reference platform's Pinia stores, so the 14 recovered store shapes port directly. TanStack Query handles server cache, retries and invalidation, which the reference platform hand-rolls (and gets wrong in places — see §2.7).Redux Toolkit. Low reversal cost; more boilerplate for the same result.
D6Product variants modelled as first-class rows in a variant table, not as separate product rowsThe reference platform creates one product document per attribute combination and re-groups them at read time by name string. This is the root cause of several defects catalogued in §2.7. A proper parent/variant split fixes them without changing any screen.Copy the reference model exactly. Not recommended; see §2.7 and §12.2 for the specific failures it causes.
D7Images stored on local disk behind a storage adapter, with S3 and Cloudinary adapters providedKeeps development and first production deployment simple and cheap, with no vendor lock-in. The adapter interface in §15.3 means switching to object storage later is a configuration change, not a rewrite.Cloudinary from day one, as the reference platform uses. Low reversal cost either way, by design.
D8Payments launch with manual mobile-money (Lipa Namba) plus cash on delivery, with an aggregator adapter readyThis is exactly what the reference platform does, and it requires no merchant integration to go live. The payment adapter interface in §13.3 means adding Selcom, ClickPesa, AzamPay or DPO later is an implementation of one interface plus a webhook route.Aggregator-first. Higher upfront cost and a commercial onboarding dependency; better as Phase 4.
D9Currency is TZS with zero fraction digits, configurable at runtimeMatches the reference platform's live currency configuration and Tanzanian retail convention. Money is stored as integer minor units regardless, so a future multi-currency change is additive.Fixed hard-coded currency. Trivial difference; runtime configuration is specified because the admin screen for it exists in the brief.
D10Interface language is English, with Swahili strings externalised from day oneThe reference platform is English throughout except its mobile-money payment instructions, which are Swahili. Externalising strings costs almost nothing now and avoids a full retrofit when Swahili is added.Hard-coded English. Low cost now, high cost later.

1.5 Legal and originality boundary

Building a competing store with the same feature set, the same information architecture and the same interaction patterns is ordinary commercial practice, and this document specifies exactly that. Three things must nevertheless be original to TechStar, and the specification is written on that basis:

  • Brand assets. Name, logo, wordmark, tagline and colour identity are TechStar's own. Part 5 defines a distinct palette rather than reusing the reference platform's.
  • Editorial and legal copy. Product descriptions, About, FAQ, Privacy Policy, Terms & Conditions and blog articles must be written for TechStar. This document specifies the structure of those pages — section headings, ordering, field lengths — and never supplies the reference platform's prose as body text. Legal pages in particular must be drafted or reviewed against Tanzanian consumer and data-protection law for TechStar's own operations.
  • Catalogue content. Product names, photography and descriptions must be TechStar's own or supplied under licence by TechStar's distributors. What Appendix A reproduces is the taxonomy — a category and subcategory tree for electronic components — which is an industry-standard classification and the right starting shape for any electronics retailer, not proprietary content.
  • Registration identifiers. The reference platform publishes its own TIN and certificate of incorporation numbers in its footer. TechStar's footer must carry TechStar's own registration details or none at all.

1.6 Glossary

TermMeaning in this document
Reference platformbafredo.co.tz, the site TechStar Store is specified to match.
Product groupA named product as a shopper perceives it — one card in a grid, one detail page. May contain one or many variants.
VariantA specific purchasable configuration of a product group, with its own price, stock and SKU. A resistor product group might have variants for 220 Ω, 1 kΩ and 10 kΩ.
AttributeA named dimension along which variants differ — Resistance, Voltage, Colour, Capacity.
SKUStock keeping unit. A structured 7-digit code, defined in §12.1.
Lipa NambaTanzanian mobile-money merchant till number. Customers push funds to it from M-Pesa, Tigo Pesa (Mixx by Yas), Airtel Money or Halopesa.
Delegated paymentPlacing an order and nominating a third party — a parent, employer or sponsor — to pay it. Called "friend payment" on the reference platform.
Minor unitsThe smallest indivisible unit of a currency. All money in the database is stored as an integer count of minor units to avoid floating-point error.
Soft deleteMarking a row as deleted with a timestamp rather than removing it, so history and foreign keys survive.
FacetA filterable attribute in the catalogue — category, subcategory, price band, availability.

Part TwoReference Platform Analysis

This part records what the reference platform actually is. It is the factual base for every requirement that follows. Nothing here is inferred from marketing material or from what an electronics store "usually" does.

2.1 Runtime architecture

LayerObserved
Client frameworkVue 3, compiled with webpack 5. Build name bafredo-client. Composition API with <script setup> (evident from the compiled __name component descriptors).
RoutingVue Router 4, history mode, 25 public routes plus a nested /dashboard tree of 26 admin routes. All non-critical routes lazily loaded — 65 async chunks.
StatePinia, 14 stores. Cart and wishlist mirrored into localStorage; auth session persisted to localStorage under the key authDetails as {isAuthenticated, user, token}.
CSSBootstrap 5.2.2 loaded from jsDelivr CDN, plus 43 component-scoped stylesheets. Single typeface: Montserrat. Brand green #246345.
APISame-origin, mounted at /api. Base URL resolved at runtime: production host → https://www.bafredo.co.tz/api; otherwise window.location.origin. A development constant https://dev.bafredo.co.tz/api and a http://localhost:2700 literal are both still present in the shipped production bundle.
Server data storeMongoDB with Mongoose. Proven by every API response carrying _id in 24-character hex ObjectId form plus a Mongoose __v version key, and by aggregation-shaped responses where _id holds a grouping key (a product name, or a month number) rather than a document identifier.
RealtimeSocket.IO client, connecting to the API origin with auth: { _id }. Used for admin notification of new orders.
MediaCloudinary. Every image is stored once as WebP and served through three named transformations — see §2.5.
AnalyticsGoogle Analytics 4 (G-6MDZJR4J3C), loaded in the HTML shell. An Ahrefs site-verification meta tag is present.
AuthBearer token in localStorage. Social sign-in via server-side redirect endpoints /auth/google and /auth/facebook, returning to a client route /OAuthRedirecting that extracts the token.
Security observations carried forward as requirements Three things in the reference implementation must not be reproduced. (1) The session token is kept in localStorage, which is readable by any injected script; TechStar uses an httpOnly refresh cookie plus a short-lived in-memory access token (§11.2). (2) Development hostnames and a localhost port are shipped in the production bundle, disclosing internal topology; TechStar's build strips non-production configuration (§22.2). (3) The realtime socket authenticates by passing a raw user identifier as auth._id, which is a guessable value rather than a proof of identity — anyone can claim to be any user; TechStar authenticates sockets with the access token (§17.5).

2.3 Complete API surface

Ninety endpoint/verb pairs recovered from the bundles. public confirmed reachable without a token; token requires a signed-in user; admin requires an administrator. Authentication class is inferred from the calling screen except where marked verified.

POST/registerpublicReturns 200 with {token, _id, accountType, username, email, validEmail}.
POST/loginpublicAccepts email or phone in one field. Returns token plus profile including phone, address, shippingDetails, profileImage.

POST/email/requesttokenRe-send verification email.
POST/email/verifypublicConsume verification token.
POST/password/resetpublicRequest a reset link.
PATCH/password/reset/:tokenpublicSet new password using token.
PATCH/passwordtokenChange password while signed in.
GET / PATCH/profiletokenRead and update profile including nested shippingDetails.
POST/profile/imagetokenMultipart upload, field name images.

2.4 Client state model

The 14 Pinia stores, with the state each holds. TechStar's Zustand slices in §19.2 map one-to-one onto this list, with the corrections noted.

StoreStateNotable behaviour
authisAuthenticated, user, token, resetPasswordTokenRehydrated from localStorage.authDetails at module load.
productallProducts, allProductsByName, bestSellingProducts, newProducts, productSubcategories, randomProducts, relatedProducts, totalProductsCount, visitedProducts, fetchedSingleProduct, imagesOfFetchedSingleProductEleven parallel collections of the same entity — a cache-coherency problem TechStar avoids by using a single query cache keyed by request.
cartcart[] of {product:{_id,name,price,image}, quantity}Entirely client-side until checkout; mirrored to localStorage. Getter computedCartPrice sums price × quantity.
wishlistwishlist[], same shape as cartCarries a quantity field it never uses meaningfully.
ordercurrentOrder, myOrderscurrentOrder persisted to localStorage so checkout survives a refresh.
category / subcategorycategories[] / subcategories[]Both short-circuit if the array is already non-empty — a permanent cache with no invalidation.
bannerallBanners, homeBanners, fetchedBanner
service / partnerservices/partners, latest*, fetched*, imagesOf*Identical shape; strong candidates for one shared abstraction.
currencycurrencyDataDrives Intl.NumberFormat money formatting everywhere.
locationregions, districtsDistricts refetched on region change.
shippingMethodsallShippingMethods
navigationauthFormState, nextRouteOpens the login/register modal and remembers where to send the user afterwards.
notificationheader, text, isSuccessSingle global toast.
socketsocketId, socket

2.5 Catalogue shape and scale

MeasureObserved value
Categories17
Subcategories102

Largest subcategoryPassive devices — 86 products
Empty subcategories13 of 102 carry zero products but remain visible in the mega-menu with a "0" badge
CurrencyTZS, maximumFractionDigits: 0

Blog articles0 — module shipped, unused
Product reviews0 — module shipped, unused
Services1 — "PCB Printing"

2.5.1 SKU scheme

The reference platform uses a strict positional SKU, and it is worth keeping because it makes physical stock control in a component store tractable.

Category      2 digits   e.g. "05"      Sensors & Conditioning Boards
Subcategory   4 digits   e.g. "0510"    = category "05" + sequence "10" (Voltage)
Product       7 digits   e.g. "0510003" = subcategory "0510" + sequence "003"

Verified examples
  0111004   Hardware Dev Tools (01) > Raspberry Pi boards (0111) > item 004
  0510003   Sensors (05) > Voltage (0510) > item 003

2.5.2 Media transformations

Each image is stored once and exposed at three sizes. Every product, banner, service and partner record carries the same triple.

KeyTransformationUsed by
smthumbnail, width 200Cart and wishlist rows, gallery thumbnails, admin table rows
mdthumbnail, width 600Product cards in grids and carousels
lgoriginalProduct detail hero, banner artwork
Do not copy this detail The reference platform's sm and lg transformation URLs pin a stale version segment (v1668076879) while md omits it, so the three sizes are not guaranteed to be the same asset after a re-upload. It also applies a face-detection crop gravity (g_face) to photographs of circuit boards, which is meaningless here and can crop badly. TechStar's pipeline (§15.2) uses content-addressed derivative names and centre-weighted crops.

2.7 Defects and gaps in the reference platform

Feature parity is the goal; bug parity is not. These are concrete faults observed in the reference implementation. Each is listed with the section where TechStar's corrected behaviour is specified. Fixing them costs little at design time and is expensive later.

#DefectConsequenceFixed in
R1Product detail URLs use the product name as the path segmentRenaming a product breaks every inbound link, every shared URL and every search-engine result. Names containing /, %, # or & require double-encoding; the code contains a manual %25 unescape to work around this. Two products may not share a name, ever.§6.6
R2Variants are separate product rows re-grouped by name at read timeGrouping is a string join. A trailing space or a case difference silently splits one product into two. Observed in live data: "Raspberry Pi 4 4gb Model B Starter Kit " carries both double spaces and trailing whitespace.§12.2
R3addToCart computes an index, then pushes when the item is absent, then indexes with the stale valueThe first add of any item stores quantity 1 but skips the increment branch; subsequent adds work. Quantity handling is inconsistent between first and later additions.§12.5
R4visitProduct deduplicates with find(e => { e.name }) — a block body with no returnThe predicate always returns undefined, so the cache never hits and every visit refetches and appends a duplicate entry, growing memory for the session's lifetime.§19.3
R5Category and subcategory stores never invalidateAn administrator adding a category cannot see it in the storefront navigation without a hard reload.§19.3
R6Cart holds a price copied at add-to-cart timeIf price or stock changes while an item sits in the cart, the customer checks out against a stale price. There is no re-validation before order creation.§12.5
R7No stock check between add-to-cart and order placementOverselling. The product page shows available stock but nothing enforces it downstream.§12.4
R8Success is asserted by exact status code (if (201 !== status) throw)A 200 from a proxy, or a 204, is treated as failure. Inconsistent across stores: services and partners expect 201 on delete, which no correct server would return.§10.1
R9Session token in localStorage; socket authenticated by raw user idToken theft via any injected script; socket identity is spoofable by anyone who knows or guesses an id.§11.2, §17.5
R10Development hostnames and localhost:2700 shipped in the production bundleInternal topology disclosure.§22.2
R11Empty subcategories rendered in the mega-menu with a "0" badgeThirteen dead ends in primary navigation.§7.1.3
R12Bulk category delete cascades to all subcategories and all their productsOne mis-click destroys a large part of the catalogue. The confirmation prompt warns, but the action is irreversible and unaudited.§9.5, §14.19
R13No customer management screen despite a "Customers" sidebar entryAdministrators cannot view, search, suspend or assist an individual customer.§14.7
R14Two identical footer columns both headed "COMPANY INFORMATION"Visible on every page. Cosmetic, but it is on every page.§7.1.5
R15Typos in shipped UI: "No products to diplay", "Sarturday", "Opps!"Erodes trust at exactly the moments the user is already unsure.Appendix C
R16A product with price 0 is purchasable ("R-DRONE NAKED — TZS 0" is live)Free orders. No validation prevents a zero or negative price.§12.3
R17No pagination on the full catalogue grid — limit=10000A single response carrying the entire catalogue. Fine at 617 products, fails at 6,000.§16.5
R18Order cancellation is DELETE /order/:idCancellation should be a state transition that preserves the record, not a deletion. Financial and audit history is lost.§12.7

Part ThreeProduct Definition

3.1 Vision

TechStar Store is the place a Tanzanian student, hobbyist, technician or engineer goes to find the part they need, understand what it does, and get it delivered or collected — without leaving the country's payment and logistics reality. It sells two adjacent things: STEM — kits, robots, drones, starter bundles, teaching aids — and electronics — components, modules, boards, instruments, tools and consumables.

Those two halves have different buyers with different needs, and the design must serve both:

  • A STEM buyer browses. They arrive without a part number, respond to imagery and bundles, and need to be told what a thing is for. They are served by the home page carousels, the category tree, rich descriptions and "what's in the box" content.
  • An electronics buyer searches. They arrive knowing "74HC14" or "10 kΩ 1/4 W" and want the fastest path from search box to cart, with accurate stock and a clear price. They are served by search quality, SKU structure, variant selection and stock accuracy.

3.2 Personas

PersonaGoalWhat the platform must get right
Amina
University student, Dar es Salaam		Buy an Arduino starter kit for a coursework project, on a budget, this week.Browsable categories; clear kit contents; low-cost shipping options; mobile money without a bank card; a mobile experience that works on a mid-range Android phone over 3G.
Joseph
Electronics technician, Mwanza		Restock twenty different components in one order and have them shipped upcountry.Fast repeat search; accurate stock; a cart that holds many small-value lines; upcountry shipping with a predictable cost; an invoice he can show his employer.
Grace
Secondary-school science teacher, Dodoma		Get a quotation for lab equipment that her school's procurement office will pay against.Admin-issued invoices; delegated payment so the school pays; a shareable invoice link; clear terms.
Daniel
Startup hardware engineer		Source a specific sensor module and understand its interface before committing.Rich technical descriptions; multiple product photographs; related products; reviews; realistic lead-time signals.
Neema
TechStar store manager		Keep the catalogue accurate, fulfil today's orders, and know what is selling.Fast product entry including variants; an order queue with status; stock adjustment; a dashboard that answers "how are we doing" in one screen.
Frank
TechStar marketing		Tell 400 past customers about a new Raspberry Pi bundle.Contact list; SMS and email campaigns; scheduling; search-term analytics to know what people wanted and did not find.

3.4 Primary user journeys

J1 — Browse to purchase, guest, mobile money

Home
  └─ banner "BUY NOW" → Product detail
       └─ select variant (if any) → set quantity → ADD TO CART
            └─ cart off-canvas slides in, badge increments
                 └─ VIEW CART → CHECKOUT
                      ├─ enter name + phone (no account required)
                      ├─ choose shipping method  → cost added to total
                      ├─ if method requires it: shipping details form
                      │    (receiver name, region → district, street, postal code)
                      ├─ choose payment method: Lipa Namba | Cash on delivery
                      ├─ if Lipa Namba: enter paying phone number (255XXXXXXXXX)
                      └─ PLACE ORDER
                           └─ Order confirmation + payment instructions
                                └─ customer pushes funds to Lipa Namba
                                     └─ admin marks payment received
                                          └─ SMS + email confirmation to customer

J2 — Search to purchase, returning customer

Header search "74HC14" → Search results (term logged for analytics)
  └─ Product detail → ADD TO CART → cart merges with server-side cart
       └─ CHECKOUT (identity, address and last shipping method prefilled)
            └─ PLACE ORDER → order appears in /orders immediately

J3 — Delegated payment (school or employer pays)

Customer builds order → CHECKOUT
  └─ enters a delegated payer email address
       └─ PLACE ORDER → order created with status AWAITING_PAYMENT
            └─ payer receives an email containing /friend_payment/:orderId
                 └─ payer opens link (no account needed)
                      └─ sees order summary + payment methods
                           └─ enters payment number → MAKE PAYMENT
                                └─ both customer and payer notified on confirmation

J4 — Administrator fulfils the day

Sign in → Dashboard (today's orders, revenue, low stock)
  └─ Orders → filter "Pending"
       └─ open order → verify payment reference → set status PAID
            └─ set status PROCESSING → pick and pack (stock decremented on PAID)
                 └─ set status SHIPPED → customer notified by SMS
                      └─ set status DELIVERED → order closes

J5 — Administrator adds a product with variants

Products → NEW PRODUCT
  └─ choose subcategory (SKU prefix derived automatically)
  ├─ name, rich-text description, images (drag & drop, max 100)
  ├─ Add Attributes → define columns e.g. "Resistance"
  │    └─ add rows: 220Ω | 500 | 200      (value | price | stock)
  │                 1kΩ   | 500 | 350
  │                 10kΩ  | 500 | 180
  └─ Submit → one product group + three variants, SKUs auto-assigned

3.5 Non-goals for version 1

Stated so that scope stays honest. Each is a deliberate exclusion, not an oversight.

  • Multi-vendor marketplace. TechStar is the single seller. No vendor onboarding, no split settlement.
  • Multi-warehouse inventory. One stock pool. Location-aware stock is a Phase 5 candidate.
  • Multi-currency pricing. Prices are held in TZS. The money column type (§9.1) is chosen so that adding currencies later is additive, not a migration.
  • Card payments. Mobile money and cash cover the market. Card acquiring can be added through the same payment adapter.
  • Native mobile applications. The web experience is specified mobile-first and installable as a PWA (§21.3).
  • Automated courier integration. Shipping is a flat per-method cost, as on the reference platform. Courier APIs are a Phase 5 candidate.
  • Full Swahili translation. Strings are externalised from day one (D10) but only English and the Swahili payment instructions ship in v1.

3.6 Success metrics

MetricTargetWhy it matters / how measured
Search success rate> 80%Share of searches that lead to a product view. Measured from the search log (§16.4). Directly exposes catalogue gaps.
Cart-to-order conversion> 35%Checkout is the narrowest point of the funnel; the single-page design exists to protect this number.
Payment confirmation lag< 30 min medianTime from order placement to payment marked received. The case for aggregator integration (§13.3) is made or lost here.
Stock accuracy> 98%Orders not requiring a stock correction. Protects against the overselling defect R7.
Mobile Largest Contentful Paint< 2.5 s on 3GMost traffic will be mid-range Android on mobile data. Budgets in §21.3.
Admin product entry time< 3 min per productCatalogue growth is bounded by data entry speed. The variant matrix (§14.3.2) exists for this reason.
Order fulfilment lead time< 24 h to SHIPPEDMeasured from the order event log (§12.7).

Part FourTechnology Stack and Architecture

4.1 Stack decision

ConcernChoiceWhy this and not the obvious alternative
UI frameworkReact 18.3 + TypeScript 5.4Fixed by the brief. TypeScript is added because the domain has many similar-shaped objects (product / variant / cart item / order item) that are easy to confuse at runtime and trivial to distinguish at compile time.
BuildVite 5Sub-second cold start and instant HMR. Rollup production build with automatic route-level code splitting, matching the reference platform's 65-chunk strategy without hand configuration.
RoutingReact Router 6.22 (data router)Loaders and actions let route-level data requirements be declared next to the route, which keeps the 51-route map in Part 6 tractable.
Server stateTanStack Query 5Directly fixes defects R4 and R5: request-keyed cache, automatic invalidation on mutation, stale-while-revalidate, retry and deduplication — all of which the reference platform hand-rolls incorrectly.
Client stateZustand 4Cart, wishlist, auth session, UI state. Small, unopinionated, and shaped almost identically to the Pinia stores being ported.
StylingBootstrap 5.3 + SCSS + React BootstrapDecision D4. Preserves the reference platform's layout vocabulary so Part 7 translates literally.
FormsReact Hook Form 7 + Zod 3Zod schemas are shared with the server, so a field's rules are written once and enforced on both sides. This is the single highest-leverage choice in the stack.
Server runtimeNode.js 20 LTS + Express 4 + TypeScriptDecision D1.
DatabaseMySQL 8.0, InnoDB, utf8mb4_0900_ai_ciFixed by the brief. MySQL 8 is required, not 5.7: the reporting queries in Part 18 use window functions and CTEs, and the search design uses ngram-capable full-text indexes.
Data accessPrisma 5Decision D2.
Cache / queueRedis 7 + BullMQCampaign sending, email and SMS dispatch, image derivative generation and scheduled jobs must not run inside a request. Redis also backs rate limiting and session revocation.
RealtimeSocket.IO 4Admin order notifications and live order status for the customer.
Object storageLocal disk via adapter; S3 and Cloudinary adapters suppliedDecision D7.
Image processingsharpGenerates the sm/md/lg WebP derivatives locally, removing the Cloudinary dependency and the transformation defects noted in §2.5.2.
EmailNodemailer over SMTP, React Email templatesProvider-neutral. Works with Zoho, Google Workspace, SES or a local relay.
SMSAdapter with Beem Africa and NextSMS implementationsBoth are Tanzanian gateways with sender-ID registration; the adapter means neither is load-bearing.
TestingVitest, React Testing Library, Supertest, PlaywrightSame runner for client and server unit tests; Playwright for the seven critical journeys in §21.1.
Process managementDocker Compose for development; PM2 or systemd behind Nginx in production§22.1.

4.2.1 Why the API is same-origin

The API is mounted at /api on the same origin as the SPA, exactly as the reference platform does. This is not cosmetic: it lets the refresh token live in a SameSite=Strict httpOnly cookie without a cross-site cookie exemption, removes CORS preflight latency from every mutation, and keeps one TLS certificate. Development uses a Vite proxy so the same-origin assumption holds locally.

4.3 Request lifecycle

Every API request passes through the same ordered pipeline. The order matters and is normative.

#StageResponsibility
1requestIdAssign a ULID, attach to the async-local context, echo as X-Request-Id. Every log line and error response carries it.
2helmetSecurity headers including a strict Content-Security-Policy (§11.8).
3corsSame-origin by default; an explicit allowlist only when an alternate front end is deployed.
4Body parsingJSON limited to 256 KB; multipart handled by multer only on upload routes, with per-file and per-request caps (§11.7).
5Rate limitingRedis-backed sliding window. Tiers in §10.9.
6AuthenticationVerify the access token if present; populate req.auth = {userId, role, permissions}. Never rejects here — absence of a token is a valid state for public routes.
7Route matchExpress router.
8AuthorisationRoute-declared guard: requireAuth(), requireRole(), requirePermission(). Rejects with 401 or 403.
9ValidationZod parse of params, query and body. On failure emits the 422 shape in §10.2. The parsed, typed value replaces the raw input — controllers never see unvalidated data.
10ControllerTranslates HTTP to a service call and back. Contains no business logic.
11ServiceDomain logic, invariants, transaction boundaries. The only layer allowed to enforce a business rule.
12RepositoryPrisma access. No domain logic, no HTTP concepts.
13SerialisationExplicit response DTO. Never returns an ORM entity directly — this is what stops password hashes and internal flags leaking.
14AuditSuccessful mutations by staff append an audit record (§14.18) on the same transaction.
15Error handlerMaps typed domain errors to status codes (§20.4); logs with request id; never leaks stack traces in production.

4.4 Environments

EnvironmentPurposeConfiguration
localDeveloper workstationDocker Compose brings up MySQL, Redis and MailHog. Media on local disk. SMS adapter is a console stub. Payments in sandbox. Seed data from Appendix F.
testAutomated suitesEphemeral MySQL schema per run, migrated then truncated between tests. All outbound adapters are fakes. No network egress permitted.
stagingPre-production verificationProduction topology at smaller size. Anonymised copy of production data. Real SMS and email to an allowlist only. Payment aggregator in sandbox. noindex enforced at Nginx.
productionLiveFull topology per §22.1. Debug endpoints absent from the build, not merely disabled.
Configuration rule Configuration is read from the environment exactly once, at boot, through a Zod schema that fails fast on a missing or malformed variable (§20.8). No process.env access anywhere else in the codebase. This is what prevents the reference platform's failure mode of shipping a development hostname to production (R10).

4.5 Repository layout

A single repository with npm workspaces. One git clone, one npm install, one command to run everything.

techstar-store/ ├── apps/ │ ├── web/ React SPA — storefront and admin console │ │ ├── public/ │ │ ├── src/ │ │ └── vite.config.ts │ └── api/ Express API + Socket.IO gateway + workers │ ├── prisma/ │ │ ├── schema.prisma │ │ ├── migrations/ │ │ └── seed.ts │ └── src/ ├── packages/ │ ├── contracts/ Zod schemas + inferred TS types shared by web and api │ ├── ui/ Design-system components and SCSS tokens │ └── config/ Shared eslint, tsconfig, prettier ├── docker/ │ ├── docker-compose.yml │ └── nginx/ ├── docs/ This specification and derived artefacts ├── scripts/ Migration, backup, seed and ops helpers └── package.json Workspace root
Why packages/contracts is the keystone Every request and response shape in Part 10 is defined once as a Zod schema in this package. The API validates against it; the React forms validate against it; TypeScript types are inferred from it on both sides. A field added to a form without a corresponding schema change fails to compile. This single package is what makes a specification this large stay true to the code six months after it is written.

Part FiveBrand and Design System

The reference platform's visual system is deliberately plain: a dark utility bar, a dark header with a wide search field, a white category strip, generous white space, flat white product cards on a white page, and a single saturated green used for every affordance. It is not fashionable and it is exactly right for the job — a components catalogue is read, not admired, and the restraint keeps 617 product photographs from fighting each other.

TechStar keeps that system's structure and proportion precisely, and replaces only the identity: name, wordmark and hue. Everything specified below is normative. Where a value is inherited from the reference platform because it works, that is stated.

5.1 Brand definition

AttributeDefinition
NameTechStar Store. Written as two words, both capitalised, no hyphen, no camel case. "TechStar" alone is acceptable in body copy after first use.
WordmarkTECHSTAR in Montserrat ExtraBold, letter-spacing 0.02em, followed by Store in Montserrat Light at the same optical size, closed by a full stop in the primary green. Mirrors the reference lockup's weight contrast without copying its forms.
MarkA four-point star formed from two overlapping chevrons, in primary green on white or white on primary green. Square-safe at 32 px for the favicon; readable at 16 px.
TaglineBuild. Learn. Innovate. — three words, full stops, set in the wordmark's light weight beneath it at roughly 28% of its cap height, in the accent amber.
VoiceDirect and technical. Say what a thing does and what it costs. No exclamation marks in interface copy. Error messages state the problem and the fix, never blame the user.
Clear spaceMinimum clear space around the lockup equals the cap height of T on all sides.
Minimum sizeFull lockup no smaller than 120 px wide. Below that, the mark alone.

5.2 Colour

5.2.1 Brand palette

SwatchTokenHexUse
--ts-primary#F7921EPrimary actions, active nav, price emphasis, badges, off-canvas headers, admin table headers. The single dominant colour.
--ts-primary-dark#F7921EHover and active states of primary buttons; headings in the admin console.
--ts-primary-pale#F7921EIcons and accents on dark backgrounds — the utility bar icons and the wordmark's inner strokes.
--ts-primary-tint#F7921ESelected table rows, active filter chips, subtle success surfaces.
--ts-accent#F2A007Sale and discount badges, "new" flags, the tagline, dashboard highlight bars. Used sparingly — never for a primary action.
--ts-ink#1F1F1FMain header bar and footer background.
--ts-ink-soft#2E2E2ETop utility bar; footer legal strip.

5.2.2 Neutrals and semantics

SwatchTokenHexUse
--ts-surface#FFFFFFPage background, card background.
--ts-surface-alt#F6F7F8Order summary panel, admin page background, zebra rows.
--ts-surface-sunken#ECEFF1Product image wells, disabled inputs.
--ts-border#D8DEE3Card and input borders, dividers.
--ts-text-muted#8B98A2Subcategory eyebrow on cards, timestamps, placeholder text.
--ts-text-secondary#4A5560Body copy in dense areas, table cells, form labels.
--ts-text#212529Default body text and headings.
--ts-success#1E8E4ASuccess toasts, in-stock, paid, delivered.
--ts-danger#C0392BErrors, destructive actions, out of stock, cancelled.
--ts-warning#D98200Pending payment, low stock, scheduled campaigns.
--ts-info#1D6FA5Informational notices, processing state.
Contrast Every foreground/background pair used for text must reach WCAG 2.1 AA — 4.5:1 for body, 3:1 for text at 18.66 px bold or 24 px regular. Two pairs need care and are specified explicitly: --ts-text-muted on white reaches 3.1:1, so it is permitted only for non-essential metadata at 14 px or larger, never for a label a user must read to act; and white on --ts-accent fails, so accent badges use --ts-text for their label, not white.

5.2.3 Status colour mapping

StateTokenRendering
Order — Pending / Awaiting payment--ts-warningAmber pill, dark text
Order — Paid / Processing--ts-infoBlue pill, white text
Order — Shipped--ts-primaryGreen pill, white text
Order — Delivered / Completed--ts-successGreen pill, white text, check icon
Order — Cancelled / Refunded--ts-dangerRed outline pill, red text
Stock — In stock (> 5)--ts-successGreen dot + count
Stock — Low (1–5)--ts-warningAmber dot + "Only n left"
Stock — Out (0)--ts-dangerRed dot + "Out of stock"; Add to cart disabled

5.3 Typography

Montserrat is the single family, inherited from the reference platform and kept: it is geometric, has a wide weight range, renders legibly at small sizes on Android, and is free. Self-host WOFF2 subsets rather than loading from a CDN — it removes a third-party request from the critical path and keeps the page working when the CDN is slow, which on Tanzanian mobile networks is often.

RoleSizeWeightLine heightNotes
Display / banner title40 / 28 px8001.05Desktop / mobile. Colour set per banner record.
Page heading (h1)28 / 22 px7001.2Uppercase in section headers, as the reference does ("OUR PRODUCTS").
Section heading (h2)22 / 18 px7001.25
Card / panel heading (h3)17 / 16 px6001.3
Body15 px4001.6Maximum measure 72 characters.
Body small13 px4001.5Table cells, helper text.
Product card title13 px6001.35Uppercase, truncated to one line with ellipsis.
Product card eyebrow11 px4001.3Uppercase, muted — the subcategory name.
Price15 px6001.3Primary green. Discounted price shows the original struck through in muted grey immediately before it.
Button14 px6001Uppercase, letter-spacing 0.04em.
Label12 px6001.3Uppercase, letter-spacing 0.05em, secondary text colour.
Caption / meta11 px4001.4Muted.
Monospace13 px4001.5SKUs, order numbers, payment references. ui-monospace, "Cascadia Mono", Consolas, monospace.
Font loading Ship four weights only — 300, 400, 600, 800 — as Latin-subset WOFF2, roughly 22 KB each. Preload the 400 and 600 files. Use font-display: swap with a metric-matched fallback stack so the layout does not reflow when the webfont arrives.

Lucide React as the primary set, with Bootstrap Icons filling gaps. Both are outline sets at a 24 px grid with a 1.5 px stroke; both tree-shake to only the icons imported, which the reference platform's FontAwesome bundle does not. Brand logos for social links and payment networks come from Simple Icons.

5.6 Grid and breakpoints

Bootstrap's 12-column grid at its default breakpoints, with a container capped at 1200 px. Product grid column counts are specified per breakpoint because they are the single most visible responsive decision in the product.

NameMin widthContainerProduct gridLayout behaviour
xs0fluid, 12 px gutter2 columnsOff-canvas navigation; search collapses to an icon that opens a modal; utility bar hidden; footer columns stack.
sm576 px540 px2 columnsAs above with larger type.
md768 px720 px3 columnsUtility bar appears; horizontal category strip replaces the off-canvas; card hover actions become available.

xl1200 px1140 px6 columnsFull mega-menu with two panels; carousels show 6 cards.
xxl1400 px1140 px6 columnsContainer does not grow past 1140 px — product photography does not benefit from more width and the measure gets unreadable.

5.7 Motion

InteractionDuration / easingBehaviour
Button / link hover120 ms ease-outBackground and border colour only. No movement.
Card hover160 ms ease-outShadow 1 → 2; the "ADD TO CART" pill slides up from the card's lower edge, as on the reference platform. Desktop only — suppressed below md and on coarse pointers.
Off-canvas cart / wishlist / nav250 ms cubic-bezier(.25,.8,.25,1)Slide from the edge with a scrim fade. Focus moves into the panel; Esc closes; focus returns to the trigger.
Modal200 ms ease-outFade plus 8 px upward translate.
Toast200 ms in, auto-dismiss 5 sBottom-right. Errors do not auto-dismiss.
Carousel400 ms ease-in-outBanner autoplay 6 s, pauses on hover and on focus within.
Skeleton shimmer1.4 s linear, infiniteAny content area whose load exceeds 300 ms.
Reduced motion Under prefers-reduced-motion: reduce, all transitions collapse to 0 ms, carousel autoplay is disabled, and the shimmer becomes a static tint. This is a hard requirement, not an enhancement.

5.8 Component tokens

ComponentSpecification
Button, primaryHeight 40 px (48 px on touch), padding 0 20 px, radius sm, background --ts-primary, white uppercase 14/600 label. Hover --ts-primary-dark. Focus: 2 px outline in --ts-primary offset 2 px. Disabled: 45% opacity, no pointer events. Loading: label replaced by a 16 px spinner, width held constant so the layout does not jump.
Button, secondarySame geometry; transparent background, 1 px --ts-primary border, --ts-primary label. Hover fills with --ts-primary-tint.
Button, dangerSame geometry; --ts-danger. Always paired with a confirmation dialog when the action is irreversible.
InputHeight 40 px, padding 0 12 px, 1 px --ts-border, radius sm, 15 px text. Focus: border --ts-primary plus a 3 px tint ring. Invalid: border --ts-danger, message beneath in 12 px --ts-danger preceded by an alert-circle icon, wired with aria-describedby.
SelectAs input, with a chevron-down 16 px inset 12 px from the right. Searchable selects (subcategory, contacts) use react-select styled to match.
Product cardWhite, 1 px --ts-border, radius md, shadow 1. Image well is a 1:1 square with object-fit: contain on --ts-surface-sunken — components photographed on white must not be cropped. Body padding 12 px: eyebrow, title, price, then a two-icon action row (wishlist, quick view). Hover reveals the ADD TO CART pill.
BadgePill radius, 11/700 uppercase, padding 2 px 8 px. Discount badges sit top-left of the image well; "NEW" top-right.
Table (admin)Header row --ts-primary-dark with white 12/600 uppercase labels. Rows 44 px, zebra on --ts-surface-alt, hover --ts-primary-tint. Selection checkbox in the first column; sticky header on scroll; horizontal scroll inside its own container below lg.
Off-canvasWidth 380 px desktop, 100% below sm. Header --ts-primary with a white uppercase title and a white close button. Body scrolls; a footer pinned to the bottom holds the subtotal and the primary action.
ModalCentred, max-width 520 px (900 px for the product quick-view). Radius md, shadow 3. Header --ts-primary, white title, white close control.
ToastBottom-right, 320 px, white, shadow 2, a 4 px left rule in the semantic colour, bold title plus body, close button.
Empty stateCentred: 48 px muted icon, a 17/600 heading, one sentence of guidance, and one primary action. Every list, grid and table in the product has one, and each is specified in Part 7 or 14 — never a bare blank area.

│   └── /admin/audit-log
│
├── /sitemap.xml   /robots.txt   /manifest.webmanifest
└── /404
Deliberate divergences from the reference route map
  • /product/:slug replaces /product/:name. Fixes defect R1 — see §6.6.
  • /products/c/:slug and /products/s/:slug replace the single ambiguous /products/:id, which could only ever filter by subcategory and gave no way to view a whole category.
  • /admin replaces /dashboard. Plainer, and it frees "dashboard" to mean the analytics page rather than the whole console.
  • /pay/:paymentToken replaces /friend_payment/:orderId. Handing a raw order identifier to a third party lets anyone with an order id open a payment page; a single-purpose signed token does not (§12.9).
  • /invoice/:invoiceToken likewise replaces a raw-id URL: invoices contain a customer's name, address, phone and purchase history and must not be enumerable.
  • /cart exists as a real page, not only an off-canvas panel. The panel remains the primary surface; the page is what a mobile user gets, and what a shared or bookmarked link resolves to.
  • /admin/comments, /admin/reviews, /admin/staff, /admin/audit-log and /admin/customers are new, closing gaps R13 and R12.

6.4 Navigation model

6.4.1 Header, desktop (≥ md)

Three stacked bars, matching the reference platform's structure exactly. The lower two are sticky.

BarHeightContents
Utility32 pxBackground --ts-ink-soft. Left: phone (tel: link), email (mailto:), location (links to /about#map), each with a pale-green icon at 11 px. Hidden below md. Not sticky.
Primary72 pxBackground --ts-ink. Left: logo lockup linking to /. Centre: search field — a flush input plus a wide primary-green "Search" button, appearing at lg and above. Right: wishlist icon with count badge, cart icon with count badge and running total, and either "Login | Register" (primary-green button, opens the auth modal) or an account menu.
Category48 pxWhite, 2 px bottom border. Left-aligned: PRODUCTS (link to /products), SHOP (mega-menu), SUPPORT (dropdown: Contact us, Blog, FAQ), SERVICES (dropdown listing each service). The active item carries a 2 px primary-green underline.

6.4.2 The SHOP mega-menu

A two-panel dropdown, 720 px wide, elevated with shadow 2.

  • Left panel, 7 of 12 columns. All categories as a vertical list, each row showing the category name and a right-aligned chevron. Hovering or focusing a row loads that category's subcategories into the right panel; the row highlights with --ts-primary-tint. Clicking navigates to the category page.
  • Right panel, 5 of 12 columns, separated by a left border. The hovered category's subcategories, each with a small primary-green pill showing its product count. Clicking navigates to the subcategory page.
  • Default state. On open, the first category is pre-selected so the right panel is never empty.
  • Keyboard. ↑/↓ move through categories; → enters the subcategory panel; ← returns; Enter navigates; Esc closes and returns focus to the SHOP trigger.
Fixing R11 Subcategories with zero products are omitted from the mega-menu rather than shown with a "0" badge. Categories whose every subcategory is empty are omitted too. They remain fully visible and editable in the admin console — this rule affects storefront navigation only, and is driven by the maintained product_count column (§9.3).

6.4.3 Header, mobile (< md)

  • Single 64 px bar on --ts-ink: hamburger, compact logo mark, search icon, wishlist, cart.
  • The hamburger opens a left off-canvas: Home · Products · Shop (an accordion of categories, each expanding to its subcategories) · Support · Services · then account links.
  • The search icon opens a full-screen modal with an autofocused field and live suggestions.
  • The utility bar is hidden; its phone and email move into the off-canvas footer.

6.4.4 Footer

Four columns on --ts-ink, stacking to one below md, then a legal strip on --ts-ink-soft.

ColumnContents
ContactLegal entity name, P.O. box, city and country, two phone numbers, email address.
CompanyCountry of registration, registration agency, TIN, certificate of incorporation number, registered address. TechStar's own identifiers only (§1.5).
Policies & SupportPrivacy policy · Terms & conditions · FAQ · About us · Contact us · Shipping information · Returns.
Follow usFacebook, YouTube, LinkedIn, Instagram, X — 24 px brand-coloured marks, each with an accessible name.
Legal strip© {year} TechStar Store. All rights reserved. centred.

This corrects R14: the reference platform's two footer columns are both headed "COMPANY INFORMATION". Here they are "Contact" and "Company".

6.4.5 Floating WhatsApp button

Fixed 56 px circular button, bottom-right, 24 px inset, WhatsApp green, above all content except modals and off-canvas scrims. Links to https://wa.me/<number> with a prefilled message. On a product page the message includes the product name and SKU so the agent has context. Accessible name: "Chat with TechStar on WhatsApp".

6.5 Route guards

GuardApplies toBehaviour when it fails
requireAuth/account/*Open the auth modal with the intended route stored as nextRoute; on success, navigate there. Never a bare redirect to a login page — the reference platform's modal-based pattern is better and is kept.
requireCart/checkoutRedirect to /cart with an informational toast, "Your cart is empty."
requireStaff/admin/*If unauthenticated, redirect to / and open the auth modal. If authenticated but not staff, render a 403 page with no navigation hints — do not confirm that /admin exists.
requirePermission(p)each admin routeRender a 403 panel inside the admin shell, listing whom to ask for access.
requireToken/pay/:t, /invoice/:tRender "This link is no longer valid" with a contact route. Never reveal whether the token was wrong, expired or already used.
requireOrderAccess/order/confirmation/:nAccessible to the order's owner, or to any session holding the one-time confirmation token issued at placement — this is what lets a guest see their own confirmation.
Guards are convenience, not security Every guard above exists to give the user a sensible experience. None of them protects data: the client bundle is public and any route can be entered directly. Authorisation is enforced server-side on every request, per §11.5. A route guard that has no matching server-side check is a vulnerability, not a feature.

6.6 URL and slug rules

Fixing defect R1 properly requires a stated rule, not just a different path shape.

RuleSpecification
Slug generationLowercase; transliterate accents to ASCII; replace every run of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen; trim leading and trailing hyphens; collapse repeats; truncate to 80 characters at a hyphen boundary.
UniquenessEnforced by a unique index. On collision, append -2, -3, … until free.
StabilityA slug is generated once at creation and never regenerated when the name changes. This is the whole point: renaming "Arduino Uno R3" to "Arduino Uno Rev3" must not break the URL.
RenamingEditing a name offers an optional "update the URL" checkbox, default off. If chosen, the old slug is written to product_slug_history and served as a 301 redirect to the new one, permanently.
Identifier in URLOnly slugs appear in public URLs. Numeric primary keys never do — they leak catalogue size and invite enumeration.
Tokenised URLs/pay/:t and /invoice/:t carry a 32-byte random token, base62-encoded, stored hashed. Payment tokens are single-use and expire after 14 days; invoice tokens are revocable from the admin console.
Canonical formEvery page emits a <link rel="canonical">. Trailing slashes are stripped; uppercase paths 301 to lowercase; query parameters that do not change content (campaign tags, utm_*) are excluded from the canonical URL.
Query parametersCatalogue state lives in the query string so a filtered view is shareable and survives a refresh: ?page, ?sort, ?minPrice, ?maxPrice, ?inStock, ?q. Defaults are omitted rather than written explicitly.
Reserved slugsadmin, account, api, auth, cart, checkout, search, products, product, blog, services, invoice, pay, new, edit may not be used as any entity slug.

Part SevenStorefront UI/UX Specification

Each screen below is specified as: purpose, layout (region by region, desktop then mobile), elements (every control, its copy, its behaviour), states (loading, empty, error, partial), and interactions (what happens on each action). Copy shown in quotation marks is the exact string to implement; the consolidated copy deck is Appendix C.

7.1 Global chrome

7.1.1 Page shell

Every storefront route renders inside StorefrontLayout:

<SkipLink>                    "Skip to main content" — visible on focus only

  <PrimaryBar />
  <CategoryBar />             ≥ md only
</StickyHeader>
