7.1.2 Search field

AspectSpecification
Placeholder"Search products, SKUs, part numbers…"
SuggestionsFires after 2 characters, debounced 250 ms. A dropdown of up to 8 results, each a 40 px thumbnail, product name, subcategory eyebrow and price. Below them, "See all n results for 'term'".
Keyboard↓/↑ move through suggestions, Enter opens the highlighted one or submits the raw term if none is highlighted, Esc closes the dropdown and keeps the text.
SubmitNavigates to /search?q=…. The term is logged for analytics once per distinct term per session — not on every keystroke, which is what would make the reference platform's search table useless.
No matchesDropdown shows "No products match 'term'" plus "Browse all products" and "Ask us on WhatsApp".
Accessibilityrole="combobox" with aria-expanded, aria-controls, aria-activedescendant; the listbox has role="listbox" and each option role="option". Result count announced in a live region.

7.1.3 Cart and wishlist indicators

  • Cart. Icon plus a pill badge (distinct item count, not total quantity) and the running total formatted per the currency setting — TZS 45,000. Clicking opens the cart off-canvas. Badge animates a single 200 ms scale pulse on change, suppressed under reduced motion.
  • Wishlist. Icon plus count badge. Clicking opens the wishlist off-canvas.
  • Zero state. The badge renders "0" and the total "TZS 0" — matching the reference platform, which always shows the badge. This is preferred to hiding it, because a persistent target is easier to hit and to learn.

7.1.4 Breadcrumb

A home icon linking to /, then >-separated ancestors, the last being plain text in --ts-text-muted. Marked up as <nav aria-label="Breadcrumb"> with an ordered list and aria-current="page" on the last item, plus BreadcrumbList JSON-LD. Trails: catalogue → Home > Products; subcategory → Home > {Category} > {Subcategory}; product → Home > {Category} > {Subcategory} > {Product}; content pages → Home > {Page}.

7.1.5 Email verification banner

When a signed-in user's email is unverified, a dismissible bar sits directly beneath the header: "Please verify your email address to receive order updates." with a "Send verification link" button. On send, the button becomes a disabled "Verification link sent" for 60 seconds. Dismissal is remembered for the session only. Never shown to guests.

7.2 Home

Purpose. Give a browsing visitor three different ways into the catalogue — promotion, novelty, popularity — and establish that the store is real, stocked and staffed.

7.2.1 Layout, top to bottom

RegionSpecification
1. Banner carouselFull-bleed. Each slide is a two-column split: text on the left (7 of 12), artwork on the right (5 of 12, object-fit: contain, full height). The slide background colour, title text and colour, subtitle text and colour, button label, button background and button text colour all come from the banner record — every banner is independently art-directed by an administrator with no code change. Title at display size, subtitle at h1 size and hidden below lg, then the call-to-action button. Autoplay 6 s, loop, pause on hover and on focus-within. Previous/next chevrons, vertically centred, 40 px circular white with shadow 2, hidden below md where swipe takes over. Dots below on mobile. Height 380 px at lg, 260 px at md, 220 px below.
2. "OUR PRODUCTS"Section heading, then one sentence of positioning copy (hidden below md), then a horizontal carousel of product cards drawn from a randomised selection. 6 cards visible at xl, 4 at lg, 3 at md, 2 below. Chevron controls sit outside the track on desktop, overlapping its vertical centre.
3. "NEW ARRIVALS"Identical structure; source is products ordered by creation date descending.
4. "HOT-SELLING PRODUCTS"Identical structure; source is products ordered by lifetime order count descending. Followed by a centred "VIEW MORE" primary button linking to /products?sort=best-selling.
5. "OUR PARTNERS"Centred heading, then a horizontally scrolling strip of partner logos — 96 px square, object-fit: contain, greyscale at rest, full colour on hover, with the partner name beneath, truncated. Each links to the partner's site with rel="noopener noreferrer". Section hidden entirely when there are no partners.

7.2.2 States

StateRendering
LoadingBanner shows a shimmer block at its final height. Each carousel shows six skeleton cards at final dimensions. No layout shift when real content arrives — this is the single most important performance requirement on the page.
No bannersCarousel region omitted. The page begins with "OUR PRODUCTS". No placeholder, no empty coloured band.
Empty railThat section is omitted. A section heading with nothing under it is never rendered.
Partial failureEach rail loads independently. One failing request degrades one rail to an inline "Could not load this section. Retry" — it never blanks the page.

7.2.3 Product card, definitive specification

This component appears in every grid and carousel in the storefront. Specified once, here.

ElementSpecification
ContainerWhite, 1 px --ts-border, radius md, shadow 1 → 2 on hover. The whole card is a link to the product page; nested action buttons stop propagation.
Image well1:1 aspect ratio, --ts-surface-sunken, object-fit: contain with 8 px inset. Uses the md derivative with an srcset offering sm for narrow viewports. loading="lazy" and explicit width/height to reserve space. alt is the product name.
BadgesDiscount badge top-left, accent amber, "−15%". "NEW" badge top-right, primary green, for products created within 30 days. "OUT OF STOCK" ribbon across the lower third of the image well, red at 90% opacity, when total stock is zero.
EyebrowSubcategory name, 11 px uppercase, muted, centred, truncated to one line.
TitleProduct name, 13 px semibold uppercase, centred, truncated to one line with ellipsis. Full name available as the link's accessible label — never rely on title alone.
PriceCentred, primary green, 15 px semibold. Multi-variant products show "From TZS 200". Discounted products show the original struck through in muted grey before the new price. Zero-price products cannot exist (§12.3), so no zero case is designed.
Action rowTwo 32 px icon buttons, centred: wishlist (heart, filled and green when present in the wishlist) and quick view (eye, opens the preview modal). Visible at all breakpoints; the reference platform hides these below md, which removes the only way to reach quick view on a phone.
Add to cartA pill button that slides up from the card's lower edge on hover, on desktop only. Label "ADD TO CART". Below md, and on any coarse pointer, it is a permanently visible full-width button in the card footer — hover-only affordances do not exist on touch.
Out of stockAdd to cart replaced by a disabled "OUT OF STOCK" and a secondary "Notify me" that captures an email against the variant.
Multi-variantAdd to cart reads "CHOOSE OPTIONS" and navigates to the product page rather than adding, because there is nothing unambiguous to add.
KeyboardCard is one tab stop; the two icon buttons and add-to-cart are separate stops in reading order. Focus ring is the 2 px primary-green outline. Hover-revealed controls become visible on focus-within.

7.2.4 Quick-view modal

Max width 900 px. Left (5 of 12): the lg image with a thumbnail strip. Right (7 of 12): name, price, stock, a two-line clamped description, variant selector if applicable, quantity stepper, "ADD TO CART", and "View full details" linking to the product page. Close by button, scrim click or Esc. Focus trapped inside while open; returned to the triggering card on close. Adding to cart from the modal closes it and opens the cart off-canvas.

7.3 Catalogue

Purpose. Let a visitor traverse 600+ products, narrowed by category, price and availability, and ordered how they choose. One component serves all three catalogue routes; only the scope differs.

7.3.1 Layout

RegionSpecification
BreadcrumbPer §7.1.4.
HeadingThe subcategory name, or category name, or "All Products". Beside it, the result count: "617 products".
Control barOne row, wrapping. Left: "Sort by:" then three inline text controls — Best Selling, New Arrivals, Price. Price toggles ascending/descending and shows an arrow indicating direction. The active control is primary green with a 2 px underline. Right: a "Filters" button showing the count of active filters, which opens the filter panel; and a view-density toggle (comfortable / compact grid) persisted to localStorage.
Active filter chipsBeneath the control bar when any filter is set: one chip per filter with an ×, plus "Clear all". Each chip states its value — "TZS 1,000 – 50,000", "In stock only".
GridProduct cards. 6 columns at xl, 4 at lg, 3 at md, 2 below. 16 px gutters. Row heights equalised so cards align regardless of name length.
PaginationBottom-centred. Previous chevron, page numbers with ellipsis for long ranges, next chevron; plus a "per page" select offering 24 / 48 / 96, default 24. This replaces the reference platform's limit=10000 single-shot load (defect R17).
Filter panelDesktop: a 260 px left column that appears when Filters is toggled, pushing the grid to 9 of 12 columns. Mobile: a full-height off-canvas from the left with a sticky "Apply" footer showing the matching count.

7.3.2 Filters

FilterControlBehaviour
CategoryCollapsible tree of categories with subcategory children and countsSelecting a node navigates to its route rather than adding a query parameter, so the URL stays meaningful and the breadcrumb stays correct.
PriceTwo numeric inputs, "MIN PRICE" and "MAX PRICE", plus an "Apply" buttonMatches the reference platform's modal. Inputs are seeded with the actual minimum and maximum in the current scope. Validation: numeric, non-negative, min ≤ max; on violation the message "Minimum price must be less than maximum price" appears and Apply stays disabled.
AvailabilityCheckbox, "In stock only"Excludes products whose total stock across variants is zero.
DiscountedCheckbox, "On offer"Products with an active discount window.

All filter state lives in the query string (§6.6) so a filtered view can be shared, bookmarked and returned to with the browser's back button.

7.3.3 States

StateRendering
Loading, first pageTwenty-four skeleton cards. Control bar renders immediately.
Loading, page changeThe existing grid dims to 50% opacity with a spinner overlay; it is not replaced by skeletons. Scroll position returns to the top of the grid, not the top of the page.
Empty, no filters"No products found" with a package-x icon and a "Browse all categories" action.
Empty, with filters"No products match your filters" plus a "Clear all filters" primary action and a summary of what was applied. Distinguishing these two is what stops a filtered empty state from reading like an empty catalogue.
Empty subcategory"No products found in the selected category" plus links to sibling subcategories that do have products.
ErrorInline panel, "We could not load products just now.", with "Try again". Filters and breadcrumb remain usable.

7.4 Product detail

Purpose. Answer "is this the right part, is it in stock, what does it cost" in the first screenful, and support the decision below the fold.

7.4.1 Layout — above the fold

RegionSpecification
Gallery (left, 6 of 12)Main image in a 1:1 well on --ts-surface-sunken, object-fit: contain, using the lg derivative. Click opens a lightbox with pan and zoom; on touch, pinch to zoom. Beneath it a thumbnail strip of sm derivatives, 4 visible with chevron paging, the active one outlined in primary green. Keyboard: ←/→ change image; thumbnails are a tab-navigable radio group.
Buy box (right, 6 of 12)An elevated white card, 1 px border, radius md, shadow 1. Contents in order: product name as h1 (uppercase, 22 px, allowed to wrap to three lines); SKU in monospace muted with a click-to-copy control; average rating stars plus "(n reviews)" linking to the reviews tab, omitted when there are none; price block; variant selector; quantity stepper and stock display side by side; then three full-width stacked buttons.
Price blockCurrent price at 24 px semibold primary green. When discounted: original struck through in muted grey above, then the discounted price, then an amber "SAVE 15%" badge and, if the discount window ends within 7 days, "Offer ends 3 March".
Variant selectorOmitted for single-variant products. One control per attribute, labelled with the attribute name in uppercase. ≤ 6 options render as a pill group; more render as a select. Options that are out of stock are shown struck through and disabled — never hidden, because their absence is information the buyer needs. Choosing a variant updates price, SKU, stock and the gallery if that variant has its own image, and writes the selection to the URL as ?v=SKU so a specific variant is shareable.
QuantityLabel "QUANTITY:". A stepper: − button, numeric input, + button, matching the reference platform's arrangement. Minimum 1; maximum is available stock; the input accepts typing and clamps on blur. − is disabled at 1; + disabled at stock, with a tooltip "Only n available".
StockLabel "AVAILABLE STOCK:" and a read-only field showing the number, as the reference platform does. Above 5: green dot and the count. 1–5: amber dot and "Only n left". 0: red dot and "Out of stock".
ActionsADD TO CART (primary, full width) · ADD TO WISHLIST (secondary) · BUY NOW (primary, adds to cart and navigates straight to checkout). Out of stock: the first and third are replaced by a disabled "OUT OF STOCK" plus "NOTIFY ME WHEN AVAILABLE".

7.4.2 Layout — below the fold

  • Tabs. "DESCRIPTION" and "REVIEWS (n)", as on the reference platform, plus "SPECIFICATIONS" when the product has attributes. Implemented as real ARIA tabs with arrow-key navigation. The panel is not lazily unmounted, so browser find-in-page works across it.
  • Description panel. Sanitised rich text from the admin editor. Permitted elements: p, strong, em, u, ul, ol, li, h3, h4, a, br, table, thead, tbody, tr, th, td, img, code, pre, blockquote. Everything else is stripped server-side on save and client-side on render — sanitising in one place only is how stored XSS happens.
  • Specifications panel. A two-column table of attribute names and values for the selected variant.
  • Reviews panel. Summary at the top: average out of 5, total count, and a five-row histogram with proportional bars. Then a list of reviews, each with rating stars, author display name, relative date, body, and a "Verified purchase" badge where the reviewer has a delivered order containing the product. Sort by most recent or highest rating; paginated at 10. A signed-in user who has not yet reviewed sees the review form; a guest sees "Please sign in to write a review" with a button that opens the auth modal. Own reviews can be edited or deleted.
  • Review form. Required 1–5 star radio group, optional 80-character title, required body of 10–2,000 characters with a live counter. Submits as pending for moderation with the message "Thank you — your review will appear once approved."
  • "RELATED PRODUCTS". Heading, one line of copy, then up to 12 cards in a carousel drawn from the same subcategory, excluding the current product, ordered by sales. Section omitted when empty rather than rendered with a stray heading.
  • Recently viewed. Up to 8 cards from a client-side ring buffer, deduplicated by product id. Omitted when fewer than two entries exist.

7.4.3 States and edge cases

CaseBehaviour
LoadingGallery and buy box render as skeletons at final dimensions. Tabs render with their labels and a skeleton body.
Slug not foundCheck product_slug_history; if the slug was retired, 301 to the current one. Otherwise render the 404 page with a search field and "Browse all products".
Product soft-deleted410 Gone semantics: "This product is no longer available" plus related products from the same subcategory. Not a bare 404 — the page existed and may be linked from elsewhere.
No imagesPlaceholder graphic bearing the TechStar mark. Never a broken image icon.
Every variant out of stockBuy box shows the out-of-stock treatment; the variant selector remains interactive so the buyer can still read prices and register interest.
Price changed since a cached viewThe buy box is authoritative. If the cart holds this item at a different price, the cart shows a change notice on its next revalidation (§12.5).
Quantity exceeds stock at submitServer rejects; the field is clamped to available stock and a toast explains "Only n available — quantity adjusted."

7.4.4 Structured data

Product           name, description (plain text), sku, image[], brand,
                  category, aggregateRating (when reviews exist)
Offer             price, priceCurrency "TZS", availability
                  (InStock | OutOfStock), url, priceValidUntil when discounted
BreadcrumbList    Home > Category > Subcategory > Product
Review[]          author, reviewRating, datePublished, reviewBody

Emitted as a single application/ld+json block. Every value must match what is rendered — divergence between structured data and visible content is a manual-action risk with search engines, not a clever optimisation.

7.5 Search results

Purpose. Serve the electronics buyer who arrived with a part number. Speed and precision matter more than presentation.

  • Heading: "Search results for 'term'" with the count beneath: "28 products found".
  • The same control bar, filter panel, grid and pagination as the catalogue — one component, one behaviour to learn.
  • The searched term is highlighted within result names using a <mark> styled with an amber tint.
  • Matching is specified in §16.2: SKU exact match ranks first, then name prefix, then name contains, then description full-text. A search for "74HC14" must surface that exact part before anything merely mentioning it.
  • Empty state. "No products match 'term'", then in order: spelling suggestion ("Did you mean arduino?") when one is available within an edit distance of 2 of an indexed term; up to 6 popular products; "Browse all categories"; and "Ask us on WhatsApp — we may be able to source it", which turns a dead end into a lead.
  • Every search with zero results is recorded with its term and result count, and surfaced in the admin console (§16.4) as the catalogue's own to-do list.

7.6 Cart and wishlist

7.6.1 Cart off-canvas

RegionSpecification
HeaderPrimary green, white uppercase title "SHOPPING CART", item count, white close button.
Line itemA 64 px sm thumbnail; name (two-line clamp, links to the product); selected variant attributes in muted 12 px; unit price; a compact quantity stepper; line total in semibold; and a trash-2 remove button. Rows separated by a 1 px border.
Change noticesWhen revalidation finds a difference, an amber inline strip on the affected row: "Price changed from TZS 5,000 to TZS 5,500" or "Only 2 left — quantity reduced". This is the visible half of the fix for defect R6.
FooterPinned to the bottom, on --ts-surface-alt. Subtotal, a note that shipping is calculated at checkout, then "CHECKOUT" (primary, full width) and "View cart" (text link).
EmptyCentred shopping-cart icon at 48 px muted, "Your cart is empty", one line of guidance, and a "Browse products" primary button. The reference platform renders a bare <h1>Cart is Empty</h1>; this replaces it.
BehaviourOpens automatically on add-to-cart, with the newly added row briefly highlighted in --ts-primary-tint. Quantity changes are optimistic and reconciled with the server; a rejected change reverts with a toast. Removal offers a 5-second "Undo" in the toast before it is committed.

7.6.2 Cart page

Same data, laid out as a two-column page: a line-item table on the left (8 of 12) with columns Product, Unit price, Quantity, Total, Remove; and an order summary card on the right (4 of 12), sticky on scroll, holding subtotal, estimated shipping once a method is chosen, total, and "PROCEED TO CHECKOUT". Below the table: "Continue shopping" and "Clear cart" (with confirmation). Stacks to one column below lg.

7.6.3 Wishlist off-canvas

Header "WISHLIST". Rows carry thumbnail, name, price, current stock status, "Move to cart" and remove. No quantity — a wishlist entry is an intent, not a line item, which is why the reference platform's unused quantity field is dropped. Empty state: heart icon, "Your wishlist is empty", "Browse products". Signed-in wishlists persist server-side and merge on sign-in per §12.5.4.

7.7 Checkout

Purpose. Convert a cart into a paid order with the fewest possible decisions, without forcing account creation. This is the highest-value screen in the product and the one where the reference platform's design is at its strongest: a single page, no steps, no wizard, everything visible at once.

7.7.1 Layout

Two columns at lg and above: the form on the left (8 of 12), the order summary on the right (4 of 12), sticky from the top of the viewport. Below lg, the summary moves above the form as a collapsed accordion showing the total, expandable to the full breakdown, and the "PLACE ORDER" button becomes a fixed bottom bar showing the total.

BlockSpecification
Page heading"Getting your order" — kept verbatim from the reference platform. It is warmer and less transactional than "Checkout", and the visitor already knows where they are.
Sign-in promptTwo lines, shown only to guests. "Returning customer? Click here to log in" and "Don't have a TechStar account? Click here to create an account". Both open the auth modal at the corresponding tab and, on success, refill the form from the profile without losing anything already typed.
1. CLIENT DETAILSBordered card. Two fields side by side at md+, stacked below. Username — the buyer's name, placeholder "Username", prefilled from the profile when signed in. Phone — placeholder "255XXXXXXXXX", inputmode="numeric".
2. SHIPPING METHODBordered card with the heading row "SHIPPING METHOD" on the left and "ESTIMATED COST" right-aligned. One radio per method: label on the left, formatted cost on the right in semibold. The method's description renders beneath its label in muted 12 px — the reference platform hides this in a tooltip, where a customer choosing between "Savings" and "Cash on delivery" will never find it. Selecting a method immediately updates the summary and reveals or hides the two dependent blocks below.
3. SHIPPING DETAILSShown only when the selected method has requiresShippingDetails. Fields: Receiver's name (prefilled from Client Details, editable — the recipient is often not the buyer); Email; Phone; Country (fixed to "Tanzania", disabled); Region (select of the 25 regions); District (select, disabled and empty until a region is chosen, then loaded for that region); Street address (textarea, 2 rows); Postal code / Zip code (optional). A "Use my saved address" checkbox appears for signed-in users with a saved address, checked by default, and collapses the block to a summary with an "Edit" link.
4. PAYMENT METHODShown only when the selected shipping method has canReceiveMobilePayments; when it does not — as with Cash on delivery — payment is implicitly cash and this block is replaced by a short explanatory note. Two radios: Lipa Namba (mobile money) and Cash. Choosing Lipa Namba reveals the payment-instructions panel (§7.8.1) and a required Payment number field, placeholder "255XXXXXXXXX", helper text "The mobile money number you will pay from."
5. SOMEONE ELSE PAYING?A collapsed disclosure, "Someone else is paying for this order". Expanding reveals one field, Payer's email address, with helper text explaining that a payment link will be emailed to them and the order will be held until they pay. Specified in §12.9.
Order summaryCard on --ts-surface-alt. Header "Your Order" with a cart icon. Then the PLACE ORDER primary button — placed at the top of the summary as the reference platform does, so it is above the fold on desktop. Then a compact line-item list (thumbnail, name, "Quantity: n", line total), then Sub Total, Shipping Cost, a rule, and TOTAL in 18 px semibold. An "Edit cart" link returns to /cart.

7.7.2 Validation

Validated on blur, then on every change once a field has errored, then in full on submit. Messages are the reference platform's own wording where it is already clear.

FieldRuleMessage
Usernamerequired, 2–80 chars"Username is required"
Phonerequired"Phone is required"
Phonedigits only"Phone must be a number"
Phoneexactly 12 digits"Phone number must be 12 characters long"
Phonestarts with 255"Enter your number in the form 255XXXXXXXXX"
Shipping methodrequired"Please select a shipping method"
Email (shipping)required, valid"Email is required" / "Enter a valid email"
Receiver's namerequired"Receiver's name is required"
Regionrequired"Region is required"
Districtrequired"District is required"
Street addressrequired, 5–255 chars"Street address is required"
Payment methodrequired when the block is shown"Please select a payment method"
Payment numberrequired when Lipa Namba"Enter a payment number"
Payment numberdigits, exactly 12"Payment number must be 12 characters long"
Payer's emailvalid when present"Enter a valid email"

On a failed submit, focus moves to the first invalid field, the page scrolls it into view beneath the sticky header, and a live region announces "n fields need attention".

7.7.3 Submit sequence

PLACE ORDER pressed
 ├─ client-side validation ─── fail → focus first error, announce, stop
 ├─ button enters loading state, label replaced by spinner, form disabled
 ├─ POST /orders  { customer, items[], shippingMethodId,
 │                  shippingAddress?, paymentMethod, paymentNumber?,
 │                  delegatedPayerEmail?, idempotencyKey }
 ├─ server revalidates EVERY line: product live, variant live,
 │  price unchanged, stock sufficient                      (§12.5.3)
 │    ├─ 409 PRICE_CHANGED  → summary updates in place, amber notice
 │    │                        "Prices have changed. Please review and
 │    │                         place your order again." Button re-enabled.
 │    ├─ 409 OUT_OF_STOCK   → affected lines flagged, quantities clamped,
 │    │                        same re-confirm treatment
 │    └─ 200/201            → continue
 ├─ order created, stock reserved, order number issued  (§12.7)
 ├─ cart cleared client-side and server-side
 └─ navigate to /order/confirmation/:orderNumber
      (if an aggregator returned a hosted checkout URL, redirect there
       instead and return to the confirmation route afterwards — this is
       the `url` field the reference platform's client already honours)
Idempotency The client generates a UUID when the checkout page mounts and sends it as idempotencyKey. The server stores it against the created order and returns the same order for any repeat of the same key within 24 hours. A double-click, a flaky mobile connection or an impatient refresh must never create two orders — on a payment screen this is not a nicety.

7.7.4 States

StateRendering
Empty cartGuard redirects to /cart with the toast "Your cart is empty."
Loading reference dataShipping method list and region select render as skeletons; the identity fields are usable immediately.
Districts loadingDistrict select shows a spinner and is disabled; it never appears empty and enabled.
SubmittingAll inputs disabled, button spinning, a scrim over the summary. Navigation away triggers a beforeunload warning.
Network failure"We could not reach the server. Your cart is safe — please try again." Form re-enabled with all input preserved. The idempotency key is retained so a retry cannot duplicate.
Signed in mid-checkoutProfile data merges into empty fields only; anything the user has already typed is never overwritten.

7.8 Payment

7.8.1 Payment instructions panel

Shown inline at checkout once Lipa Namba is selected, and again on the confirmation page. It is a two-column card of network-specific instructions, one block per mobile money network, plus a QR code for the primary network. The instructions are the operational heart of the manual payment flow and are therefore content managed in the admin console (§14.16), not hard-coded — till numbers change, and a code deploy is the wrong way to change one.

BlockContents
M-Pesa (Vodacom)Numbered steps: dial the USSD string or open the app, choose "Lipa kwa M-Pesa", enter the till number, enter the amount, enter the PIN, await the confirmation SMS. Accompanied by the app QR code.
Mixx by Yas (Tigo Pesa)Numbered steps for the Tigo USSD menu and till number.
Airtel MoneyNumbered steps for the Airtel USSD menu and till number.
Other networksSteps for sending to another network, ending at the M-Pesa till number.
AmountRendered prominently above the blocks: "Pay exactly TZS 45,000" with a copy control, and the order number beneath as the reference to quote.
LanguagePresented in Swahili with an English toggle. The reference platform ships Swahili only; both matter, because the buyer's phone menus are in one language and the site is in the other.

7.8.2 Order confirmation page

/order/confirmation/:orderNumber. Contents in order:

  • A success panel: green check-circle, "Order placed", the order number in monospace with a copy control, and "We have emailed a copy to address" when an email is known.
  • What happens next — three or four numbered steps that differ by payment method. Lipa Namba: pay, we confirm within one business hour, we pack, we ship. Cash on delivery: we call to confirm, we pack, you pay the courier on delivery. Delegated: we have emailed the payer, the order is held until they pay.
  • The payment instructions panel, when the method is Lipa Namba and payment is not yet confirmed.
  • Order summary: items, quantities, unit prices, subtotal, shipping, total, delivery address and chosen shipping method.
  • Actions: "View order", "Print", "Continue shopping".
  • A live status strip that updates over the realtime channel — when an administrator marks payment received, the page changes to "Payment confirmed" without a refresh (§17.5).

7.8.3 Delegated payment page

/pay/:paymentToken, opened by the nominated payer, who is usually not a TechStar account holder and may never become one.

AspectSpecification
Heading"Payment request" and beneath it "Amina M. has asked you to pay for this order." Only the requester's first name and last initial — never their full contact details.
Order detailsRead-only: item thumbnails, names, "Quantity: n", line totals, Sub Total, Shipping Cost, TOTAL. No delivery address, no phone number, no email.
Payment methodsThe same radio group and instructions panel as checkout, plus the required payment-number field.
Action"MAKE PAYMENT" primary button, full width.
After submitSuccess panel confirming the payment is recorded and pending verification; both payer and requester are notified.
Token statesAlready paid → "This order has already been paid." Expired → "This payment link has expired." plus a "Contact TechStar" action. Invalid → the same generic message as expired, so tokens cannot be probed.
Robotsnoindex, nofollow. Never included in the sitemap.

7.9 Order history and detail

7.9.1 Order list — /account/orders

  • Heading "My orders". Filter chips: All · Pending · Processing · Shipped · Delivered · Cancelled, each with a count.
  • Desktop: a table with Order (monospace number), Date, Items (count), Total, Status pill, and a "View" action. Mobile: stacked cards carrying the same fields.
  • Sorted newest first; paginated at 20.
  • Empty: package icon, "You have not placed any orders yet", "Browse products".

7.9.2 Order detail — /account/orders/:orderNumber

RegionSpecification
Header"Order TS-2026-000148", placement date, status pill, and a payment-status pill ("Awaiting payment", "Paid", "Refunded").
TimelineA vertical stepper built from the order event log: Placed → Paid → Processing → Shipped → Delivered, each completed step showing its timestamp, the current step highlighted, future steps muted. Cancellation replaces the remainder with a red terminal node.
ItemsThumbnail, name (linking to the product), selected attributes, SKU, unit price, "Quantity: n", line total.
Client details"Client:", "Email:", "Phone:" — falling back to "Anonymous" and "Not specified" as the reference platform does, since guest orders legitimately lack these.
ShippingMethod name and cost; delivery address as "Receiver's name:", "Country:", "City/Town:", "Street Address:", "Postal Code:".
TotalsSub Total, Shipping Cost, TOTAL.
ActionsOrder again — adds every still-available line to the cart and reports what could not be added. Print invoice — opens the print stylesheet. Cancel order — only while the status is Pending or Awaiting payment; opens a confirmation dialog reading "Are you sure you want to cancel this order?" with an optional reason. Pay now — when awaiting payment, reopens the instructions panel.
Cancellation is a transition, not a deletion Fixing R18: the reference platform cancels by sending DELETE /order/:id. Here, cancelling writes a CANCELLED transition and an event-log entry, releases any stock reservation, and keeps the order permanently visible to both customer and administrator. Financial records are never deleted by a customer action.

7.10 Account

/account renders a layout with a left navigation rail (a horizontal tab bar below md): Account · Orders · Shipping address · Change password. Above the rail sit the avatar, display name and email.

TabFields and behaviour
AccountAvatar with "Upload image" (JPEG/PNG/WebP, max 3 MB, cropped square client-side, live preview). Then Email (changing it re-triggers verification and warns so before saving), Username, Phone Number (255XXXXXXXXX). "Update" primary button, disabled until something changes. Success toast "Successfully updated user account".
OrdersThe list from §7.9.1 rendered inside the account shell.
Shipping addressReceiver's Name, with a "Details similar to my account" checkbox that copies name, email and phone from the profile. Email, Phone, Country (fixed "Tanzania"), Region, District (dependent), Street Address, Postal Code/Zip Code. Saved address prefills checkout. Success toast "Successfully updated shipping details".
Change passwordCurrent password, New password with a live strength meter, Confirm new password. Rules in §11.4.3. On success, all other sessions are revoked and the user is told so: "Password changed. You have been signed out on other devices."

7.11 Content pages

7.11.1 About

Hero band with the admin-managed image behind a dark scrim, the page title centred, and a breadcrumb. Then, in order: Our Story — long-form copy with a "Read more" expander that reveals the remainder in place; Vision and Mission as two cards side by side; Core values as a four-up grid of icon, name and one line each; and finally a map. The map is Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles, a single marker at the store's coordinates with a popup showing name, address and a "Get directions" link. Height 400 px, non-scroll-hijacking (zoom requires Ctrl + wheel or a two-finger gesture), with the address also rendered as text beneath so the information does not depend on tiles loading. The anchor /about#map is linked from the utility bar and must scroll to it accounting for the sticky header offset.

7.11.2 Contact

Heading, then two columns. Right (5 of 12) — CONTACT DETAILS: address, "E-Mail:", "Tel:" with two numbers, opening hours, and social links, each with an icon. Left (7 of 12) — GET IN TOUCH WITH US: Your name (required), Email (required, valid), Subject (optional), Message (required, 10–2,000 characters with a counter), a checkbox "Send me a copy of this message", and an invisible honeypot plus a timing check for spam. "Send Message" primary button. On success the form is replaced in place by a confirmation panel — "Thank you for getting in touch with TechStar Store" — and a "Submit another message" link. Errors: "Name is required", "Email is required", "Email not valid", "Message is required", and on failure "Failed to send message" with the input preserved.

7.11.3 FAQ

Centred heading "Frequently Asked Questions", optional intro, then an accordion grouped by topic — Ordering, Payment, Shipping & Delivery, Returns, Products, Account. One item open by default. Real <button> headers with aria-expanded and aria-controls; chevron rotates on expand. Below the accordion, a support panel: "Can't find your answer?" with "Contact us" and "Chat on WhatsApp". FAQPage JSON-LD is emitted. Entries are admin-managed with an ordering field.

7.11.4 Privacy policy and terms

Same shell: hero band with admin-managed image, page title, breadcrumb, then long-form content with a sticky table of contents on the left at lg+ that highlights the section in view. Sections are anchored so individual clauses can be linked. A "Last updated" date is shown at the top and maintained by the administrator. Both documents must be drafted for TechStar (§1.5); the platform provides the structure, the ordering and the anchors, not the words.

7.12 Blog

  • Index. Heading "Blog", then a responsive card grid — 3 columns at lg, 2 at md, 1 below. Each card: 16:9 cover image, title (two-line clamp), publication date, excerpt (three-line clamp), comment count, and "Read more". Paginated at 9.
  • Article. Cover image, title as h1, author and date, then sanitised rich-text body constrained to a 72-character measure. A right rail at lg+ carries "Latest Articles" — five entries with 56 px thumbnails.
  • Comments. Beneath the article: count, then a list of comments each with avatar, display name, relative timestamp and body. A signed-in user sees a composer with the placeholder "Write something here" and a "Post comment" button; a guest sees "Please create an account or log in to comment" with a button opening the auth modal. Submitted comments enter moderation with "Thank you for your comment — it will appear once approved." Authors may delete their own.
  • Empty. "No articles yet" with a newspaper icon. The reference platform ships this module with zero content; TechStar should launch with at least three articles or hide the Blog navigation entry entirely — an empty section in primary navigation reads as neglect.

7.13 Services

  • Index. Card grid of services: image, heading, excerpt, "Learn more".
  • Detail. Hero image, heading, publication date, rich-text body. A right rail lists "Other Services". At the foot, a call to action: "ASK FOR DESIGN SERVICE" with a "Contact Us" button that opens the contact form with the subject prefilled with the service name.
  • Each service also appears in the SERVICES header dropdown, sourced from the latest-services endpoint.

7.14 Authentication surfaces

Authentication is a modal, never a page — the reference platform's choice, and the right one, because it preserves cart and checkout context. The modal opens at the tab requested and remembers the route to return to.

SurfaceFields and behaviour
Modal shellCentred, max-width 460 px, with the TechStar mark in a white circle overlapping the top edge. Header bar in primary green with the current mode as a white uppercase title and an "×" close control. Focus trapped; Esc closes; focus returns to the trigger.
LOGINEMAIL OR PHONE — one field accepting either, as the reference platform does; the server resolves which. PASSWORD with a show/hide eye toggle. "Forgot password?" link. "LOGIN" primary button, full width. "Don't have an account? Create your account here". Then a divider and two social buttons: "Continue with Facebook" (brand blue) and "Continue with Google" (white, outlined, with the Google mark).
REGISTERUSERNAME, EMAIL, PHONE (255XXXXXXXXX), PASSWORD with a strength meter, CONFIRM PASSWORD, and a required checkbox "I agree to the Terms & Conditions and Privacy Policy" with both linked. "REGISTER" primary button. On success the user is signed in immediately, the modal closes, a verification email is sent and the verification banner appears.
FORGOT PASSWORDOne EMAIL field and "SEND RESET LINK". The response is always the same regardless of whether the address exists: "If an account exists for that address, we have sent a reset link." Anything else is an account-enumeration oracle.
Reset page/auth/reset-password/:token. New password plus confirmation, strength meter, and the same rules as registration. On success: sign in, revoke all other sessions, toast "Password changed", redirect home. Invalid or expired token: "This reset link is no longer valid" with a control to request a new one.
Email verification/auth/verify-email/:token. Shows a spinner, then either "Email verification successful" or "Verification failed" with a resend control, then redirects home after 3 seconds.
OAuth callback/auth/oauth/callback. Full-screen spinner with "Signing you in…", then exchanges the one-time code, then redirects to the stored return route. Failure: "Failed to sign in" with a control to return to the sign-in modal.
ErrorsCredential failures render one message above the form — "Email, phone or password is incorrect" — never distinguishing which was wrong. Rate-limit lockout renders "Too many attempts. Please try again in n minutes."

7.15 Errors and empty states

CaseCopyActions
404"Oops!" / "Page not found" / "The page you're looking for doesn't exist.""Go home" primary; a search field; popular categories. (The reference platform ships this as "Opps!" — see R15.)
403"You don't have access to this page.""Go home"; "Sign in as a different user".
500"Something went wrong at our end.""Try again"; "Go home"; the request id in small monospace so support can trace it.
Offline"You appear to be offline."Auto-retries on reconnect. The cart survives, because it is in local storage.
Maintenance"TechStar Store is briefly down for maintenance."Expected return time; WhatsApp contact.
Slow responseAfter 8 seconds, an inline "This is taking longer than usual…" beneath the spinnerNever an indefinite spinner with no explanation.
Error-copy rule Every error message states what happened, whether the user's data is safe, and what to do next. "Failed to place order" alone is not acceptable; "We could not place your order. Nothing has been charged and your cart is unchanged. Please try again." is. This rule applies equally to the admin console.

Part EightComponent Catalogue

Every component the platform needs, with its responsibility and its public contract. Components live in packages/ui when they are design-system primitives with no domain knowledge, and in apps/web/src/components when they know about products, orders or carts. That boundary is enforced by lint rule: nothing in packages/ui may import from apps/web.

8.4 Admin components

8.4.1 DataTable

The workhorse of the admin console. One component serves products, orders, invoices, customers, categories, subcategories, shipping methods, banners, campaigns, contacts, searches, comments, reviews, staff and the audit log.

interface DataTableProps<T> {
  columns: Column<T>[]          // key, header, render?, sortable?, width?, hideBelow?
  rows: T[]
  rowKey: (row: T) => string

  loading?: boolean             // renders skeleton rows at final height
  error?: Error                 // renders an inline retry panel
  emptyState: EmptyStateProps

  // selection
  selectable?: boolean
  selectedKeys?: string[]
  onSelectionChange?: (keys: string[]) => void
  bulkActions?: BulkAction[]    // label, icon, tone, requiresConfirm, onRun

  // server-driven state — the table never fetches, it only reports
  sort?: { key: string; dir: 'asc' | 'desc' }
  onSortChange?: (s: Sort) => void
  page: number
  pageSize: number
  total: number
  onPageChange: (p: number) => void
  onPageSizeChange?: (n: number) => void

  search?: { value: string; placeholder: string; onChange: (v: string) => void }
  filters?: FilterDescriptor[]
  rowActions?: (row: T) => RowAction[]
  onRowClick?: (row: T) => void
  density?: 'comfortable' | 'compact'
}

Behaviour that is uniform everywhere it is used: sticky header on scroll; sortable headers with an aria-sort attribute; a select-all checkbox that selects the current page only, with a "Select all n matching" affordance when more exist; a bulk-action bar that slides in above the table when anything is selected, showing the count and a "Clear" control; search debounced at 300 ms and written to the query string; row actions in a trailing column, collapsing to an overflow menu below lg; and horizontal scroll confined to the table's own container so the page never scrolls sideways.

8.4.2 Other admin components

ComponentResponsibility
StatCardDashboard tile: label, large value, delta versus the previous period with direction and colour, sparkline, optional click-through.
ChartPanelChart.js wrapper with a title, a period selector (Last 7 Days / Last 30 Days / Last 12 Months), a loading skeleton, an empty state and an accessible data table alternative behind a "View as table" toggle.
ImageUploaderFilePond wrapper. Drag-and-drop, multiple files, reordering, per-file progress, delete with confirmation, and the accepted-types and size limits from §11.7. Copy: "Drag & drop your files or Browse". Uploads immediately on drop and reports server ids to the form; never holds base64 in form state.
RichTextEditorTipTap. Toolbar: bold, italic, underline, H3, H4, bullet list, ordered list, link, blockquote, code block, table, image, undo, redo, clear formatting. Emits HTML constrained to the allowlist in §7.4.2, with a character counter and an HTML source view for administrators who want it.
AttributeMatrixThe variant editor described in §14.3.2 — dynamic attribute columns plus fixed Price and Stock columns, add/remove row, add/remove column, delete table, per-cell validation.
ColorFieldSwatch, native colour input and a hex text field kept in sync. Used by the banner builder for its six colour fields.
EntityPickerModal search-and-select over a catalogue entity. Used by the invoice builder to add products.
FormLayoutStandard admin form scaffold: section headings, two-column field grid, a sticky footer with Cancel and Submit, unsaved-changes guard, and a disabled submit until the form is dirty and valid.
PermissionGateRenders children only when the signed-in staff member holds a permission; otherwise renders nothing, or a disabled control with an explanatory tooltip when mode="disable".
AuditTrailCompact reverse-chronological list of changes to one entity: who, when, what changed, before and after.

8.5 Contracts every component honours

  • Controlled by default. Components take value and onChange. Uncontrolled variants exist only where React Hook Form needs an uncontrolled input for performance.
  • No data fetching in presentation components. Fetching lives in route loaders and hooks. A component that fetches cannot be reused with different data and cannot be tested without a network mock.
  • Every interactive element has an accessible name. Enforced by eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y and by an axe pass in the component test suite.
  • Loading, empty and error are props, not afterthoughts. Any component that renders a collection must accept all three; the type system requires emptyState.
  • No hard-coded colour, spacing or font values. Only tokens. Enforced by a stylelint rule.
  • No hard-coded user-facing strings. All copy is resolved through the i18n layer (§19.8), even while only English ships.
  • Each component ships with a Storybook story per state. The story set is the visual regression suite (§21.1).

Part NineData Model — MySQL

9.1 Modelling principles

These rules are applied without exception across all 50 tables. Where a rule differs from what the reference platform does, the reason is given — the reference platform stores documents in MongoDB, and a direct transliteration of that shape into MySQL would inherit its defects.

PrincipleRule and rationale
Primary keysBIGINT UNSIGNED AUTO_INCREMENT. Compact, ordered, and ideal for InnoDB's clustered index. Public identifiers are separate: slugs for catalogue entities, human-readable numbers for orders and invoices, and random tokens for shareable links. A primary key is never exposed in a URL (§6.6).
Public identifiersEvery entity that appears in a URL carries either a slug VARCHAR(96) with a unique index, or a formatted number such as TS-2026-000148. This is what makes defect R1 structurally impossible.
MoneyBIGINT holding minor units — for TZS, whole shillings, since TZS has no subunit in practice. Never FLOAT or DOUBLE. Every money column is named with an _amount suffix and is accompanied by a currency CHAR(3) on the owning row wherever the value is customer-facing, so a future multi-currency change is additive.
Timestampscreated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) and updated_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3) on every table. All stored in UTC; the application converts to Africa/Dar_es_Salaam (UTC+3) for display. DATETIME not TIMESTAMP, to avoid the 2038 limit.
Soft deletesdeleted_at DATETIME(3) NULL on every entity a customer or an order can reference. A deleted product must still render correctly inside a three-year-old order. Queries filter deleted_at IS NULL by default through a Prisma middleware, with an explicit escape hatch for the admin console's trash view.
Character setutf8mb4 with utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci collation, database-wide. Product names contain ®, µ, Ω and emoji appear in reviews; utf8 (three-byte) would truncate them.
EngineInnoDB throughout. Foreign keys and transactional integrity are required by the order and stock logic in Part 12.
EnumerationsVARCHAR with a CHECK constraint, not MySQL's ENUM. Adding a value to a native ENUM rewrites the table; a check constraint is altered cheaply, and the values stay legible in a dump.
BooleansTINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0. Named affirmatively — is_active, never is_not_disabled.
NullabilityNOT NULL is the default. NULL is used only where "unknown" is genuinely different from "empty" — a guest order's email is NULL; a product with no description holds ''.
Denormalised countersproduct_count, order_count, review_count, average_rating are maintained in the same transaction as the fact that changes them, never recomputed on read. The reference platform does the same and it is right — but it must be transactional, or the counts drift.
SnapshotsOrders and invoices store a copy of the name, SKU, price and attributes at the moment of purchase, alongside a nullable reference to the live entity. An order must be reprintable after the product is renamed, repriced or deleted. This is not redundancy; it is the correct model for a financial record.
Namingsnake_case, plural table names, singular column names, foreign keys as <singular_table>_id. Indexes named idx_<table>_<cols>, uniques uq_, foreign keys fk_<table>_<ref>.

9.3 Table-by-table specification

Columns are given as name · type · constraints · notes. Every table also carries created_at and updated_at as defined in §9.1; these are omitted below for brevity and are present in the DDL in Appendix E.

9.3.1 users

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
public_idCHAR(26) UNIQUEULID. Used in admin URLs and support conversations, so the sequential primary key is never exposed.
emailVARCHAR(255) NULL UNIQUENullable because a phone-only account is valid. Stored lowercase; uniqueness is case-insensitive by collation.
email_verified_atDATETIME(3) NULLReplaces the reference platform's boolean validEmail — knowing when is worth the same storage.
phoneVARCHAR(15) NULL UNIQUEStored canonically as 255XXXXXXXXX, digits only, no plus.
phone_verified_atDATETIME(3) NULLFor a future SMS OTP flow.
usernameVARCHAR(80) NOT NULLDisplay name. Not unique — two customers may both be "John Mushi".
password_hashVARCHAR(255) NULLArgon2id. NULL for OAuth-only accounts.
account_typeVARCHAR(16) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'customer'CHECK IN ('customer','staff'). Fine-grained authority comes from roles.
statusVARCHAR(16) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active'CHECK IN ('active','suspended','closed').
profile_image_idBIGINT UNSIGNED NULL FK → media_filesON DELETE SET NULL.
marketing_opt_inTINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Consent for campaigns. Absent from the reference platform, which mails every contact — a compliance risk.
last_login_atDATETIME(3) NULL
failed_login_countSMALLINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Drives progressive lockout (§11.6).
locked_untilDATETIME(3) NULL
deleted_atDATETIME(3) NULL

Constraints. CHECK (email IS NOT NULL OR phone IS NOT NULL) — an account must be reachable by at least one channel. Indexes. uq_users_email, uq_users_phone, uq_users_public_id, idx_users_status_created, idx_users_account_type.

9.3.2 user_addresses

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
user_idBIGINT UNSIGNED FK → usersON DELETE CASCADE.
labelVARCHAR(40) NULL"Home", "Office".
receiver_nameVARCHAR(120) NOT NULLOften not the account holder.
emailVARCHAR(255) NULL
phoneVARCHAR(15) NOT NULLThe courier's contact number.
countryVARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Tanzania'

9.3.3 oauth_accounts

id · user_id FK CASCADE · provider VARCHAR(20) CHECK IN ('google','facebook') · provider_user_id VARCHAR(191) · email VARCHAR(255) NULL · raw_profile JSON NULL · linked_at DATETIME(3). Unique on (provider, provider_user_id); index on user_id. A user may link both providers.

9.3.4 sessions

id · user_id FK CASCADE · refresh_token_hash CHAR(64) UNIQUE (SHA-256 — the raw token is never stored) · user_agent VARCHAR(255) · ip_address VARBINARY(16) · expires_at DATETIME(3) · revoked_at DATETIME(3) NULL · replaced_by_id BIGINT NULL (rotation chain, §11.2). Indexes on (user_id, revoked_at) and expires_at for the cleanup job.

9.3.5 email_verification_tokens and password_reset_tokens

Identical shape: id · user_id FK CASCADE · token_hash CHAR(64) UNIQUE · expires_at DATETIME(3) · used_at DATETIME(3) NULL · requested_ip VARBINARY(16). Verification tokens live 24 hours, reset tokens 60 minutes. Issuing a new token invalidates all outstanding ones for that user.

9.3.6 roles, permissions, role_permissions, user_roles

TableColumns
rolesid · key VARCHAR(40) UNIQUE · name VARCHAR(80) · description VARCHAR(255) · is_system TINYINT(1) — system roles cannot be deleted.
permissionsid · key VARCHAR(60) UNIQUE (e.g. product.create) · group VARCHAR(40) · description VARCHAR(255).
role_permissionsrole_id · permission_id, composite PK, both FK CASCADE.
user_rolesuser_id · role_id, composite PK · granted_by BIGINT NULL · granted_at.

Seeded roles: owner (all permissions, undeletable, at least one must always exist), manager (everything except staff and settings), catalogue (products, categories, media), fulfilment (orders, invoices, customers read-only), marketing (campaigns, contacts, articles, banners), support (orders and customers read-only, comment and review moderation).

9.3.7 categories

id · name VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL · slug VARCHAR(96) UNIQUE · sku_code CHAR(2) UNIQUE (the two-digit prefix, §12.1) · description VARCHAR(500) NULL · image_id FK → media_files NULL · position INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 (manual ordering) · subcategory_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 · product_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 · is_active TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 · deleted_at. Indexes: uq_categories_slug, uq_categories_sku_code, idx_categories_position.

9.3.8 subcategories

id · category_id FK → categories RESTRICT · name VARCHAR(140) · slug VARCHAR(96) UNIQUE · sku_code CHAR(4) UNIQUE · description VARCHAR(500) NULL · image_id NULL · position INT · product_count INT UNSIGNED · is_active TINYINT(1) · deleted_at. Indexes: uq_subcategories_slug, uq_subcategories_sku_code, idx_subcategories_category.

Why exactly two levels A general tree with a self-referencing parent is more flexible and worse here. The SKU scheme is positional and assumes two levels; the mega-menu is a two-panel design that assumes two levels; and the observed taxonomy — 17 categories, 102 subcategories — has never needed a third. Two explicit tables give simpler queries, cheaper counts and stronger constraints. If a third level is ever required, it is an additive migration, not a redesign.

9.3.9 products (the product group)

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
subcategory_idBIGINT UNSIGNED FK RESTRICTA subcategory holding products cannot be hard-deleted.
nameVARCHAR(255) NOT NULLTrimmed and internal whitespace collapsed on write — the direct fix for the dirty names observed in the reference data (R2).
slugVARCHAR(96) NOT NULL UNIQUEGenerated once; never regenerated (§6.6).
sku_prefixCHAR(7) NOT NULL UNIQUEThe group's seven-digit code; variants extend it (§12.1).
short_descriptionVARCHAR(500) NULLPlain text, used for meta descriptions and card tooltips.
description_htmlMEDIUMTEXT NOT NULLSanitised on write.
description_textMEDIUMTEXT NOT NULLPlain-text projection, maintained on write, used by the full-text index — searching HTML matches tag names.
brandVARCHAR(120) NULL
manufacturer_part_numberVARCHAR(80) NULLIndexed. An electronics buyer searches by MPN as often as by name.
display_image_idBIGINT UNSIGNED NULL FKThe card and hero image.
statusVARCHAR(16) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'draft'CHECK IN ('draft','active','archived'). The reference platform has no draft state, so a half-entered product is immediately live.
total_stockINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Maintained sum across variants. Drives the in-stock facet without a join.
min_price_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Maintained. Drives "From TZS 200" and price-range filtering.
max_price_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Maintained.
order_countINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Lifetime units sold; drives best-seller ordering.
view_countINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
review_countINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
average_ratingDECIMAL(3,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Maintained on review approval.
meta_title, meta_descriptionVARCHAR(70) / VARCHAR(160) NULLSEO overrides; fall back to name and short description.
published_atDATETIME(3) NULLDrives "NEW" badging independently of created_at.
deleted_atDATETIME(3) NULL

Indexes. uq_products_slug, uq_products_sku_prefix, idx_products_subcategory_status, idx_products_status_published, idx_products_order_count, idx_products_min_price, idx_products_mpn, and ft_products_search — a FULLTEXT index on (name, description_text, brand, manufacturer_part_number).

9.3.10 product_slug_history

id · product_id FK CASCADE · slug VARCHAR(96) UNIQUE · retired_at. Looked up on a 404 to serve a 301 to the current slug. Retired slugs are reserved forever and can never be claimed by another product.

9.3.11 product_images

id · product_id FK CASCADE · media_file_id FK RESTRICT · variant_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL (an image may belong to one specific variant) · alt_text VARCHAR(255) NULL · position INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0. Unique on (product_id, media_file_id); index on (product_id, position).

9.3.12 product_attributes

The attribute columns a product's variant matrix defines. id · product_id FK CASCADE · name VARCHAR(80) ("Resistance", "Capacity", "Colour") · position INT · unit VARCHAR(20) NULL ("Ω", "µF", "mm"). Unique on (product_id, name).

9.3.13 product_variants

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
product_idBIGINT UNSIGNED FK CASCADE
skuVARCHAR(20) NOT NULL UNIQUEGroup prefix plus a two-digit variant suffix (§12.1).
price_amountBIGINT NOT NULLCHECK (price_amount > 0) — this constraint alone makes defect R16 impossible.
compare_at_amountBIGINT NULLThe "was" price, for showing a saving.
cost_amountBIGINT NULLPurchase cost. Never exposed to the storefront; drives margin reporting.
currencyCHAR(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'TZS'
stock_quantityINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0CHECK (stock_quantity >= 0).
reserved_quantityINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Held by unpaid orders. Available = stock − reserved (§12.4).
low_stock_thresholdINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 5Drives the dashboard alert and the "Only n left" treatment.
weight_gramsINT NULLFor future weight-based shipping.
barcodeVARCHAR(64) NULL
positionINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Display order in the selector.
is_activeTINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1
deleted_atDATETIME(3) NULL

Indexes. uq_product_variants_sku, idx_variants_product_active, idx_variants_stock, idx_variants_price.

Every product has at least one variant A product with no options still gets a single variant row. Uniform structure removes every "does this product have variants?" branch from cart, order, stock and reporting code. The storefront simply hides the selector when the count is one. This is the structural fix for defect R2.

9.3.14 variant_attribute_values

variant_id FK CASCADE · product_attribute_id FK CASCADE · value VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL. Composite PK on (variant_id, product_attribute_id); index on (product_attribute_id, value) for future attribute faceting.

9.3.15 tags and product_tags

tags: id · name VARCHAR(60) · slug VARCHAR(60) UNIQUE. product_tags: product_id · tag_id, composite PK, both CASCADE. Tags feed search boosting and curated collections. The reference platform has a tags array on every product and never populates it; here it is at least usable.

9.3.16 discounts

id · scope VARCHAR(20) CHECK IN ('product','variant','subcategory','category') · scope_id BIGINT UNSIGNED · type VARCHAR(16) CHECK IN ('percentage','fixed') · value INT NOT NULL (percent 1–90, or minor units) · starts_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL · ends_at DATETIME(3) NOT NULL · is_active TINYINT(1) · created_by FK → users. Index on (scope, scope_id, is_active, starts_at, ends_at). CHECK (ends_at > starts_at). Resolution order when several apply is defined in §12.3.

9.3.17 media_files

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
public_idCHAR(26) UNIQUEULID; forms the served filename.
storage_driverVARCHAR(20) NOT NULL'local' | 's3' | 'cloudinary'.
storage_keyVARCHAR(512) NOT NULLPath or key within the driver.
original_filenameVARCHAR(255)Sanitised; never used as a path component.
mime_typeVARCHAR(80)Determined by magic-byte sniffing, not by the client-supplied header (§11.7).
byte_sizeINT UNSIGNED
width, heightINT UNSIGNED NULLUsed to reserve layout space and prevent shift.
checksum_sha256CHAR(64)Indexed — identical uploads are deduplicated to one stored file.
derivativesJSON NOT NULL{"sm":{...},"md":{...},"lg":{...}} each with key, width, height and byte size.
uploaded_byBIGINT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL
reference_countINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Maintained. Zero-reference files older than 24 hours are swept (§15.4).
deleted_atDATETIME(3) NULL

9.3.18 carts and cart_items

TableColumns
cartsid · user_id BIGINT NULL FK CASCADE · guest_token CHAR(32) NULL UNIQUE · currency CHAR(3) · last_activity_at DATETIME(3) · converted_order_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL. CHECK (user_id IS NOT NULL OR guest_token IS NOT NULL). Unique on user_id where not null — one open cart per account.
cart_itemsid · cart_id FK CASCADE · product_variant_id FK RESTRICT · quantity INT CHECK (quantity > 0) · unit_price_amount BIGINT — the price seen when added, kept only to detect a change, never to charge against · added_at. Unique on (cart_id, product_variant_id) so adding an existing line increments rather than duplicating.

Abandoned guest carts are purged after 30 days of inactivity; signed-in carts are kept indefinitely. Cart persistence server-side is what lets a customer start on a phone and finish on a laptop — the reference platform keeps carts only in localStorage and posts them at checkout, so that journey loses everything.

9.3.19 wishlists and wishlist_items

wishlists: id · user_id FK CASCADE UNIQUE · guest_token CHAR(32) NULL UNIQUE. wishlist_items: id · wishlist_id FK CASCADE · product_id FK CASCADE · product_variant_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · added_at. Unique on (wishlist_id, product_id, product_variant_id). No quantity column.

9.3.20 shipping_methods

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
nameVARCHAR(80) NOT NULL"Savings", "Cash on delivery", "Office Pickup".
slugVARCHAR(60) UNIQUE
descriptionVARCHAR(500) NOT NULLShown beneath the option at checkout, not hidden in a tooltip.
cost_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0CHECK (cost_amount >= 0).
requires_shipping_addressTINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1Office pickup does not.
accepts_mobile_paymentTINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1Cash on delivery does not — this flag is what hides the payment block at checkout.
is_cash_on_deliveryTINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Explicit rather than inferred from the name, so the order flow does not depend on a string.
free_over_amountBIGINT NULLFree shipping above this subtotal. Not present on the reference platform; a cheap, effective lever.
estimated_days_min/maxSMALLINT NULLRenders "2–5 working days".
available_regionsJSON NULLNULL means everywhere. Lets an upcountry method be hidden for Dar es Salaam addresses.
position, is_active, deleted_at

9.3.21 orders

ColumnTypeNotes
idBIGINT UNSIGNED PK
order_numberVARCHAR(24) NOT NULL UNIQUETS-{YYYY}-{000000}, sequence per year. Human-quotable over the phone.
user_idBIGINT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULLNULL for guest orders. The order survives account deletion.
statusVARCHAR(24) NOT NULLCHECK against the state machine in §12.7.
payment_statusVARCHAR(24) NOT NULLCHECK IN ('unpaid','pending_verification','paid','partially_refunded','refunded','failed').
fulfilment_statusVARCHAR(24) NOT NULLCHECK IN ('unfulfilled','processing','shipped','delivered','returned').
customer_nameVARCHAR(120) NOT NULLSnapshot. Never joined from users for display.
customer_emailVARCHAR(255) NULL
customer_phoneVARCHAR(15) NOT NULLThe one field every order must have — it is how the store contacts the buyer.
shipping_method_idBIGINT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL
shipping_method_nameVARCHAR(80) NOT NULLSnapshot.
subtotal_amountBIGINT NOT NULLSum of line totals.
discount_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
shipping_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
tax_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Zero at launch; the column exists so adding VAT is not a migration of every historical row.
total_amountBIGINT NOT NULLStored, not computed on read — the total charged must never change because a formula changed.
paid_amountBIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0Sum of settled payments. Supports partial payment.
currencyCHAR(3) NOT NULL
payment_methodVARCHAR(24) NULLCHECK IN ('lipa_namba','cash','aggregator').
payment_numberVARCHAR(15) NULLThe mobile-money number the buyer will pay from — the key to manual reconciliation (§13.6).
delegated_payer_emailVARCHAR(255) NULLSet when a third party will pay.
payment_token_hashCHAR(64) NULL UNIQUEBacks /pay/:token.
payment_token_expires_atDATETIME(3) NULL
idempotency_keyCHAR(36) NULL UNIQUEPrevents duplicate orders (§7.7.3).
customer_noteVARCHAR(500) NULL
internal_noteTEXT NULLStaff only; never returned to a customer-facing endpoint.
cancelled_at, cancel_reasonDATETIME(3) / VARCHAR(255) NULL
placed_at, paid_at, shipped_at, delivered_atDATETIME(3) NULLDenormalised from the event log for cheap reporting.
sourceVARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'web''web' | 'admin' | 'phone'.

Indexes. uq_orders_order_number, idx_orders_user_placed, idx_orders_status_placed, idx_orders_payment_status, idx_orders_customer_phone, idx_orders_placed_at, uq_orders_idempotency_key, uq_orders_payment_token_hash.

Three statuses, not one The reference platform carries a single order status ("Completed", "Pending", "Cancelled"), which cannot express the ordinary case of an order that is paid but not yet shipped, or shipped but not yet paid for on delivery. Splitting lifecycle, payment and fulfilment into three independent axes is what makes the admin console usable and the reports truthful.

9.3.22 order_items

id · order_id FK CASCADE · product_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · product_variant_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · product_name VARCHAR(255) snapshot · variant_sku VARCHAR(20) snapshot · variant_attributes JSON snapshot — e.g. [{"name":"Resistance","value":"10kΩ"}] · image_url VARCHAR(512) NULL snapshot · unit_price_amount BIGINT · quantity INT CHECK (> 0) · discount_amount BIGINT DEFAULT 0 · line_total_amount BIGINT. Index on order_id and on product_id for "who bought this" reporting.

9.3.23 order_shipping_addresses

One row per order, a frozen copy: order_id FK CASCADE UNIQUE · receiver_name · email · phone · country · region · district · street_address · postal_code. Absent for pickup orders. Kept in its own table rather than as columns on orders so that the order row stays narrow and the address can be omitted cleanly.

9.3.24 order_events

The append-only audit trail of an order's life. id · order_id FK CASCADE · event_type VARCHAR(40) ('placed', 'payment_recorded', 'payment_verified', 'status_changed', 'note_added', 'cancelled', 'refunded', 'shipped', 'delivered', 'email_sent', 'sms_sent') · from_value, to_value VARCHAR(40) NULL · actor_type VARCHAR(16) ('customer','staff','system','webhook') · actor_user_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · message VARCHAR(500) NULL · metadata JSON NULL · created_at. Index on (order_id, created_at). Never updated, never deleted. This table is the source for the customer timeline (§7.9.2), the admin history panel and every dispute.

9.3.25 payments and payment_events

TableColumns
paymentsid · order_id FK RESTRICT · method VARCHAR(24) · provider VARCHAR(40) NULL ('manual','selcom','clickpesa','azampay','dpo') · amount BIGINT · currency CHAR(3) · status VARCHAR(24) CHECK IN ('initiated','pending','succeeded','failed','cancelled','refunded') · payer_phone VARCHAR(15) NULL · payer_name VARCHAR(120) NULL · provider_reference VARCHAR(120) NULL — the mobile-money transaction id · merchant_reference VARCHAR(64) — our own reference sent to the provider · verified_by BIGINT NULL FK — the staff member who confirmed a manual payment · verified_at · failure_reason VARCHAR(255) NULL · raw_payload JSON NULL. Unique on (provider, provider_reference) where both are non-null — the single constraint that makes double-crediting impossible.
payment_eventsid · payment_id BIGINT NULL FK CASCADE · provider · event_type · external_event_id VARCHAR(120) UNIQUE — webhook idempotency · signature_valid TINYINT(1) · payload JSON · processed_at DATETIME(3) NULL · processing_error VARCHAR(500) NULL. Every inbound webhook is written here before it is acted upon, so a mis-processed callback can be replayed (§13.5).

9.3.26 stock_movements

Every change to stock, ever. id · product_variant_id FK RESTRICT · delta INT NOT NULL (signed) · balance_after INT NOT NULL · reason VARCHAR(32) CHECK IN ('purchase','sale','reservation','release','adjustment','return','damage','stocktake') · reference_type VARCHAR(32) NULL · reference_id BIGINT NULL · actor_user_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · note VARCHAR(255) NULL · created_at. Index on (product_variant_id, created_at). Stock is never adjusted without a corresponding movement row, in the same transaction — which is what makes "why is this figure wrong?" an answerable question.

9.3.27 invoices and invoice_items

invoices: id · invoice_number VARCHAR(24) UNIQUE (INV-{YYYY}-{000000}) · order_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL — an invoice may be raised without an order, which is exactly the quotation case (persona Grace) · client_name VARCHAR(120) · client_email VARCHAR(255) · client_phone VARCHAR(15) NULL · client_address VARCHAR(500) NULL · shipping_method_name VARCHAR(80) NULL · subtotal_amount · shipping_amount · tax_amount · total_amount · currency · status VARCHAR(20) CHECK IN ('draft','issued','paid','void') · issued_at · due_at NULL · paid_at NULL · share_token_hash CHAR(64) UNIQUE NULL · notes TEXT NULL · created_by FK → users · deleted_at.

invoice_items: id · invoice_id FK CASCADE · product_variant_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · description VARCHAR(255) · sku VARCHAR(20) NULL · unit_price_amount BIGINT · quantity INT · line_total_amount BIGINT · position INT. Free-text lines are permitted so an invoice can carry a service charge that is not a catalogue product.

9.3.28 Content tables

TableColumns
bannersid · title VARCHAR(120) · title_color CHAR(7) · subtitle VARCHAR(300) NULL · subtitle_color CHAR(7) · background_color CHAR(7) · image_id FK RESTRICT · link_url VARCHAR(512) NULL · button_text VARCHAR(40) NULL · button_background CHAR(7) · button_text_color CHAR(7) · placement VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'home' · position INT · starts_at, ends_at DATETIME(3) NULL — scheduled banners, which the reference platform lacks · is_active · deleted_at.

commentsid · article_id FK CASCADE · user_id FK SET NULL · parent_id BIGINT NULL FK CASCADE (one level of replies) · author_name VARCHAR(120) snapshot · body TEXT · status CHECK IN ('pending','approved','rejected','spam') · moderated_by, moderated_at · ip_address VARBINARY(16) · deleted_at.
