9.3.29 Marketing and analytics tables

TableColumns
contactsid · user_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · name VARCHAR(120) · email VARCHAR(255) NULL · phone VARCHAR(15) NULL · source VARCHAR(24) ('registration','order','manual','import','contact_form') · email_opt_in, sms_opt_in TINYINT(1) · unsubscribed_at DATETIME(3) NULL · tags JSON · deleted_at. Unique on email and on phone where non-null. A marketing audience distinct from the user table, because a contact may come from a form or an import and never hold an account.
campaignsid · channel CHECK IN ('sms','email') · name VARCHAR(160) — the subject line for email · audience CHECK IN ('all','specific','tag','segment') · audience_filter JSON NULL · body_text TEXT — SMS body or email plain-text part · body_html MEDIUMTEXT NULL · additional_recipients JSON NULL — ad-hoc numbers or addresses typed in the composer · scheduled_for DATETIME(3) NULL · status CHECK IN ('draft','scheduled','sending','sent','failed','cancelled') · recipient_count, sent_count, failed_count, delivered_count INT · cost_amount BIGINT NULL — SMS is metered, so the spend belongs on the record · created_by FK · sent_at · deleted_at.
campaign_recipientsid · campaign_id FK CASCADE · contact_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · destination VARCHAR(255) — the address or number actually used · status CHECK IN ('queued','sent','delivered','failed','bounced','unsubscribed') · provider_message_id VARCHAR(120) NULL · error VARCHAR(255) NULL · sent_at, delivered_at. Index on (campaign_id, status).
search_logsid · term VARCHAR(160) · normalised_term VARCHAR(160) — lowercase, trimmed, whitespace collapsed; this is what is aggregated · result_count INT · user_id BIGINT NULL · session_id CHAR(32) · clicked_product_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL — the click-through that makes success measurable · created_at. Index on (normalised_term, created_at) and on (result_count, created_at) to find zero-result searches fast.
contact_messagesid · name · email · subject VARCHAR(200) NULL · message TEXT · send_copy TINYINT(1) · status CHECK IN ('new','read','replied','archived','spam') · handled_by FK NULL · ip_address · user_agent.
notify_requestsid · product_variant_id FK CASCADE · email VARCHAR(255) · user_id BIGINT NULL · notified_at DATETIME(3) NULL. Unique on (product_variant_id, email). Backs "Notify me when available" — and doubles as a demand signal for restocking.

9.3.30 audit_logs

id · actor_user_id BIGINT NULL FK SET NULL · actor_email VARCHAR(255) snapshot · action VARCHAR(60) ('product.create', 'order.status_change', 'user.suspend') · entity_type VARCHAR(60) · entity_id BIGINT NULL · entity_label VARCHAR(255) — a human-readable name captured at the time · before JSON NULL · after JSON NULL · ip_address VARBINARY(16) · user_agent VARCHAR(255) · request_id CHAR(26) · created_at. Indexes on (entity_type, entity_id, created_at), (actor_user_id, created_at) and (action, created_at). Append-only, enforced by database privileges: the application's runtime user is granted INSERT and SELECT on this table and nothing else. Retained 24 months.

9.4 Indexing strategy

Indexes are specified for the queries that actually run, listed here with the query each serves. Anything not on this list is speculative and should not be created.

QueryIndex
Catalogue grid, subcategory scope, sorted by newestproducts(subcategory_id, status, published_at DESC)
Catalogue grid, sorted by best sellingproducts(status, order_count DESC)
Catalogue grid, price filterproducts(status, min_price_amount)
Product detail by slugproducts(slug) unique
Retired-slug redirectproduct_slug_history(slug) unique
Keyword searchFULLTEXT products(name, description_text, brand, manufacturer_part_number); plus product_variants(sku) unique for the exact-SKU fast path
Variant lookup for a productproduct_variants(product_id, is_active, position)
Low-stock dashboard alertproduct_variants(stock_quantity) — scanned with a threshold predicate
Customer order historyorders(user_id, placed_at DESC)
Admin order queue by statusorders(status, placed_at DESC)
Payment reconciliation by phoneorders(customer_phone), payments(payer_phone)
Revenue over a periodorders(payment_status, paid_at)
Order timelineorder_events(order_id, created_at)
Stock ledger for one variantstock_movements(product_variant_id, created_at DESC)
Approved reviews for a productreviews(product_id, status, created_at DESC)
Zero-result searchessearch_logs(result_count, created_at)
Search-term aggregationsearch_logs(normalised_term, created_at)
Campaign delivery reportcampaign_recipients(campaign_id, status)
Audit history for one entityaudit_logs(entity_type, entity_id, created_at DESC)
Session validation and cleanupsessions(refresh_token_hash) unique, sessions(expires_at)
Full-text configuration MySQL's default full-text minimum token length is 3, which silently drops two-character searches. Electronics part numbers are full of short tokens. Set innodb_ft_min_token_size = 2 and add an innodb_ft_user_stopword_table that is empty, because the default stopword list removes real terms. Verify after any server upgrade — a reverted setting fails silently and shows up only as poor search results.

9.5 Referential integrity and delete semantics

RelationshipOn deleteReasoning
category → subcategoriesRESTRICTA category with subcategories cannot be removed. This is the direct fix for defect R12: the reference platform cascades a category delete through its subcategories and into every one of their products.
subcategory → productsRESTRICTSame reasoning. The admin console offers "move products to another subcategory" as the supported path.
product → variants, images, attributesCASCADEThese have no meaning apart from their product.
variant → order_itemsSET NULLHistorical orders survive; the snapshot columns keep them readable.
variant → cart_itemsRESTRICTForces the deletion path to clear affected carts explicitly and notify their owners, rather than silently emptying a cart.
user → ordersSET NULLFinancial records outlive accounts. The snapshot fields preserve who bought what.
user → sessions, tokens, addresses, cartCASCADEPersonal data goes with the account.
order → items, events, addressCASCADEOnly relevant if an order is ever hard-deleted, which the application never does.
order → paymentsRESTRICTAn order with a recorded payment cannot be deleted, under any circumstances.
media_file → any referenceRESTRICTA referenced file cannot be deleted; the reference count enforces it before the constraint has to.
article → commentsCASCADE
campaign → recipientsCASCADE
Soft delete is the default; hard delete is an operation Every customer-facing and administrator-facing delete sets deleted_at. Rows are removed from the database only by a scheduled purge — 90 days for catalogue entities, never for anything with a financial or audit dimension. The admin console gains a "Trash" view per entity with restore. This one rule converts the reference platform's most dangerous action, a bulk category delete that destroys a swathe of catalogue with no recovery, into a reversible mistake.

9.6 Full DDL

The complete, executable CREATE TABLE script — all 50 tables with every column, constraint, index and foreign key — is Appendix E. It is written to run against a clean MySQL 8.0 schema in a single pass, ordered so that no foreign key references a table that does not yet exist.

Part TenREST API Specification

10.1 Conventions

RuleSpecification
Base path/api/v1. Versioned from day one — the reference platform's unversioned /api leaves no way to change a response shape without breaking every cached client.
Content typeapplication/json; charset=utf-8, except upload endpoints which accept multipart/form-data.
CasingcamelCase in JSON; snake_case in the database. The mapping happens once, in the serialisation layer.
IdentifiersPublic identifiers only — slugs, order numbers, ULIDs. Numeric primary keys are never returned.
MoneyReturned as an object: {"amount": 35000, "currency": "TZS", "formatted": "TZS 35,000"}. The server formats, so a price never renders differently in two places.
TimestampsISO 8601 with an explicit offset: 2026-08-19T14:32:05.123Z. Always UTC.
Status codes200 read or update · 201 create, with a Location header · 204 delete · 400 malformed · 401 unauthenticated · 403 unauthorised · 404 absent · 409 conflict · 410 gone · 422 validation · 429 rate limited · 500 server.
Success assertionClients must test response.ok — the 2xx class — never a specific code. Fixes defect R8.
CollectionsAlways an envelope, never a bare array:
{"data":[…],"meta":{"page":1,"pageSize":24,"total":617,"pageCount":26},"links":{"next":"…","prev":null}}. The reference platform returns bare arrays, which means a paginated response can never be introduced without a breaking change.
Single resources{"data":{…}}. Same reason.
Sparse fields?fields=id,name,price on read endpoints for expensive lists.
Expansion?include=variants,images,reviews. Each endpoint documents which expansions it accepts; unknown values are a 422, never silently ignored.
Sorting?sort=-createdAt,name. A leading minus is descending. Only whitelisted fields.
Pagination?page= and ?pageSize=, default 24, maximum 100. Cursor pagination (?cursor=) additionally on audit_logs, order_events and search_logs, where offsets get expensive.
IdempotencyIdempotency-Key header honoured on POST /orders and POST /payments. Replays within 24 hours return the original response.
CorrelationEvery response carries X-Request-Id, echoed into logs and shown on error screens.
DeprecationRetiring an endpoint sends Deprecation and Sunset headers for at least 90 days before removal.

10.2 Error format

One shape for every error, at every status code.

{
  "error": {
    "code": "VALIDATION_FAILED",
    "message": "Some fields need attention.",
    "requestId": "01JBQ7X2K9P3M4N5Q6R7S8T9V0",
    "details": [
      { "field": "phone",    "code": "invalid_length",
        "message": "Phone number must be 12 characters long" },
      { "field": "items[2].quantity", "code": "insufficient_stock",
        "message": "Only 2 available", "meta": { "available": 2 } }
    ]
  }
}
CodeStatusRaised when
VALIDATION_FAILED422Zod parse failed. details lists every failing field, not just the first.
UNAUTHENTICATED401Missing, malformed or expired access token.
FORBIDDEN403Authenticated but lacking the required permission.
NOT_FOUND404No such resource, or one the caller may not see. Never distinguishes the two.
GONE410Soft-deleted resource that used to be public.
CONFLICT409Unique constraint or a state-machine violation.
PRICE_CHANGED409Checkout revalidation found a different price. details carries old and new per line.
INSUFFICIENT_STOCK409Checkout revalidation found too little stock. details carries the available quantity per line.
INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION409Order status change not permitted by §12.7.
TOKEN_INVALID400Verification, reset, payment or invoice token bad, used or expired — one code for all four cases, deliberately.
RATE_LIMITED429With a Retry-After header.
PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE413Body or upload over the cap.
UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE415Upload MIME type not on the allowlist.
INTERNAL_ERROR500Message is always generic. Details go to logs, never to the client.

10.3 Authentication endpoints

VerbPathAccessBehaviour
POST/auth/registerpublicBody {username, email?, phone?, password, acceptedTerms}. At least one of email or phone required. Creates the user, issues tokens, queues a verification email. 201.
POST/auth/loginpublicBody {identifier, password} where identifier is an email or a phone. Sets the refresh cookie, returns the access token and profile. Rate limited per §10.9.
POST/auth/refreshcookieRotates the refresh token, returns a new access token. Reuse of a rotated token revokes the whole family (§11.2).
POST/auth/logouttokenRevokes the current session and clears the cookie. 204.
POST/auth/logout-alltokenRevokes every session for the user.
GET/auth/oauth/:providerpublic302 to the provider with PKCE and a state parameter.
GET/auth/oauth/:provider/callbackpublicValidates state, exchanges the code, links or creates the user, then 302 to the SPA with a one-time exchange code — never with a token in the URL, which would leak through referrers and history.
POST/auth/oauth/exchangepublicSwaps the one-time code for a session.
POST/auth/email/verify/requesttokenRe-sends verification. Limited to 3 per hour.
POST/auth/email/verifypublicBody {token}. Marks verified.
POST/auth/password/forgotpublicAlways 200 with the same body, whether or not the address exists.
POST/auth/password/resetpublicBody {token, password}. Revokes all sessions.
PATCH/auth/passwordtokenBody {currentPassword, newPassword}. Revokes other sessions.
GET/metokenCurrent profile, roles and permission keys.
PATCH/metokenBody {username?, email?, phone?, marketingOptIn?}. Changing email clears verification and re-sends.
POST/me/avatartokenMultipart, field file.
GET POST/me/addressestokenList and create.
PATCH DELETE/me/addresses/:idtokenUpdate and soft-delete.

10.3.1 Login response

200 OK
Set-Cookie: ts_rt=<opaque>; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/api/v1/auth; Max-Age=2592000
{
  "data": {
    "accessToken": "eyJhbGciOi…",
    "expiresIn": 900,
    "user": {
      "id": "01JBQ7X2K9P3M4N5Q6R7S8T9V0",
      "username": "Amina Mushi",
      "email": "amina@example.com",
      "emailVerified": false,
      "phone": "255712345678",
      "accountType": "customer",
      "profileImage": { "sm": "…", "md": "…", "lg": "…" },
      "roles": [],
      "permissions": []
    }
  }
}

10.4 Catalogue endpoints

VerbPathAccessBehaviour
GET/categoriespublic?include=subcategories, ?activeOnly=true. Cached 5 minutes at the edge and invalidated on write.
GET/categories/:slugpublicWith its subcategories and counts.
GET/subcategoriespublic?categorySlug=.
GET/productspublicThe catalogue feed. Query: page, pageSize, sort (bestSelling|newest|priceAsc|priceDesc|random), categorySlug, subcategorySlug, minPrice, maxPrice, inStock, onOffer, tag, ids.
GET/products/:slugpublicFull detail with variants, attributes, images and review summary. 410 when soft-deleted; 301 hint when the slug is retired.
GET/products/:slug/relatedpublicUp to 12 from the same subcategory, by sales.
GET/products/:slug/reviewspublicApproved only, paginated, with the rating histogram.
POST/products/:slug/reviewstokenBody {rating, title?, body}. Created as pending. 409 if the user has already reviewed.
PATCH DELETE/reviews/:idtokenOwn review only, or with review.moderate.
GET/searchpublic?q= plus every catalogue filter. Logs the term with its result count. ?suggest=true returns the lightweight 8-item shape for the header dropdown and does not log.
GET/locations/regionspublicCached 24 hours.
GET/locations/regions/:region/districtspublicCached 24 hours.
GET/shipping-methodspublic?region= filters to methods available there.

10.6 Payments

VerbPathAccessBehaviour
GET/pay/:tokentokenOrder summary for the delegated payer. Deliberately omits the buyer's address, phone and email.

POST/webhooks/payments/:providersignatureAggregator callback. Verifies the signature, writes a payment_events row, then processes idempotently by external_event_id. Always returns 200 once persisted, even if processing is deferred — a provider that receives a non-200 will retry forever.

POST/admin/orders/:orderNumber/refundsadminBody {amount, reason}. Records a refund and restores stock when the order was already fulfilled.

Part ElevenAuthentication, Authorisation and Security

11.1 Identity model

One users table holds both customers and staff, distinguished by account_type and by the roles attached to them. Two tables would duplicate authentication, password reset, session handling and profile management for no benefit, and would make "promote this customer to staff" a data migration.

ConceptDefinition
IdentifierA user is reachable by email, by phone, or by both. Login accepts either in a single field, as the reference platform does; the server decides which by shape. At least one must exist, enforced by a check constraint.
CredentialA password hash, an OAuth link, or both. A user who registered with Google and later sets a password holds both and may use either.
GuestNo account. Identified within a session by a guest_token cookie that owns a cart and a wishlist. A guest may place an order; the order stores their name and phone directly.
Staffaccount_type = 'staff' plus at least one role. Staff sign in through the same modal; the admin console appears in their account menu.
RoleA named bundle of permissions. Users may hold several; effective permissions are the union.
PermissionAn atomic capability string, resource.action. This is the unit checked at every guard.

11.2 Token strategy

The single most important divergence from the reference platform The reference platform stores its bearer token in localStorage (defect R9). Any script that executes on the page — an injected third-party tag, a compromised dependency, a stored XSS — can read it and exfiltrate a full session. TechStar splits the session in two so that no single compromise is sufficient.
TokenSpecification
Access tokenJWT, 15-minute lifetime, signed HS256 with a 32-byte secret (or RS256 if the API is ever split across services). Held in memory only — a JavaScript variable inside the auth store, never in localStorage, sessionStorage or a cookie. Claims: sub (user public id), typ, roles, perms, sid (session id), iat, exp, jti. Sent as Authorization: Bearer.
Refresh token32 bytes of cryptographic randomness, base64url, stored server-side as a SHA-256 hash. Delivered as a cookie: HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/api/v1/auth; Max-Age=2592000 (30 days). Unreadable from JavaScript by construction.
RotationEvery call to /auth/refresh issues a new refresh token and marks the old one replaced. Refresh tokens are strictly single-use.
Reuse detectionPresenting a refresh token that has already been replaced means it was stolen. The entire session family is revoked immediately, the user is signed out everywhere, and a security email is sent. This is the mechanism that limits the damage of a stolen cookie to a single refresh cycle.
Silent refreshThe client refreshes at 80% of the access token's life, and once on application boot to restore a session after a reload. Concurrent 401s are queued behind a single refresh, then replayed.
RevocationSign-out revokes the current session; password change and reset revoke all others. Revoked session ids are held in a Redis set until their natural expiry, so an unexpired access token cannot outlive its revocation.
Socket authenticationThe Socket.IO handshake carries the access token and is verified server-side. Fixes defect R9's second half: the reference platform authenticates sockets with a raw user id, which anyone can supply.

11.3 OAuth

Google and Facebook, both authorization-code flow with PKCE. Implicit flow is not used under any circumstances.

1  Client → GET /api/v1/auth/oauth/google
2  Server generates code_verifier + state, stores both against a short-lived
   cookie, redirects to the provider
3  Provider → GET /auth/oauth/google/callback?code=…&state=…
4  Server validates state (CSRF), exchanges code + verifier for tokens,
   fetches the profile
5  Account resolution
     a. oauth_accounts row for (provider, providerUserId) exists → sign in
     b. no row, but a verified user has that email → link, then sign in
     c. no row, no match → create the user with email_verified_at set,
        since the provider has already verified it
6  Server issues its own session, redirects to
   /auth/oauth/callback?code=<one-time>
7  Client → POST /auth/oauth/exchange { code } → session established
Why the extra exchange step Redirecting with a token in the URL leaks it into browser history, the Referer header of any subsequent request, and server access logs. The one-time exchange code is valid for 60 seconds, single-use, and bound to the originating browser. Case (b) links only against a verified email — otherwise anyone could claim an account by registering its address at a provider.

11.4 Email verification, password reset, password rules

11.4.1 Email verification

Registration queues a verification email carrying a 32-byte token, hashed at rest, valid 24 hours, single-use. Until verified, the account can browse, buy and pay — verification is not a gate on commerce, which would cost orders — but it is required to write reviews or comments, and the persistent banner (§7.1.5) keeps asking. Requesting a new link invalidates all outstanding ones. Rate limited to 3 per hour.

11.4.2 Password reset

Token of 32 bytes, hashed at rest, valid 60 minutes, single-use. The request endpoint always returns the same response whether or not the address exists, and takes the same measured time either way — a fast negative is as good an oracle as a different message. On success, every session is revoked and a confirmation email is sent to the address on file, so an unauthorised reset is visible to the real owner.

11.4.3 Password rules

RuleSpecification
LengthMinimum 10 characters, maximum 128. Length is the only requirement that reliably increases strength.
CompositionNo mandatory character classes. Forced classes push users toward Password1!; they measurably reduce entropy.
BlocklistRejected against the 100,000 most common passwords and against the user's own email, username and phone.
Breach checkOptional k-anonymity lookup against Have I Been Pwned's range API — only the first five characters of the SHA-1 hash leave the server. Fails open if the service is unreachable.
Strength meterzxcvbn, shown live during registration and change. Advisory, not blocking, beyond the hard rules above.
HashingArgon2id, memory 19 MiB, iterations 2, parallelism 1 — the OWASP baseline. Parameters are stored with the hash so they can be raised later and rehashed on next successful login.
ComparisonConstant-time. On an unknown identifier, a dummy hash is still verified so that response time does not reveal whether the account exists.

11.5 Authorisation — the RBAC matrix

Permissions are checked on the server for every request. Route guards in the client are convenience only (§6.5).

Permission groupKeys
Catalogueproduct.read create update delete publish · category.read create update delete · discount.read create update delete · stock.read adjust · media.read upload delete
Commerceorder.read update cancel refund export · invoice.read create update delete send · shipping.read create update delete · payment.verify refund
Customerscustomer.read update suspend export
Contentbanner.* service.* partner.* article.* faq.* (read create update delete) · comment.moderate · review.moderate
Marketingcampaign.read create send delete · contact.read import export
Platformsettings.read update · staff.manage · audit.view · analytics.view · dashboard.view
RoleCatalogueOrdersCustomersContentMarketingPlatform
ownerfullfullfullfullfullfull
managerfullfullfullfullfullanalytics, audit
cataloguefullread—media only—dashboard
fulfilmentread, stockfullread——dashboard
marketingreadreadreadfullfullanalytics
supportreadread, cancelreadmoderate—dashboard
Structural rules At least one active owner must exist at all times — the system refuses to remove the last one. Nobody may edit their own roles. payment.verify and order.refund are never granted to support; separating who takes the order from who confirms the money is basic segregation of duties. Every permission change is written to the audit log with before and after.

11.6 Input validation

LayerSpecification
SchemaEvery endpoint declares Zod schemas for params, query and body in packages/contracts. The middleware replaces the raw request values with the parsed, typed result, so a controller cannot accidentally read an unvalidated field.
StrictnessObjects are strict — an unexpected property is a 422, not silently dropped. This catches typos in client code and blocks mass-assignment.
CoercionExplicit only. Query strings coerce to number or boolean where the schema says so; nowhere else.
NormalisationEmail lowercased and trimmed. Phone stripped to digits and canonicalised to 255XXXXXXXXX, accepting +255…, 0… and 255… as input. Names trimmed with internal whitespace collapsed. Slugs generated per §6.6.
HTMLRich text is sanitised with an allowlist on write and on render. Two independent passes, because a single point of sanitisation is a single point of failure.
SQLPrisma parameterises everything. The few raw queries in Part 18 use $queryRaw with tagged-template parameters; string concatenation into SQL is forbidden and caught by lint rule.
Mass assignmentControllers pass explicit field lists to services. No ...req.body spread into an update, ever — that is how a customer sets account_type = 'staff'.
OwnershipEvery resource read or write verifies the caller owns the row or holds the permission. Never trust an id in a URL to belong to its bearer.
EnumerationSequential ids never appear in URLs (§9.1), so resources cannot be walked.

11.7 Upload security

ControlSpecification
Accepted typesimage/jpeg, image/png, image/webp, image/avif. SVG is rejected — it is a script-execution vector, and vector logos should be converted to PNG on the way in.
Type determinationMagic bytes, not the client's Content-Type and not the file extension. A mismatch between claimed and actual type is a rejection, not a correction.
Size3 MB per file, 30 MB per request, 100 files per product. Enforced by the multipart parser before anything touches disk.
DimensionsMaximum 8000 × 8000 px. Larger images are rejected rather than resized, which stops decompression-bomb attacks.
Re-encodingEvery accepted image is decoded and re-encoded by sharp. The original bytes are never served. This strips any embedded payload and, incidentally, all EXIF including GPS coordinates.
NamingStored under a generated ULID with a canonical extension. The user's filename is retained as metadata only, and never used as a path component.
Storage locationOutside the web root. Served through a handler that sets Content-Type, Content-Disposition: inline, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff and long-lived immutable cache headers.
ExecutionThe upload directory is configured with execution disabled at the web-server level, belt and braces.
AuthorisationOnly staff with media.upload may upload, except the customer avatar endpoint, which is limited to one file, 2 MB, 1024 px.
Rate30 uploads per hour per user.

11.8 Security checklist

RiskControl
Broken access controlServer-side permission check on every route; ownership check on every resource; no sequential ids in URLs; strict schemas prevent mass assignment; automated tests assert that each admin route rejects a customer token.
Cryptographic failuresTLS 1.2+ only, HSTS with a one-year max-age and preload. Argon2id for passwords, SHA-256 for token storage. Secrets from the environment, never in the repository. Database at rest on an encrypted volume. Backups encrypted with a separately managed key.
InjectionParameterised queries throughout; HTML sanitised twice; no shell interpolation anywhere; JSON body size capped.
Insecure designOrder state machine rejects illegal transitions server-side; stock changes are transactional with a ledger row; payment verification is a separate permission from order handling; idempotency keys on order and payment creation.
Security misconfigurationConfig validated at boot and fails fast; production build strips development hosts (fixing R10); X-Powered-By removed; directory listing off; error responses carry no stack traces; distinct credentials per environment.
Vulnerable componentsnpm audit and Dependabot in CI; the build fails on a high or critical advisory; a monthly patch window; lockfiles committed.
Authentication failuresRate limits and progressive lockout; identical responses for unknown and wrong credentials; refresh rotation with reuse detection; sessions listed and revocable by the user.
Data integrity failuresWebhook signatures verified before processing; every inbound event persisted before it is acted on; Subresource Integrity on the few external assets; CI publishes build provenance.
Logging failuresStructured logs with a request id on every line; authentication events, permission denials, payment verifications and admin mutations all logged; passwords, tokens, card-like numbers and full phone numbers redacted by a logger serializer; audit log is insert-only at the database-privilege level.
SSRFNo endpoint fetches a user-supplied URL. Outbound calls go only to a fixed allowlist of provider hosts.
XSSReact escapes by default; dangerouslySetInnerHTML permitted in exactly one component (§8.2), enforced by lint; CSP without unsafe-inline, using nonces for the few inline scripts; access token never in a readable store.
CSRFRefresh cookie is SameSite=Strict and scoped to /api/v1/auth; all other authentication is a bearer header, which CSRF cannot forge; state-changing requests require Content-Type: application/json, which blocks simple form posts.
Clickjackingframe-ancestors 'none' plus X-Frame-Options: DENY.
EnumerationIdentical responses and timing for unknown accounts, expired tokens and invalid tokens throughout.
Denial of serviceRate limits at several scopes; body and upload caps; query complexity bounded by a maximum page size; slow-query logging with alerting; Nginx connection limits.

11.8.1 Response headers

Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload
Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self';
  script-src 'self' 'nonce-{random}' https://www.googletagmanager.com;
  style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline';
  img-src 'self' data: blob: https://*.tile.openstreetmap.org;
  font-src 'self';
  connect-src 'self' https://www.google-analytics.com wss://{host};
  frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self';
  object-src 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests
X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff
X-Frame-Options: DENY
Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin
Permissions-Policy: geolocation=(), microphone=(), camera=(), payment=()
Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin
Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin

style-src 'unsafe-inline' is required because banners apply administrator-chosen colours as inline styles. The alternative — emitting a nonce per banner — is achievable and preferable, and is listed as a hardening item for Phase 5.

11.8.2 Personal data

ObligationImplementation
MinimisationOnly what an order needs: name, phone, and an address when the shipping method requires one. Date of birth, gender and national identifiers are never collected.
Consentmarketing_opt_in is off by default and captured explicitly. Campaign sending excludes anyone without consent or with an unsubscribe timestamp — the reference platform mails every contact, which is a live compliance exposure.
UnsubscribeOne-click, honoured within seconds, present in every campaign email and every marketing SMS.
Access and portabilityA customer may request an export of their personal data; the admin console produces a JSON bundle of profile, addresses, orders, reviews and comments.
ErasureAccount closure anonymises the user row — name replaced with "Deleted user", email and phone nulled, avatar removed — while orders are retained with their snapshot fields for statutory record-keeping. This is the only way to satisfy both obligations at once.
RetentionGuest carts 30 days · search logs 24 months · audit logs 24 months · sessions until expiry · orders and invoices retained per Tanzanian record-keeping requirements, minimum 7 years.

Part TwelveBusiness Rules and Domain Logic

This part is the contract the server enforces. Every rule here is checked server-side regardless of what the client does. Where a rule exists to correct a defect in the reference platform, the defect number is given.

12.1 SKU scheme

Inherited from the reference platform because it is genuinely good: a positional code makes a physical component store tractable, since a technician can read a bin label and know exactly where it sits in the taxonomy.

Category      2 digits    00–99      assigned on category creation
Subcategory   4 digits    CCSS       category code + 2-digit sequence within it
Product       7 digits    CCSSPPP    subcategory code + 3-digit sequence within it
Variant       9 digits    CCSSPPPVV  product code + 2-digit sequence within it

  05        Sensors & Conditioning Boards
  0510      Sensors > Voltage
  0510003   Sensors > Voltage > PZEM-004T AC Energy Meter
  051000301 …the 100 A variant
RuleSpecification
AssignmentAutomatic. The sequence is the lowest unused number within the parent's scope, allocated inside the creating transaction with a row lock on the parent so two concurrent creations cannot collide.
Manual overrideAn administrator may type a specific code when creating a category or subcategory — this is how an existing physical numbering scheme is preserved. Uniqueness is still enforced.
ImmutabilityA SKU never changes. Moving a product to a different subcategory keeps its original SKU and records the move in the audit log. Renumbering would invalidate every printed bin label, every past invoice and every supplier order.
ReuseNever. A deleted product's code is retired permanently, so a historical invoice can always be traced.
Capacity100 categories, 100 subcategories each, 1,000 products each, 100 variants each — 10⁹ variants. Ample. If a subcategory ever exceeds 1,000 products, the correct response is to split it, which is also the correct merchandising response.
SearchThe full SKU and any left-anchored prefix are searchable. Typing 0510 returns everything in Voltage.

12.2 Product grouping and variants

The defect being fixed (R2) The reference platform creates one document per attribute combination and reconstructs the product at read time by grouping on the name string. Consequences observed in live data: names carrying double spaces and trailing whitespace; grouping that silently splits on any typographical difference; a hard prohibition on two distinct products ever sharing a name; and a product URL that is a name, so a rename breaks every link. All of it follows from one modelling choice.
RuleSpecification
StructureA product row is the group. Each purchasable configuration is a product_variant row. Every product has at least one variant, even when it has no options.
AttributesA product declares its attribute columns in product_attributes — ordered, optionally with a unit. Each variant supplies one value per attribute. A variant missing a value for a declared attribute is invalid and rejected on save.
UniquenessWithin one product, no two variants may share the same complete set of attribute values. Enforced by an application check plus a generated hash column with a unique index.
Name hygieneNames are trimmed and internal whitespace collapsed on write. Duplicate names across products are permitted, since identity is the slug, not the name.
Which variant is defaultThe lowest position among active, in-stock variants; if all are out of stock, the lowest position overall. The product page preselects it.
Aggregatestotal_stock, min_price_amount and max_price_amount are recomputed on the product row inside any transaction that changes a variant's price, stock or active flag. They are never computed on read — the catalogue grid must not join to variants.
Deleting a variantSoft delete. Refused if it is the last active variant — delete the product instead. Refused with a warning if it appears in any open cart, listing how many.
Adding an attribute laterAdding a column to a product that already has variants requires a value for each existing variant. The admin console prompts for them in one screen rather than silently writing blanks.

12.3 Pricing and discounts

RuleSpecification
StorageInteger minor units on the variant. Never a float, never a string.
FloorCHECK (price_amount > 0). A zero-price product cannot exist — fixing defect R16, where a live product sells for TZS 0. Genuinely free items are modelled as a 100%-discounted line on an order, never as a zero price.
Compare-atOptional compare_at_amount, which must exceed price_amount when set. Renders as a struck-through "was" price. This is a permanent list price, distinct from a time-boxed discount.
Discount scopesA discount targets a variant, a product, a subcategory or a category. Type is percentage (1–90) or a fixed amount.
ResolutionWhen several discounts apply, the most specific scope wins: variant, then product, then subcategory, then category. Within one scope, the one that saves the customer more wins. Discounts never stack — stacking is the source of most pricing incidents in retail systems.
Windowstarts_at and ends_at are mandatory and compared in UTC. A discount with no end date is not permitted; a permanent price cut is a price change.
Floor after discountThe discounted price is floored at 1 minor unit. A fixed discount larger than the price is clamped, never negative.
RoundingPercentage discounts round to the nearest whole shilling, half away from zero. Computed once, server-side, and returned formatted — the client never calculates a price.
Price at order timeThe price is resolved and frozen into order_items.unit_price_amount when the order is created. A discount expiring one minute later does not change an existing order.
CurrencyTZS with zero fraction digits, per the runtime currency setting. Formatting uses Intl.NumberFormat with that configuration, on the server.

12.4 Stock and reservation

The defect being fixed (R7) The reference platform displays stock on the product page and then never checks it again. Two customers can buy the last unit; nothing prevents it. For a store that holds one or two of many parts, this is a routine failure, not an edge case.
RuleSpecification
Two countersEach variant carries stock_quantity (physically on hand) and reserved_quantity (committed to unpaid orders). Available = stock − reserved. Availability is what the storefront shows and what every check uses.
ReservationCreated when an order is placed. Reserved stock is not deducted from stock_quantity — the goods are still on the shelf — but it is not sellable.
ExpiryReservations for unpaid orders expire after 48 hours. A scheduled job releases them, moves the order to expired and notifies the customer with a link to reorder. Cash-on-delivery orders are exempt, since payment happens at handover.
Deductionstock_quantity is decremented and the reservation released at the moment payment is confirmed — or, for cash on delivery, when the order is marked shipped. Deducting earlier makes the figure lie about what is on the shelf.
RestorationCancellation releases the reservation. A refund after fulfilment restores stock_quantity only when the goods physically return, which the administrator confirms explicitly.
ConcurrencyEvery stock mutation runs inside a transaction that takes SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the variant row, in ascending variant-id order across the whole order to prevent deadlock. The check and the write are never separated.
LedgerEvery change writes a stock_movements row in the same transaction, with reason, reference and actor. No exceptions — an adjustment with no ledger row is a bug.
Manual adjustmentRequires stock.adjust, a reason from the fixed list, and a note when the delta exceeds 10 units. Fully audited.
BackorderNot supported at launch. An out-of-stock variant cannot be added to a cart. "Notify me when available" captures the demand instead.
Low stockCrossing low_stock_threshold downward raises a dashboard alert and, once daily, a digest email to staff holding stock.read.

12.5 Cart rules

12.5.1 Composition

  • A cart line is (variant, quantity). Adding an existing variant increments rather than duplicating, enforced by a unique index.
  • Quantity is at least 1 and at most the available stock. Requesting more clamps to available and reports it.
  • A cart holds at most 100 distinct lines and 999 units per line — bounds that prevent abuse without troubling a real bulk buyer.

12.5.2 Persistence

A guest cart is server-side, keyed by a guest_token cookie, and mirrored to localStorage for instant first paint. A signed-in cart is server-side against the user. The server is always authoritative; local storage is a cache, not a source of truth. This is what lets a customer add on a phone and check out on a laptop — a journey the reference platform cannot support, since its cart never leaves the browser until checkout.

12.5.3 Revalidation

Every cart read, and the checkout submit, revalidates each line against live data. This is the fix for defects R6 and R7.

ConditionResponse
Product or variant soft-deletedLine removed; notice "This item is no longer available."
Variant deactivatedSame.
Price increasedLine updated to the new price; amber notice "Price changed from X to Y." Checkout requires an explicit re-confirmation.
Price decreasedLine updated silently, with a positive notice. Nobody objects to paying less, and no confirmation is required.
Available < quantityQuantity clamped to available; notice "Only n left — quantity reduced."
Available = 0Line marked unavailable and excluded from the total, but kept visible so the customer knows why the total changed.

12.5.4 Merge on sign-in

for each line in guestCart:
    if accountCart has the same variant:
        quantity = max(guest.quantity, account.quantity)   # never sum —
        # summing silently doubles an order the customer did not intend
    else:
        add the line
clamp every line to available stock
delete the guest cart, clear the guest cookie
report a summary: "3 items added to your cart"

The wishlist merges by union, with duplicates dropped.

12.6 Shipping

RuleSpecification
ModelFlat cost per method, as the reference platform does. Not weight- or distance-based. The columns for weight exist (§9.3.13) so a future change is additive.
AvailabilityA method may declare available_regions. Methods not available for the chosen region are hidden once a region is selected. Before a region is chosen, all are shown.
Address requirementrequires_shipping_address drives whether the address block appears. Office pickup does not require one.
Payment couplingaccepts_mobile_payment drives whether the payment block appears. A cash-on-delivery method sets it false and is_cash_on_delivery true.
Free thresholdWhen free_over_amount is set and the subtotal reaches it, the cost renders as "FREE" with the original struck through, and the order records zero shipping.
Cost freezingThe cost is copied into the order at placement. A later change to the method's price does not alter existing orders.
DefaultNo method is preselected. Preselecting the cheapest looks helpful and produces orders shipped by a method the customer never chose.

12.7 Order state machine

Three independent axes. Lifecycle answers "where is this order"; payment answers "have we been paid"; fulfilment answers "have we shipped". Collapsing them into one field, as the reference platform does, makes the common case — paid but not yet shipped — inexpressible.

12.7.1 Lifecycle

                    ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
                    │                                      ▼
  [placed] ──▶ AWAITING_PAYMENT ──▶ CONFIRMED ──▶ PROCESSING ──▶ SHIPPED
                    │  │                 │             │            │
                    │  │                 │             │            ▼
                    │  │                 │             │        DELIVERED
                    │  │                 │             │            │
                    │  ▼                 ▼             ▼            ▼
                    │ EXPIRED         CANCELLED    CANCELLED     COMPLETED
                    │ (48 h, no payment)                            │
                    ▼                                               ▼
                CANCELLED  ◀────── customer or staff            REFUNDED

  Cash on delivery skips AWAITING_PAYMENT and is placed as CONFIRMED.
StateMeaning and permitted transitions
AWAITING_PAYMENTPlaced, stock reserved, money not yet received. → CONFIRMED (payment verified) · CANCELLED (customer or staff) · EXPIRED (48-hour job).
CONFIRMEDPaid, or cash on delivery accepted. Stock deducted for paid orders. → PROCESSING · CANCELLED (staff only, with reason; triggers a refund when money was taken).
PROCESSINGBeing picked and packed. → SHIPPED · CANCELLED (staff only).
SHIPPEDHanded to the courier or ready for pickup. For cash on delivery, stock is deducted here. → DELIVERED. No cancellation.
DELIVEREDReceived by the customer. → COMPLETED automatically after 7 days · REFUNDED (returns window).
COMPLETEDTerminal. Unlocks review eligibility.
CANCELLEDTerminal. Reservations released; refund raised if money was taken.
EXPIREDTerminal. Reservations released. Distinguished from CANCELLED because it is a system action, and the analytics meaning is different.
REFUNDEDTerminal. Full or partial; stock restored only on confirmed physical return.
Enforcement Transitions are validated against an explicit table in the order service. Anything not listed returns 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION. Every accepted transition writes an order_events row with actor, timestamp, from and to. No code path anywhere may write orders.status directly — the state machine is the only door.

12.7.2 Payment and fulfilment axes

Payment: unpaid → pending_verification → paid, with failed, partially_refunded and refunded as branches. Fulfilment: unfulfilled → processing → shipped → delivered, with returned as a branch. Each axis moves independently; the lifecycle state is derived from the pair and never contradicts it.

12.7.3 Notifications per transition

TransitionCustomerStaff
Order placedEmail + SMS with the order number and payment instructionsRealtime toast and a bell notification in the admin console
Payment recordedEmail "We have received your payment details"Realtime alert flagging an order awaiting verification
Payment verifiedEmail + SMS "Payment confirmed"—
ShippedEmail + SMS with any tracking reference—
DeliveredEmail, plus a review invitation after 3 days—
CancelledEmail with the reasonNotification when the customer cancelled
Expiring soonSMS at 36 hours: "Your order expires in 12 hours"—
ExpiredEmail with a one-click reorder linkDaily digest

12.8 Payment reconciliation

With manual Lipa Namba, the money arrives at the till outside the system. Reconciliation is the process of tying an arrival to an order, and it is where a manual flow either works or quietly loses money.

Customer pays from 255712345678 to the till
      │
      ▼
Mobile money sends the merchant an SMS or email confirmation
      │
      ▼
Administrator opens Orders ▸ Awaiting verification
      │  the queue is sorted by age, oldest first
      ▼
Matching, in priority order
   1. exact match on payment_number ↔ payer's number       ← strongest
   2. exact match on amount + a ±2 h window
   3. manual search by customer name or phone
      │
      ▼
Verify payment
   ├─ enter the provider transaction reference (required, unique)
   ├─ enter the amount received  (defaults to the order total)
   ├─ system compares to the expected total
   │     equal      → mark paid
   │     less       → mark partially paid, order stays awaiting payment,
   │                   customer notified of the shortfall
   │     greater    → mark paid, flag an overpayment for refund
   └─ writes payments row + order_events row + notifications
      │
      ▼
Order → CONFIRMED, stock deducted, customer notified
ControlSpecification
Reference uniquenessUnique on (provider, provider_reference). The same mobile-money transaction can never be credited to two orders — the most likely and most costly manual error.
Separation of dutiespayment.verify is a distinct permission, not granted to the support role.
AuditEvery verification records who, when, from where, and the reference. Reversal is possible but never silent, and always leaves both records.
AgeingOrders awaiting verification for more than 2 hours are highlighted amber on the dashboard; more than 24 hours, red.
Automation pathAn aggregator (§13.3) replaces steps 1–3 with a signed webhook carrying the reference and amount. The verification record is written identically, with actor_type = 'webhook', so the audit trail and the reports do not change shape when automation arrives.

12.9 Delegated payment

The "friend payment" feature, redesigned around a token. Persona Grace — a teacher whose school pays — is the primary case, and it is common enough in this market to be a first-class flow rather than a workaround.

StepSpecification
1. NominateAt checkout the buyer expands "Someone else is paying" and supplies the payer's email. The order is created as AWAITING_PAYMENT with stock reserved as normal.
2. TokenA 32-byte token is generated, stored hashed on the order, valid 14 days, single-use. It is the only credential for the payment page — no order id appears anywhere, which is what closes the reference platform's exposure of using a raw order identifier in the URL.
3. NotifyThe payer receives an email naming the requester by first name and last initial, stating the amount and what it is for, with the link. The buyer receives a copy confirming it was sent.
4. PayThe payer opens /pay/:token, sees only the line items and totals, picks a payment method, enters their payment number and submits. No account, no sign-in.
5. ConfirmVerification is identical to §12.8. Both payer and buyer are notified on success.
6. ExpiryAn unpaid order expires at 48 hours like any other; the token is invalidated with it. The buyer may re-issue a new link from their order detail page, which resets both clocks.
PrivacyThe payer never sees the delivery address, phone number or email of the buyer, nor their order history. The buyer never sees the payer's phone number beyond the last four digits.

12.10 Invoicing

RuleSpecification
Two originsAn invoice may be generated from an order, or created standalone by an administrator. The standalone case is the quotation a school or company needs before it will release funds, and is the reason the module exists.
NumberingINV-{YYYY}-{000000}, gapless within a year, allocated under a row lock. Gaps in an invoice sequence raise questions in an audit.
ImmutabilityA draft invoice is editable. Once issued it is frozen; corrections are made by voiding and re-issuing, never by editing. Voided invoices keep their number.
Line itemsCatalogue lines carry a variant reference and snapshot fields; free-text lines are allowed for services and charges outside the catalogue.
Sharing"Share" mints a token backing /invoice/:token. Revocable at any time. The page is noindex and never appears in the sitemap.
PrintingA print stylesheet renders A4 with the TechStar letterhead, registration details, client block, line table, totals, payment instructions and standard terms. No browser headers or footers.
Standard termsThree lines carried on every invoice, as the reference platform does — goods shipped on confirmation of full payment; terms and conditions apply to handling, processing and shipping; payment only through TechStar's designated payment methods. Wording is administrator-editable in settings.
StockIssuing an invoice does not reserve or deduct stock. Only an order does. This must be explicit, because it is the assumption most often got wrong.

12.11 Returns and refunds

RuleSpecification
Window7 days from delivery for a fault; 48 hours for an incorrect item shipped. Both are configurable in settings and must be reflected in the published Terms.
EligibilityComponents sold in opened anti-static packaging are non-returnable unless faulty — a necessary rule in this trade and one that must be stated plainly at the point of sale, not only in the Terms.
ProcessThe customer contacts support; staff raise a return against the order; goods are received and inspected; a refund is recorded; stock is restored only when the item is confirmed resaleable.
Refund methodMobile money back to the paying number, which is why payments.payer_phone is captured. Cash refunds are recorded with the receiving staff member named.
Partial refundsSupported per line. The order moves to partially_refunded and both amounts remain visible.
AuditEvery refund records actor, amount, reason and reference, and appears on the order timeline for the customer to see.

Part ThirteenPayments in Tanzania

Payment is where an e-commerce specification written for one market fails in another. Card penetration is low, mobile money is near-universal, and the dominant flow is a customer pushing funds to a merchant till number from a USSD menu — an action the website cannot observe. Everything in this part follows from that fact.

13.1 Landscape

ChannelCharacteristics and implications for TechStar
Mobile money — customer-initiated push (Lipa Namba / merchant till)The customer dials a USSD string or uses their wallet app, selects the merchant-payment option, enters the till number and the amount, and confirms with a PIN. The merchant receives an SMS or email notification. No API is involved. This is what the reference platform implements, it works without any commercial integration, and it is what TechStar launches with. The cost is manual reconciliation (§12.8).
Mobile money — merchant-initiated pull (USSD push / STK)The merchant's system asks the network to prompt the customer's handset; the customer approves with a PIN and the merchant is notified over a webhook within seconds. This requires an aggregator or a direct operator integration, and it removes reconciliation entirely. It is the target state, specified as Phase 4.
Cash on deliveryWidely expected, especially for first-time buyers, and materially raises conversion. The cost is delivery risk and a cash-handling process. The reference platform charges a lower shipping fee for it, which is a sensible way to price the risk down.
Bank transferRelevant for institutional buyers — schools, universities, companies — who pay against an invoice. Handled by the invoice module plus manual reconciliation; no integration needed.
CardsLow volume domestically, useful for diaspora and corporate purchases. Available through the same aggregators. Out of scope for v1 (§3.5).

13.1.1 Networks to support

NetworkOperatorNotes
M-PesaVodacomLargest by subscriber base. Wallet app supports scanning a merchant QR code, which is why the payment panel carries one.
Mixx by YasYas (formerly Tigo)The reference platform still labels this "Tigo Pesa". Show both names during the transition — a customer whose phone menu says one thing and whose screen says another will hesitate.
Airtel MoneyAirtel
HaloPesaHalotelSmaller, but the "other networks" instruction block covers it, since all Tanzanian wallets interoperate.
T-PesaTTCLCovered by the same interoperability path.
Verify before you publish USSD strings, menu positions and till-number formats change, and they differ between consumer and merchant products. Every instruction block must be verified against the live network — by an actual test payment — before launch, and re-verified whenever an operator changes its menus. That is precisely why the instruction content is stored as editable settings (§14.16) rather than compiled into the front end: correcting a wrong menu step must take two minutes, not a release.

13.2 Manual Lipa Namba flow

Checkout                          Order created                Reconciliation
────────                          ─────────────                ──────────────
choose "Lipa Namba"       ──▶  status AWAITING_PAYMENT   ──▶  admin queue
enter paying number             stock reserved (48 h)          "Awaiting verification"
PLACE ORDER                     payments row: initiated              │
      │                         SMS + email with                     │
      │                         amount + reference                    │
      ▼                                 │                             ▼
confirmation page                       │                      match on payer number
 ├─ amount, copyable                    │                      or amount + time window
 ├─ order number as reference           │                             │
 ├─ per-network instructions            │                             ▼
 └─ live status strip  ◀────────────────┴──────────────────  verify → CONFIRMED
                          realtime push                       stock deducted
                                                              SMS + email sent
Design pointRationale
Capture the paying numberThe single strongest reconciliation key. Required, validated as 12 digits beginning 255. It is what turns matching from a search into a lookup.
Order number as referenceShown prominently and copyable. Some wallets allow a reference; where they do, it makes matching exact.
Exact amount"Pay exactly TZS 85,000." Round-number habits produce under- and over-payments, and each one costs a phone call.
Reservation window48 hours, communicated at placement and again by SMS at 36 hours. Long enough for someone who pays on payday; short enough that stock is not held indefinitely.
Live statusThe confirmation page subscribes to the order's realtime channel, so verification updates the page the customer is already looking at. It is a small thing that removes a large share of "have you received my payment?" contacts.
No payment proof uploadDeliberately omitted. Screenshots are trivially forged and create a queue of items requiring judgement. The merchant's own notification is the source of truth.

13.3 Aggregator integration

Automation is introduced behind one interface, so that the choice of provider is a configuration decision and never a rewrite. Nothing outside the adapter knows which provider is in use.

interface PaymentProvider {
  readonly key: string                       // 'selcom' | 'clickpesa' | …
  readonly capabilities: {
    mobileMoneyPush: boolean                 // prompt the customer's handset
    hostedCheckout: boolean                  // redirect to a provider page
    cards: boolean
    refunds: boolean
    statusQuery: boolean
  }

  createPayment(input: {
    orderNumber: string
    amount: Money
    customer: { name: string; phone: string; email?: string }
    method: 'mobile_money' | 'card'
    network?: 'mpesa' | 'mixx' | 'airtel' | 'halopesa'
    returnUrl: string
    webhookUrl: string
    idempotencyKey: string
  }): Promise<{
    providerReference: string
    status: 'pending' | 'succeeded' | 'failed'
    redirectUrl?: string                     // hosted checkout
    instructions?: string                    // e.g. "approve the prompt on your phone"
  }>

  verifyWebhook(headers, rawBody): { valid: boolean; event: ProviderEvent }
  queryStatus(providerReference): Promise<PaymentStatus>
  refund(providerReference, amount: Money, reason: string): Promise<RefundResult>
}
CandidateWhat to evaluate at selection time
SelcomEstablished Tanzanian aggregator with broad wallet coverage and a merchant network. Evaluate settlement period, per-transaction pricing and webhook reliability.
ClickPesaDeveloper-oriented, positioned at exactly this use case. Evaluate onboarding time and sandbox quality.
AzamPayLocal aggregator with multi-wallet collection. Evaluate documentation depth and support responsiveness.
DPO Group / PesapalPan-African, card plus mobile money, hosted checkout available. Evaluate pricing on low-value transactions — a TZS 200 resistor order must not cost more in fees than it earns.
Direct operatorVodacom and Airtel offer merchant APIs directly. Better unit economics at volume; heavier onboarding and one integration per network.
Selection criteria, in priority order (1) Coverage of all four major wallets through one integration. (2) Reliable, signed webhooks with documented retry semantics. (3) A sandbox that behaves like production. (4) Settlement within two business days. (5) Per-transaction cost that is viable on a TZS 5,000 basket. (6) Refund support through the API. Providers that fail (2) create more manual work than they remove.

13.4 Cash on delivery

RuleSpecification
Order statePlaced directly as CONFIRMED — payment is not pending in any meaningful sense. Payment status stays unpaid until the courier settles.
StockReserved at placement, deducted at SHIPPED, not at confirmation.
Reservation expiryDoes not apply. The order is confirmed, so there is nothing to expire.
Basket capConfigurable maximum order value for cash on delivery, default TZS 300,000. Above it the method is hidden with an explanation. Unpaid delivery risk scales with basket size and should be bounded.
Verification callStaff telephone the customer to confirm before dispatch. Recorded as an order event, so a repeat no-answer is visible.
SettlementThe courier records collection; staff mark the payment received with the collecting person named. This creates the payments row with method cash.
Refusal on deliveryOrder moves to CANCELLED with reason "refused on delivery"; stock is restored on return to the store. Repeat refusals from one phone number are surfaced to staff on that customer's record.

13.5 Webhooks and idempotency

An aggregator's webhook is the only moment where money and system state meet automatically. It must be impossible to double-credit, and impossible to lose an event.

POST /api/v1/webhooks/payments/:provider

 1  Read the RAW body — never the parsed one. Signature verification over
    re-serialised JSON fails intermittently and inexplicably.
 2  Verify the signature with the provider's secret, constant-time compare.
    Fail → log, return 401, do not process.
 3  Check the source IP against the provider's published range when one exists.
 4  INSERT the event into payment_events, keyed by external_event_id.
    Duplicate key → this is a retry. Return 200 immediately. Do nothing else.
 5  Return 200 NOW. Everything below runs on a queue.
    A provider that does not get a prompt 200 will retry, sometimes forever.
 6  Worker:
      ├─ resolve the order by merchant_reference
      ├─ compare the amount to the expected total
      ├─ update the payments row inside a transaction
      ├─ run the order state transition through the state machine
      ├─ deduct stock with a ledger row
      ├─ write order_events with actor_type = 'webhook'
      ├─ queue customer notifications
      └─ mark payment_events.processed_at
 7  Processing failure → record processing_error, alert staff, leave the event
    replayable from the admin console. The event is never lost.
GuaranteeMechanism
At-most-once creditingUnique index on payment_events.external_event_id, and on payments(provider, provider_reference). Two independent constraints, because this is the one thing that must not fail.
No lost eventsPersist before processing. Every webhook body is stored whether or not it can be handled.
Out-of-order deliveryEvents carry a provider timestamp; an event older than the payment's current state is recorded and ignored.
Unknown orderRecorded with processing_error = 'order_not_found' and raised to staff. Never silently dropped — an unmatched payment is money that arrived.
Amount mismatchNever auto-confirms. Flagged for manual review with both figures shown.
ReplayStaff with payment.verify can replay a stored event from the admin console after a bug fix, without asking the provider to resend.
Reconciliation sweepA scheduled job queries provider status for every payment stuck in pending beyond 30 minutes, catching webhooks that never arrived.

13.6 The reconciliation ledger

Three questions must be answerable at any moment, from the database alone, without a spreadsheet:

  1. What have we been paid? Sum of payments in status succeeded over a period, grouped by method and provider.
  2. What are we owed? Sum of orders.total_amount − paid_amount where the order is not cancelled, expired or refunded.
  3. What arrived that we cannot place? payment_events with a processing error, and payments whose amount does not match their order.
ReportDefinition
Daily settlementEvery payment verified in the day, with order number, method, provider reference, amount and verifier. Exportable to CSV; reconciled against the mobile-money statement.
OutstandingOrders awaiting payment, aged into 0–2 h, 2–24 h and over 24 h buckets. Drives the dashboard alert.
ExceptionsAmount mismatches, unmatched events, duplicate reference attempts, overpayments awaiting refund. Should normally be empty; a persistently non-empty exceptions list is the signal to move to an aggregator.
Refund registerAll refunds with reason, amount, destination number and authorising staff member.
Method mixShare of orders and of revenue by payment method over time — the evidence base for deciding whether cash on delivery is worth its risk and whether automation is worth its fee.
Financial invariant For every order: paid_amount = SUM(payments.amount WHERE status = 'succeeded') − SUM(refunds). A nightly job asserts this across all orders and alerts on any row that fails. An invariant that is checked is a control; one that is merely intended is a hope.

Part FourteenAdmin Console Specification

The brief asks for "full CRUD ability to manipulate anything in the platform". This part delivers that literally: every entity in the data model has a create, read, update and delete path in the console, plus the bulk operations, filters and safeguards that make those paths usable at scale. §14.19 states the universal contract that every module below conforms to, so the individual sections describe only what differs.

14.1 Shell and navigation

RegionSpecification
SidebarFixed, 240 px, on --ts-ink, collapsible to a 64 px icon rail with the state persisted per user. TechStar wordmark at the top. Items are grouped with small uppercase muted labels. The active item carries a 3 px primary-green left rule and a lighter background. Each item renders only if the signed-in staff member holds the governing permission — an empty group header is never shown.
GroupsOverview — Dashboard. Catalogue — Products, Categories, Subcategories, Stock, Discounts, Media. Sales — Orders, Invoices, Payments, Customers, Shipping. Content — Banners, Articles, Comments, Reviews, Services, Partners, FAQs. Marketing — Campaigns, Contacts, Searches. System — Currency, Settings, Staff & Roles, Audit Log. Then Sign out.
BadgesLive counts beside items needing attention: Orders (awaiting verification), Comments (pending), Reviews (pending), Stock (below threshold). Updated over the realtime channel.
Top bar64 px, white, bottom border. Left: breadcrumb, and the sidebar collapse toggle. Centre: global search — an omnibox over orders (by number or phone), products (by name or SKU), customers (by name, email or phone) and invoices, opened with Ctrl+K. Right: a "View store" link opening the storefront in a new tab; a notification bell with a dropdown of recent events; the account menu.
Content--ts-surface-alt background, 24 px padding, max width 1600 px. Every page opens with a PageHeader: title, optional description, and a right-aligned action cluster.
ResponsiveBelow lg the sidebar becomes an off-canvas behind a hamburger. Tables scroll horizontally inside their own container. The console is expected to be used on a tablet in the store; it is not designed for a phone, and says so if opened on one.
NotificationsBell dropdown lists the last 20 events: new order, payment recorded, low stock, new comment, new review, campaign completed, webhook failure. Each links to the relevant record. Unread count badge; marking all read is one action.

14.2 Dashboard

Purpose. Answer "what needs my attention right now" and "how are we doing" in one screen, without scrolling past the fold for the first of those.

14.2.1 Attention strip

The top row, and the reason the page is opened. Each tile is a filtered link; a tile with a zero count renders muted rather than disappearing, so its absence is never mistaken for a missing feature.

TileContents and threshold colouring
Awaiting payment verificationCount of orders with payment status pending_verification. Amber past 2 hours, red past 24. Links to the filtered order queue.
Orders to fulfilCONFIRMED and PROCESSING counts.
Low stockVariants at or below their threshold. Links to the stock report sorted ascending.
Pending moderationComments plus reviews awaiting a decision.
Failed webhooksUnprocessed payment_events. Red whenever above zero — this tile should always read zero.

14.2.2 Metric tiles

Four StatCards, each with a period selector shared across the row (Today · Last 7 Days · Last 30 Days · Last 12 Months) and each showing the value, the change against the immediately preceding period of equal length, and a sparkline.

  • Sales — sum of total_amount for orders paid in the period.
  • Orders — count of orders placed in the period.
  • Average order value — sales ÷ orders.
  • Customers — new registrations in the period, clickable to a breakdown modal by region.

14.2.3 Charts and lists

PanelSpecification
Sales analyticsBar chart of revenue by bucket — day for 7 and 30 days, month for 12 months. Primary green bars. Tooltip shows the amount and the order count. A "View as table" toggle exposes the same figures accessibly.
Orders analyticsLine chart of order count over the same buckets, on the same period selector.
Best sellingTop 10 products by units sold in the period, with thumbnail, name, units and revenue. A region filter answers "what sells upcountry versus in Dar", which is the reference platform's own best_selling?region= feature and a genuinely good one.
Recent ordersLast 10, with number, customer, total, status pill and time. Rows link to detail.
Top searchesTop 10 search terms in the period with their result counts. Terms returning zero are highlighted red — this list is the catalogue's own to-do list.
Empty stateBefore any orders exist, the charts are replaced by a short onboarding checklist: add a category, add a product, configure shipping, set the payment till number, place a test order.

14.3 Products

14.3.1 Product list

AspectSpecification
ColumnsSelection checkbox · 48 px thumbnail · Name (with SKU prefix in monospace beneath) · Subcategory · Price (range when multi-variant) · Stock (total, coloured by threshold) · Variants (count) · Status pill (Draft / Active / Archived) · Sold (lifetime units) · Updated · Actions.
SearchName, SKU, MPN and brand. Debounced, reflected in the URL.
FiltersCategory, subcategory, status, stock state (in stock / low / out), discounted, created-date range.
SortName, price, stock, units sold, created, updated.
Row actionsEdit · Duplicate · Set discount · View on storefront · Archive · Delete.
Bulk actionsDelete · Archive · Activate · Set discount · Move to subcategory · Export CSV. All bulk actions follow §14.19.4.
Header actions"New product" primary · "Import" · "Export".
Empty"No products yet" with "Add your first product" and a link to the import guide.

14.3.2 Product create and edit — the variant matrix

This is the most important screen in the console: catalogue growth is bounded by how fast a product can be entered, and the target is under three minutes (§3.6). The reference platform's attribute-matrix design is good and is kept, with its rough edges removed.

SectionFields and behaviour
1. ClassificationSubcategory — a searchable select grouped by category, required. Choosing it derives and displays the SKU prefix immediately, so the administrator sees the code the product will receive before saving.
2. DetailsProduct name (required, 3–255, trimmed and whitespace-collapsed on blur, with a live duplicate-name warning that informs but does not block) · Short description (plain text, 500, used for meta description with a live SEO preview) · Description (rich text, required) · Brand · Manufacturer part number · Tags (create-as-you-type).
3. PhotosDrag-and-drop uploader, up to 100 files, 3 MB each, JPEG/PNG/WebP/AVIF. Reorderable by drag; the first is the display image unless another is starred. Each accepts alt text. Upload begins on drop with per-file progress. Derivatives are generated asynchronously and the UI shows a "processing" state rather than a broken thumbnail.
4. Pricing & stock — simpleWhen no attributes are defined: Price (required, > 0) · Compare-at price (optional, must exceed price) · Cost (optional, staff-only) · Available stock (required, ≥ 0) · Low-stock threshold (default 5) · Barcode. One variant is created behind the scenes.
5. Pricing & stock — variantsIntroduced by "Add attributes", with the explanatory line the reference platform uses: "Add specifications to your product here. Skip this step if the product does not have any special specifications." The matrix then appears as a table whose columns are the attributes plus fixed Price and Stock. "+" adds an attribute column, prompting for its name and optional unit. "Add new values" adds a row. Each cell validates on blur. A row may be removed; "Delete table" clears the matrix back to the simple form after confirmation. Every row becomes one variant with an automatically assigned SKU shown read-only in a leading column.
6. VisibilityStatus — Draft / Active / Archived, default Draft, so a half-entered product is never live. Publish date (optional, schedules activation). Featured flag.
7. SEOURL slug — auto-generated, editable at creation. On edit it is read-only with a "Change URL" control that warns about link breakage and offers the 301 redirect described in §6.6. Meta title (70) and meta description (160) with counters and a search-result preview.
FooterSticky. "Cancel" · "Save as draft" · "Save and add another" · "Save" primary. Disabled until dirty and valid. Leaving with unsaved changes prompts.
ValidationMessage
Subcategory missing"Subcategory is required"
Name missing"Product name is required"
Description missing"Description is required"
No images"At least one image is required"
Price ≤ 0"Price must be greater than zero"
Compare-at ≤ price"Compare-at price must be higher than the price"
Stock negative"Stock cannot be negative"
Attribute value blank"Every variant needs a value for each attribute"
Duplicate attribute set"Two variants have the same combination of values"
Attribute name duplicated"Attribute names must be unique within a product"
Improvements over the reference implementation A draft status, so nothing goes live half-finished. A visible SKU before saving. A duplicate-name warning. Editable alt text. Variant SKUs shown in the matrix. A "Save and add another" path for bulk entry sessions. And the matrix keeps its state when validation fails — the reference platform's implementation loses unsaved rows on a failed submit, which is the single most expensive small defect in the whole console.

14.3.3 Stock management

A dedicated screen at /admin/stock, listing variants rather than products: thumbnail, product name, attribute values, SKU, on hand, reserved, available, threshold, last movement. Filterable to low or out of stock; sortable by available ascending, which is its default. Inline "Adjust" opens a dialog taking a signed delta or an absolute count, a reason from the fixed list (purchase, adjustment, damage, return, stocktake), and a note required for deltas above 10. Each variant has a "Movements" drawer showing its full ledger. Bulk adjustment by CSV upload — SKU, delta, reason — is provided for stocktakes, with a dry-run preview before commit.

14.3.4 Discounts

Reachable from a product row action and from a dedicated list. The modal takes Discount (%) or a fixed amount, Discount start date and Discount end date — the reference platform's three fields — plus scope (this variant / this product / this subcategory / this category) and an active toggle. A live preview shows the resulting price for the affected items and warns if the discount would take any price below one shilling. The list view shows all discounts with scope, value, window, affected item count and status, filterable to active, scheduled or expired.

14.4 Categories and subcategories

AspectSpecification
Category listColumns: selection · Name · SKU code · Subcategories (count) · Products (count) · Position (drag handle) · Status · Created · Actions. Rows are drag-reorderable, which writes position and immediately changes the storefront mega-menu order.
Create / editA modal, as the reference platform does — it is the right weight for four fields. Category name (required, unique) · SKU (2 digits) (required, digits only, unique; auto-suggested as the next free code) · Description (optional) · Image (optional) · Active.
Validation"Name is required" · "SKU is required" · "SKU should only contain digits" · "SKU must be exactly 2 digits" · "That SKU is already in use" · "A category with this name already exists".
Subcategory listColumns: selection · Name · Category · SKU code · Products (count) · Position · Status · Actions. Filterable by category.
Subcategory create / editCategory (searchable select, required) · Sub-category name · SKU (4 digits), auto-suggested as the parent's code plus the next free sequence, with the parent's two digits shown fixed and only the last two editable — which makes an invalid code impossible to type.
DeleteRefused while children exist, per §9.5. The dialog states the blocking count and offers the two supported paths: "Move products to another subcategory" or "Delete the products first". This is the fix for defect R12 — the reference platform instead offers to cascade-delete every product in the tree.
Move productsA dedicated action: choose a destination subcategory, review the count, confirm. SKUs are preserved (§12.1) and the move is audited.

14.5 Orders

14.5.1 Order queue

AspectSpecification
Status tabsAll · Awaiting payment · Awaiting verification · Confirmed · Processing · Shipped · Delivered · Completed · Cancelled · Expired — each with a live count. The reference platform offers only All / Completed / Pending / Cancelled, which cannot express the working day.
ColumnsOrder number (monospace) · Date · Customer name · Phone · Items (count) · Total · Payment status pill · Fulfilment status pill · Shipping method · Region · Actions.
SearchOrder number, customer name, phone, email, payment number, or a product SKU within the order.
FiltersDate range, payment method, shipping method, region, amount range, has-delegated-payer.
Default sortPlaced date descending — except on the "Awaiting verification" tab, where it is ascending, oldest first, because that is a work queue and the oldest item is the most urgent.
Bulk actionsUpdate fulfilment status · Print packing slips · Export CSV. Deliberately no bulk cancel and no bulk delete — an order is a financial record.
RealtimeA new order inserts at the top with a brief highlight and an audible chime, subject to a per-user mute. This is what the reference platform's Socket.IO connection exists for.

14.5.2 Order detail

Two columns. Left (8 of 12): status bar, items, payment panel, fulfilment panel, timeline. Right (4 of 12): customer card, shipping card, totals card, internal notes.

PanelSpecification
Status barOrder number, placed date, and the three status pills. A "Change status" control offering only the transitions legal from the current state (§12.7) — an illegal transition is not merely rejected, it is not offered. Each change requires a confirmation and accepts an optional note.
ItemsThumbnail, name (linking to the product), attributes, SKU, unit price, quantity, line total. Items whose product has since been deleted render from their snapshot with a muted "no longer in catalogue" marker.
PaymentMethod, the number the customer said they would pay from, expected total, amount received. Recorded payments listed with status, provider reference, amount, verifier and timestamp.
