A "Verify payment" action opens the dialog from §12.8; a "Record manual payment" action covers cash. "Issue refund" is present when the order is paid, behind the payment.refund permission.
FulfilmentShipping method and cost, delivery address with a copy-all control, a tracking reference field, "Print packing slip", "Print invoice", and "Mark shipped" / "Mark delivered".
TimelineThe full order_events stream, newest first: actor, action, before and after, timestamp, and any note. Notifications sent appear here too, so "did we tell the customer?" is answerable.
CustomerName, email, phone, and whether the account is registered or guest. For registered customers: total lifetime orders and value, and a link to their record. A "Contact" control offers WhatsApp, call and email with the order number prefilled.
Internal notesThreaded, timestamped, attributed. Staff only, never exposed on any customer-facing endpoint.
ActionsResend confirmation · Regenerate payment link · Cancel order (with reason) · Duplicate as a new order · Create invoice from this order.

14.6 Invoices

ScreenSpecification
ListColumns: Invoice number · Order (linked, when derived from one) · Client name · Total · Status pill (Draft / Issued / Paid / Void) · Issued date · Due date · Actions. Search by number, client name or email. Filter by status and date range.
BuilderClient details — name (required), email (required, valid), phone, address. Line items — a product search field with the reference platform's own placeholder, "Please search here …", adding catalogue lines with SKU, price and an editable quantity; plus "Add custom line" for free-text charges. Shipping method (optional). Notes. A live totals panel on the right.
Validation"Client name is required" · "Email is required" · "Enter a valid email" · "Add at least one line item" · "Quantity must be at least 1".
DetailRendered as the printable document, with an action bar above: Print · Download PDF · Email to client · Share link (mint, copy, revoke) · Mark paid · Void · Delete (drafts only).
PrintA4, TechStar letterhead, registration details, client block, line table, totals, payment instructions and the three standard terms (§12.10).

14.7 Customers

Entirely new — the reference platform has a "Customers" sidebar entry and no screen behind it (defect R13). Without this, staff cannot answer a customer's question about their own account.

ScreenSpecification
ListColumns: Avatar · Name · Email (with a verified tick) · Phone · Orders (count) · Lifetime value · Last order · Registered · Status pill. Search by name, email or phone. Filters: verified, has ordered, region, registration date, marketing consent, status.
Detail — profileAll fields, with edit for name, email, phone and marketing consent. Shows verification state with a "Resend verification" action, and account status with suspend and reactivate.
Detail — ordersFull order history with totals, linking through to each order.
Detail — addressesSaved addresses, read-only.
Detail — activityReviews and comments written, wishlist contents, last sign-in, active sessions with a "Revoke all sessions" action.
Detail — actionsSend password reset · Suspend (with reason; suspended users can sign in but cannot order, and are told why) · Export personal data (§11.8.2) · Anonymise account.
GuardrailsStaff cannot read or set a password. Every view of a customer record is written to the audit log — access to personal data is itself an auditable event.

14.8 Shipping methods

AspectSpecification
ListColumns: drag handle · Name · Cost · Description (truncated) · Requires address · Accepts mobile payment · Cash on delivery · Free over · Regions · Active · Actions. Drag-reordering sets the order shown at checkout.
Create / edit modalShipping method (name, required) · Shipping cost (required, ≥ 0, may be zero for pickup) · Description (required — it is shown to the customer at checkout and is how they choose) · Estimated delivery days min and max · Free over (optional) · Available regions (multi-select; empty means everywhere) · three checkboxes with the reference platform's own explanatory copy: "Check the box if this shipping method requires the client to provide shipping details.", "Check the box if this shipping method can receive mobile payments.", and "This method is cash on delivery." · Active.
Validation"Shipping method is required" · "Shipping cost is required" · "Please enter a valid shipping cost" · "Description is required" · "A method with this name already exists".
DeleteSoft delete. A method referenced by existing orders is never hard-deleted; deactivating hides it from checkout while past orders keep their snapshot name and cost. Deleting the last active method is refused — checkout would have nothing to offer.

14.9 Banners

AspectSpecification
ListColumns: drag handle · Thumbnail · Title · Placement · Schedule (start–end, or "Always") · Status · Created · Actions. Reordering sets carousel order.
BuilderA two-column screen: form on the left, a live preview on the right rendering the banner exactly as the storefront will, updating on every keystroke. Fields: Title and Title colour · Subtitle and Subtitle colour · Background colour · Image (required) · Link — "Where does the banner redirect to" · Button text, Button background, Button text colour · Placement · Start and End date-times (both optional) · Active.
Colour fieldsEach is a ColorField — swatch, picker and hex input in sync. A contrast checker beside the title and subtitle fields warns when the chosen text colour fails 4.5:1 against the background, showing the computed ratio. Advisory, not blocking; the reference platform has no such check and its banners rely on the author's eye.
Image guidanceRecommended 1200 × 800, transparent PNG or WebP for product cut-outs. The uploader warns when a file's aspect ratio would crop badly at the banner's dimensions.
Link validationMust be a valid absolute or site-relative URL. Internal links are offered as a picker over products, categories and pages, so a typo cannot produce a dead banner.
Preview modal"Preview" opens the banner full width at desktop and mobile widths side by side, matching the reference platform's preview modal.
Image replacementEditing text and replacing the image are separate operations, as on the reference platform — replacing an image is a re-upload, and coupling it to a text save makes both slower.

14.10 Services · 14.11 Partners

ModuleSpecification
ServicesCard grid list — image, heading, excerpt, edit and delete. Composer: Heading (required, maximum 15 words, with the reference platform's own rule "Heading should not exceed 15 words") · Excerpt · Content (rich text, required) · Image (required, "Drag & drop your image") · Position · Active. Validation: "Service heading is required" · "Heading should not exceed 15 words" · "Service content is required" · "Image is required".
PartnersCard grid — logo, name, website. Composer: Partner name (required) · Partner's website link (required, valid URL) · Logo (required; square recommended, transparent background preferred) · Position · Active. Validation: "Partner name is required" · "Link to partner's website is required" · "Image is required".

14.12 Blog, comments and reviews

ModuleSpecification
ArticlesList as cards or a table, filterable by status and author. Composer: Heading (required, ≤ 15 words) · Description — the excerpt, required · Content (rich text, required, placeholder "Compose an epic article…") · Cover image (required) · Tags · Status · Publish date · SEO title and description with counters. Actions: Save draft · Preview (opens the storefront article route in a draft-token mode) · Publish · Unpublish.
CommentsModeration queue with tabs Pending · Approved · Rejected · Spam. Columns: Author · Article · Excerpt · Submitted · Status. A detail drawer shows the full body with the article for context and Approve / Reject / Mark spam / Delete. Bulk moderation supported. Approving fires no notification to the commenter; rejecting is silent by design.
ReviewsSame queue shape. Columns: Product · Rating · Author · Excerpt · Verified-purchase badge · Submitted · Status. Approving recalculates the product's review_count and average_rating in the same transaction. Reviews are never edited by staff — only approved, rejected or deleted, with the reason recorded.
FAQsSimple list with drag ordering and a grouping field. Composer: Question · Answer (rich text) · Group · Position · Active.

14.13 Campaigns and contacts

14.13.1 Campaign list

Four counters across the top — Total campaigns · Total contacts · SMS sent · Emails sent — then a table: Name · Channel · Audience · Scheduled · Status · Recipients · Sent · Delivered · Failed · Cost · Created by · Actions. Header actions "New SMS campaign" and "New email campaign". A detail drawer shows the full message, audience definition, schedule and the per-recipient delivery report.

14.13.2 SMS composer

FieldBehaviour
Campaign nameRequired. Internal label only.
Send to"All contacts" or "Specific contacts". Choosing specific reveals a multi-select over the contact list with search.
Additional contactsFree entry of numbers, space or comma separated, with the reference platform's own hint: "Press the space key to add additional numbers, e.g. 255729XXXXXX, 255729YYYYYY". Each is validated as 12 digits beginning 255; invalid entries are rejected inline with "Invalid phone number. Please enter a 12-digit number."
MessageRequired. A live counter in the reference platform's format — "Chars. Remaining (0) 0/160" — that also reports the number of SMS segments and switches to the 70-character limit when a non-GSM character is typed, since one accented character silently triples the cost of a campaign.
When to send"Immediately" or "Later" with a date-time picker. Validation: "Date and time are required for sending the campaign later"; the time must be in the future.
EstimateBefore sending, a panel states recipient count, segments per message, total segments and estimated cost. Sending requires confirming that figure. The reference platform sends without any cost estimate, which on a metered channel is a real risk.
Test send"Send test" delivers to one number without recording a campaign send.
ConsentRecipients without sms_opt_in, or with an unsubscribe timestamp, are excluded automatically and the exclusion count is shown. This is not optional.

14.13.3 Email composer

The same shape with Subject in place of campaign name, a rich-text body with a variable palette ({{name}}, {{firstName}}), a desktop and mobile preview, and a test send. Additional recipients accept addresses with the hint "Press the space key to add additional emails, e.g. person1@example.com, person2@example.com". Every email carries a one-click unsubscribe link and a physical postal address, both required for deliverability as much as for compliance.

14.13.4 Contacts

Table: Name · Email · Phone · Source · Email consent · SMS consent · Tags · Orders · Joined. Search across name, email and phone. Filters by source, consent and tag. Actions: import from CSV with a column-mapping step and a duplicate-handling choice; export; edit tags in bulk; unsubscribe. Customers are synchronised into contacts on registration and on order placement, carrying their consent flags with them.

14.14 Search analytics

Two tables. Top searches: Search query · Number of searches · Average result count · Click-through rate · Last searched. Zero-result searches, shown first and highlighted, since it is the actionable one: term, count, last searched, and a "Create product" shortcut that opens the product form with the term prefilled as the name. Both are filterable by date range and exportable. This turns the reference platform's read-only search table into a merchandising instrument.

14.15 Currency

A small screen matching the reference platform's: two cards showing the current Display currency and Fraction digits, and an "Update currency format" modal taking Currency type (ISO 4217 select) and Maximum fraction digits (0–4). A live preview shows a sample amount formatted with the pending settings before saving. Changing the display currency does not convert stored prices — a warning states this plainly, because the opposite assumption is the obvious one and would be catastrophic.

14.16 Settings

Grouped into tabs. Every field writes to site_settings and is audited.

TabFields
StoreStore name · Legal entity name · Tagline · Logo · Favicon · Registration country · Registration agency · TIN · Certificate of incorporation number · Registered address · P.O. box.
ContactPrimary and secondary phone · Email · WhatsApp number and default message · Physical address · Map latitude and longitude · Opening hours.
SocialFacebook · Instagram · X · LinkedIn · YouTube · TikTok. Empty entries are hidden from the footer rather than rendered as dead icons.
PaymentsM-Pesa till number · Mixx by Yas till number · Airtel Money till number · Other-network instructions · Merchant QR image · Per-network instruction blocks in Swahili and English, each a rich-text field · Cash-on-delivery maximum order value · Payment reservation window in hours.
Page imagesHero images for About, Privacy Policy and Terms & Conditions, with a preview each — the reference platform's own settings screen, kept. Validation: "About Us page image is required" and equivalents.
EmailFrom name · From address · Reply-to · SMTP host, port, user, password, TLS mode · a "Send test email" action that reports the exact failure when it fails.
SMSProvider · API credentials · Sender ID · a "Send test SMS" action · a low-balance warning threshold.
CatalogueProducts per page · Default sort · "New" badge window in days · Low-stock default threshold · Whether to hide empty subcategories in navigation (default on, §6.4.2).
PoliciesReturns window in days · Warranty statement · The three standard invoice terms · Cancellation policy text.
FeaturesToggles: reviews · comments · wishlist · guest checkout · delegated payment · back-in-stock notifications · maintenance mode with a message.

14.17 Staff and roles

ScreenSpecification
Staff listName · Email · Roles · Last sign-in · Status · Created. Header action "Invite staff member".
InviteEmail plus role selection. Sends a single-use invitation valid 7 days; the recipient sets their own password. Staff accounts are never created with a password chosen by someone else.
DetailProfile, roles (add and remove), effective permission list computed from the union of roles, active sessions with revoke, and that member's audit history.
RolesList of roles with member counts. A role editor showing the full permission catalogue as a grouped checkbox matrix. System roles are visible but locked.
GuardrailsNobody may edit their own roles. The last owner cannot be removed, demoted or suspended. Every change is audited with before and after permission sets. Granting staff.manage requires the owner role.

14.18 Audit log

Read-only. Columns: Timestamp · Actor · Action · Entity type · Entity · IP address · Request id. Filterable by actor, action, entity type, entity and date range; cursor-paginated because it grows without bound. Expanding a row shows a field-level diff of before and after. Exportable to CSV for a compliance request. Every entity's own detail screen carries an AuditTrail panel showing just its own history, which is where it is actually consulted.

What is logged: every create, update and delete on every entity; order status transitions; payment verifications and refunds; stock adjustments; permission and role changes; settings changes; customer record views; data exports; sign-in, sign-out and failed sign-in for staff accounts; and campaign sends. What is not logged: read access to non-personal data, which would swamp the useful signal.

14.19 The universal CRUD contract

Every module above conforms to this. It is what makes "full CRUD on anything" a coherent promise rather than fifteen slightly different screens.

14.19.1 Create

  • Reached from a primary "New {entity}" button in the page header, or from a "+" in an empty state.
  • Modal for entities with 6 or fewer fields; a full page for anything larger.
  • Required fields marked with an asterisk and an explanatory legend. Optional fields are labelled "(optional)" rather than left ambiguous.
  • Validation on blur, then live once a field has errored, then in full on submit. Submit is disabled until dirty and valid.
  • On success: a toast "{Entity} created", and navigation either to the new record or back to the list with the new row highlighted for three seconds.
  • "Save and add another" wherever bulk entry is realistic — products, categories, subcategories, FAQs.

14.19.2 Read

  • Every list is a DataTable (§8.4.1) with search, filters, sort, pagination and density, all reflected in the URL so a filtered view is shareable with a colleague.
  • Every list has a specified empty state naming the primary action.
  • Every detail screen carries created and updated timestamps, the creating and last-updating actor, and an audit panel.
  • Every list offers CSV export of the current filtered set, capped at 50,000 rows and delivered as a queued job with an email link when large.

14.19.3 Update

  • Same form as create, prefilled, with the entity name in the heading.
  • Only changed fields are sent; the payload is a partial.
  • Optimistic concurrency: the form carries the record's updatedAt; a mismatch returns 409 and the UI offers "Reload and reapply my changes" with a field-level diff of what someone else altered.
  • Navigating away with unsaved changes prompts.
  • Inline editing is offered only for single scalar fields where it is genuinely faster — position, active flag, stock.

14.19.4 Delete

RuleSpecification
Soft by defaultSets deleted_at. Recoverable from the entity's Trash view for 90 days.
ConfirmationAlways. The dialog names the specific record — "Delete Arduino Uno R3?" — never "Delete this item?".
Consequences statedThe dialog lists what else is affected and the counts: "This product has 3 variants and appears in 2 open carts."
Blocked deletesWhere §9.5 says RESTRICT, the dialog explains why and offers the supported alternative rather than a bare refusal.
Bulk deletesRequire typing the exact count to confirm — "Type 14 to confirm" — using ConfirmDialog's requireTyping mode. Slow enough to interrupt an automatic click, which is the point.
UndoThe success toast offers "Undo" for 8 seconds on single soft deletes.
Hard deleteAvailable only from the Trash view, only to owner, and never for orders, payments, invoices or audit records.
AuditDeletion writes the full prior state to audit_logs.before, so a hard-deleted record can still be described even when it can no longer be restored.

14.19.5 Cross-cutting behaviours

BehaviourApplies to
Permission gatingEvery action control is wrapped in PermissionGate. A missing permission hides the control entirely; a conditionally unavailable action is disabled with a tooltip explaining why. Server-side enforcement is independent and mandatory.
Optimistic UIToggles and reordering apply immediately and revert with an error toast on failure.
KeyboardCtrl+K global search · / focuses the list search · N new record · Esc closes any overlay · Ctrl+S saves a form.
Deep linksEvery filtered list state, every drawer and every modal is addressable by URL.
LoadingSkeletons at final dimensions, never a full-page spinner over content that is already on screen.
ErrorsField errors inline; request errors as a dismissible banner above the form carrying the request id; nothing is ever lost from the form on failure.
TimestampsRelative for anything under 7 days ("2 hours ago") with the absolute value on hover, absolute beyond that. Always in Africa/Dar_es_Salaam.

14.19.6 Coverage matrix

The literal answer to "full CRUD on anything in the platform". C create, R read, U update, D delete, B bulk operations, X export.

EntityCRUDBXNotes
Products✓✓✓✓✓✓Import too

Where a cell reads "—", it is a deliberate refusal with a stated reason, not a missing feature. Financial and audit records are never editable or deletable, because a system in which they are is not a system anyone can rely on.

Part FifteenMedia Pipeline

15.1 Upload flow

Browser                          API                          Worker
───────                          ───                          ──────
select / drop files
  ├─ client-side pre-check
  │   type, size, dimensions      (fail fast, no round trip)
  └─ POST /admin/media  ──────▶  multer memory storage
                                  ├─ magic-byte type sniff
                                  ├─ size + dimension caps
                                  ├─ sharp: decode → re-encode
                                  │   (strips EXIF and any payload)
                                  ├─ SHA-256 → dedupe check
                                  ├─ write original as WebP q82
                                  ├─ INSERT media_files
                                  └─ enqueue derivatives ─────▶ generate sm/md/lg
  ◀── 201 { id, lg, status:"processing" }                       update derivatives JSON
                                                                emit socket event
  ◀────────────────────── "ready" ───────────────────────────────┘

The response returns before derivatives exist so the interface is never blocked by image processing. The uploader shows a "processing" shimmer on the thumbnail until the ready event arrives, or until a poll confirms it.

15.2 Derivative sizes

KeyWidthFitUsed by
sm200 pxcontain, white padCart and wishlist rows, gallery thumbnails, admin table rows
md600 pxcontain, white padProduct cards in grids and carousels
lg1400 px maxinside, no upscaleProduct detail hero, lightbox, banners

All derivatives are WebP at quality 82, with a JPEG fallback generated only if analytics ever show meaningful traffic from browsers without WebP support — as of now, none does. Names are content-addressed: {publicId}-{key}-{checksum8}.webp, so a re-upload can never be served from a stale cache. Cache headers are public, max-age=31536000, immutable.

Two corrections to the reference platform's pipeline It applies a face-detection crop (g_face) to photographs of circuit boards, which is meaningless and occasionally crops the subject out. TechStar uses contain with white padding, so a component photographed on a white background is never cut. And its three sizes carry inconsistent version segments, so they can drift apart after a re-upload; content-addressed names make that impossible.

15.3 Storage adapters

interface StorageAdapter {
  put(key: string, body: Buffer, contentType: string): Promise<void>
  get(key: string): Promise<Buffer>
  delete(key: string): Promise<void>
  exists(key: string): Promise<boolean>
  publicUrl(key: string): string
}
AdapterBehaviour
localWrites under MEDIA_ROOT, outside the web root, sharded two levels by the ULID's first four characters so no directory grows unbounded. Served by Nginx with execution disabled. The default.
s3Any S3-compatible endpoint. Objects private; served through CloudFront or an equivalent, or by signed URL.
cloudinaryFor parity with the reference platform's hosting, should it ever be wanted. Derivatives are still generated locally so the URL contract does not change.

15.4 Cleanup and orphans

  • media_files.reference_count is incremented and decremented in the same transaction as the referencing row.
  • A nightly job soft-deletes files with zero references older than 24 hours — the delay covers an upload whose form was never submitted.
  • A weekly job hard-deletes soft-deleted files older than 30 days and removes their bytes.
  • A monthly reconciliation compares storage keys against database rows in both directions and reports discrepancies rather than acting on them.
  • Deleting a referenced file is refused by the API before the foreign key has to intervene, with a message naming what references it.

Part SixteenSearch, Filtering, Sorting, Pagination

16.1 Engine choice

MySQL full-text at launch; a dedicated engine only when measurement demands it At 617 products — and at ten times that — MySQL's InnoDB full-text index with the tuning in §9.4 returns results in single-digit milliseconds. Adding Elasticsearch or Meilisearch on day one buys typo tolerance and synonyms at the cost of a second datastore to run, secure, back up and keep in sync. The trigger for revisiting is explicit: the catalogue passes 20,000 products, or the zero-result rate exceeds 15% and is traceable to matching rather than to genuine catalogue gaps. The search service is written behind an interface so the swap is contained.

16.2 Query grammar and ranking

A search runs four passes and returns them in this order, deduplicated. Ordering by relevance means ordering by which pass matched.

PassMatchRationale
1Exact SKU, or exact manufacturer part numberSomeone typing 74HC14 or 0510003 wants that part and nothing else. This pass short-circuits: a hit here returns immediately.
2Name prefix (name LIKE 'term%')Prefix matches are near-certain intent.
3Name containsCatches mid-string matches like "uno" in "Arduino Uno R3".
4MATCH … AGAINST in boolean mode over name, description, brand and MPNFull-text relevance, with name weighted 4×, brand and MPN 2×, description 1×.
RuleSpecification
NormalisationLowercase, trim, collapse whitespace, strip punctuation except -, . and /, which carry meaning in part numbers.
Multi-wordAll terms required (boolean +), then relevance-ordered. "arduino uno" must not return every Arduino.
Short tokensinnodb_ft_min_token_size = 2 with an empty stopword table, so "IC", "DC" and "5V" are searchable.
SuggestionsSpelling suggestions come from a term dictionary built nightly from product names, matched by trigram similarity within an edit distance of 2, and only offered when the original returned nothing.
SynonymsA small hand-maintained table for this trade: resistor ↔ resistance, capacitor ↔ cap, LED ↔ light emitting diode, pot ↔ potentiometer, uC ↔ microcontroller, PSU ↔ power supply. Editable in the admin console.
FiltersApplied as SQL predicates after matching, never in the application layer, so pagination totals stay correct.
HighlightingPerformed client-side on the returned names, so the server does not have to escape and return HTML.

16.3 Facets

Facet counts are computed in one additional aggregate query against the same filtered set, so a count never contradicts the grid beneath it. Price bands are derived from the actual distribution in scope — quartiles rounded to readable boundaries — rather than fixed bands that would be empty for most subcategories.

16.4 Search analytics

SignalCaptured how, used for what
Term and result countWritten once per distinct term per session, on submit — not per keystroke, which is what makes the reference platform's search table too noisy to act on.
Zero resultsSurfaced first in the admin console with a "Create product" shortcut. This is the highest-value report in the system: it is customers telling you exactly what to stock.
Click-throughclicked_product_id written when a result is opened. Terms with many searches and no clicks indicate matching that finds the wrong things.
TrendsWeek-over-week movement per term, to catch seasonal and course-driven demand.

16.5 Pagination contracts

ContextStrategy
Catalogue, search, admin listsOffset pagination with a total count. Page size default 24, maximum 100. Predictable page numbers matter more than raw speed at this scale. Fixes defect R17.
Audit log, order events, search logsCursor pagination on (created_at, id). These grow without bound and a deep offset scan on them is a slow query waiting to happen.
ExportsStreamed in 1,000-row chunks; never materialised in memory.
CarouselsFixed 24-item fetch, no pagination.

Part SeventeenMessaging: Email, SMS, Realtime, Campaigns

17.1 Transactional email

TriggerContents
Welcome / verify emailVerification link, 24-hour validity stated.
Order placedOrder number, items, totals, delivery method and address, payment instructions for the chosen method.
Payment received (recorded)Acknowledgement that details were received and verification is pending.
Payment confirmedReceipt with the transaction reference.
Order shippedTracking reference where available, estimated delivery window.
Order deliveredThank-you, plus a review invitation sent separately 3 days later.
Order cancelled / expiredReason, and a one-click reorder link.
Delegated payment requestTo the nominated payer: who asked, what for, how much, the payment link.
Password reset · Password changedReset link; and a security notice to the address on file after any change.
Back in stockTo everyone on notify_requests for that variant.
Invoice sentPDF attached plus the share link.
Staff invitationSingle-use link, 7-day validity.
Low stock digestDaily to staff holding stock.read.

Templates are React Email components rendering to both HTML and plain text, sharing one layout: TechStar header, content, footer with contact details and, for marketing mail only, an unsubscribe link. Transactional mail carries no unsubscribe, because a receipt is not marketing. SPF, DKIM and DMARC must be configured before launch; without them, delivery to Gmail addresses is unreliable regardless of code quality.

17.2 SMS

SMS matters more than email in this market — a Tanzanian customer will see an SMS within minutes and an email possibly never. Sent for: order placed, payment confirmed, order shipped, order ready for pickup, and the 36-hour expiry warning. Kept under 160 GSM-7 characters so each is one segment. The adapter interface:

interface SmsProvider {
  send(to: string, message: string, opts?: { senderId?: string })
    : Promise<{ messageId: string; segments: number; cost?: Money }>
  sendBulk(messages: { to: string; message: string }[])
    : Promise<BulkResult>
  deliveryStatus(messageId: string): Promise<DeliveryStatus>
  balance(): Promise<Money>
}

Implementations for Beem Africa and NextSMS, plus a console stub for development. Numbers are canonicalised to 255XXXXXXXXX before sending. A sender ID must be registered with the operators in advance — allow two to three weeks for this in the launch plan, because it cannot be hurried and blocks go-live.

17.3 Realtime events

ChannelEvents
admin:ordersorder.placed, order.payment_recorded, order.cancelled — drives the queue insertion and the chime.
admin:alertsstock.low, webhook.failed, comment.pending, review.pending — drives the bell badge.
order:{orderNumber}status.changed, payment.confirmed — drives the live strip on the confirmation page.
user:{publicId}cart.updated for multi-tab and multi-device consistency.

Every connection is authenticated with the access token at handshake and re-authenticated on refresh; channel subscription is authorised per channel. This closes defect R9's second half. Redis is used as the Socket.IO adapter so events reach every application instance.

17.4 Campaign engine

compose ─▶ save draft ─▶ resolve audience ─▶ estimate ─▶ confirm
                                │                          │
                                │  excludes: no consent,    │
                                │  unsubscribed, invalid,   │
                                │  duplicates               │
                                ▼                          ▼
                        campaign_recipients rows      enqueue BullMQ job
                        (status: queued)                    │
                                                            ▼
                                            rate-limited worker
                                            ├─ 20 SMS/s, 10 email/s
                                            ├─ per-recipient retry ×3
                                            │  with exponential backoff
                                            ├─ update recipient status
                                            └─ update campaign counters
                                                            │
                                                            ▼
                                            delivery receipts (webhook)
                                            update delivered / failed

A scheduled campaign is a delayed job; cancelling before it fires removes it. A campaign in sending can be paused, which stops the worker taking new recipients without losing those already sent. Every send is audited with actor, audience size and cost.

17.5 Queues

QueueJobs · concurrency · retry
emailTransactional sends · 5 · 3 attempts, exponential backoff
smsTransactional sends · 5 · 3 attempts
campaignBulk fan-out · 2 · per-recipient retry
mediaDerivative generation · 3 · 2 attempts
webhookPayment event processing · 5 · 5 attempts
scheduledReservation expiry (every 5 min) · stock digest (daily 07:00) · media cleanup (nightly) · session purge (nightly) · search dictionary rebuild (nightly) · payment status sweep (every 30 min) · financial invariant check (nightly)

Every queue has a dead-letter queue and an admin view listing failed jobs with their error and a retry action. A failed job that nobody can see is a silent outage.

Part EighteenAnalytics and Reporting

18.1 Metric definitions

Ambiguity in a metric definition is worse than not having the metric. These are normative.

MetricDefinition
RevenueSum of total_amount for orders whose paid_at falls in the period. Recognised on payment, not on placement — an unpaid order is not revenue.
OrdersCount of orders whose placed_at falls in the period, excluding EXPIRED. Cancelled orders are included, because cancellation rate is itself a signal.
Average order valueRevenue ÷ paid order count.
ConversionOrders placed ÷ distinct sessions that added at least one item to a cart.
Units soldSum of order_items.quantity for paid orders.
New customersUsers whose created_at falls in the period.
Repeat rateCustomers with two or more paid orders ÷ customers with at least one, over a trailing 12 months.
Search successSearches with at least one result and a click ÷ all searches.
Payment lagMedian minutes from placed_at to paid_at for Lipa Namba orders.
Fulfilment lead timeMedian hours from paid_at to shipped_at.

18.2 Aggregation approach

Dashboard queries run against a small set of hand-written SQL statements using window functions and CTEs — this is where the ORM is set aside deliberately. Two things keep them fast: a date-spine CTE so that periods with no orders still produce a zero row (otherwise charts silently skip days), and the denormalised paid_at, shipped_at and delivered_at columns on orders, which avoid joining the event log for every chart.

-- Revenue and order count by month, last 12 months, with zero-filled gaps
WITH RECURSIVE spine AS (
  SELECT DATE_FORMAT(DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL 11 MONTH), '%Y-%m-01') AS d
  UNION ALL
  SELECT DATE_ADD(d, INTERVAL 1 MONTH) FROM spine
  WHERE d < DATE_FORMAT(CURDATE(), '%Y-%m-01')
)
SELECT  DATE_FORMAT(s.d, '%Y-%m')                AS bucket,
        COALESCE(SUM(o.total_amount), 0)         AS revenue,
        COUNT(o.id)                              AS orders
FROM    spine s
LEFT JOIN orders o
       ON o.paid_at >= s.d
      AND o.paid_at <  DATE_ADD(s.d, INTERVAL 1 MONTH)
      AND o.payment_status = 'paid'
GROUP BY bucket
ORDER BY bucket;

Results are cached in Redis for 5 minutes, keyed by period and filter. Above roughly 100,000 orders, a nightly rollup into a daily_metrics table replaces the live aggregation; the API contract does not change when that happens.

18.3 Charts

Chart.js, following the house rules: one hue family for a single series; the categorical palette only when series are genuinely unrelated; axes always labelled with units; the y-axis always starting at zero for bar charts; no more than six series on one chart; and a "View as table" toggle on every chart so the data is reachable without sight. Money on axes is abbreviated (TZS 1.2M) and shown in full in tooltips.

18.4 Exports

ExportColumns
OrdersNumber, date, customer, phone, email, region, district, method, payment method, payment status, fulfilment status, subtotal, shipping, total, paid, items count
Order linesOrder number, date, SKU, product, attributes, unit price, quantity, line total
ProductsSKU, name, category, subcategory, brand, MPN, price, compare-at, cost, stock, reserved, status, units sold, created
Stock movementsDate, SKU, product, delta, balance after, reason, reference, actor, note
CustomersName, email, phone, verified, region, orders, lifetime value, first order, last order, consent flags
PaymentsDate, order, method, provider, reference, amount, status, payer phone, verified by
SearchesTerm, searches, average results, click-through rate, last searched

UTF-8 with a BOM so Excel opens Swahili and symbol characters correctly, comma-separated, dates in ISO 8601, money as plain integers without a currency symbol so spreadsheets can sum them. Exports over 5,000 rows run as a queued job and are delivered by an emailed download link valid 24 hours.

Part NineteenFrontend Architecture

19.1 Folder structure

apps/web/src/ ├── main.tsx entry, providers, error boundary ├── router.tsx route tree, guards, lazy boundaries ├── app/ │ ├── providers/ query client, auth, toast, currency, i18n │ └── layouts/ StorefrontLayout, AdminLayout, AccountLayout ├── features/ vertical slices — the primary organising axis │ ├── catalogue/ components, hooks, api, types │ ├── product/ │ ├── cart/ │ ├── checkout/ │ ├── orders/ │ ├── account/ │ ├── auth/ │ ├── content/ │ └── admin/ │ ├── products/ orders/ customers/ categories/ banners/ │ ├── campaigns/ settings/ staff/ analytics/ │ └── shared/ DataTable, FormLayout, PermissionGate ├── components/ cross-feature composites ├── hooks/ useDebounce, useMediaQuery, useLocalStorage… ├── lib/ apiClient, formatters, validators, analytics ├── stores/ zustand slices ├── styles/ tokens.scss, bootstrap overrides, globals └── locales/ en.json, sw.json

Features are vertical slices, not horizontal layers. Everything about checkout lives in features/checkout. A file is promoted to components/ only when a second feature imports it — moving code up when it is shared beats guessing what will be shared.

19.2 State management

StateWhereWhy
Server dataTanStack QueryProducts, orders, customers, settings. Cache keyed by request, invalidated by mutation. Directly fixes defects R4 and R5.
Cart, wishlistZustand + server syncNeeds optimistic local mutation and cross-tab sync; the server remains authoritative.
Auth sessionZustand, memory onlyAccess token never leaves memory (§11.2).
UI stateZustandOff-canvas open, modal mode, sidebar collapsed, toast queue.
Filters, pagination, tabsURL query stringShareable, bookmarkable, back-button correct. Never component state.
Form stateReact Hook FormUncontrolled inputs, minimal re-render, Zod resolver.
PreferenceslocalStorageGrid density, recently viewed, dismissed banners.
 
// stores/cart.ts — the shape ported from the reference platform's Pinia store,
// with the increment defect (R3) removed and server sync added.
interface CartStore {
  lines: CartLine[]
  notices: CartNotice[]
  isSyncing: boolean
  add(variantSku: string, quantity?: number): Promise<void>
  setQuantity(variantSku: string, quantity: number): Promise<void>
  remove(variantSku: string): Promise<void>
  clear(): Promise<void>
  hydrate(): Promise<void>      // localStorage first paint, then server truth
  merge(): Promise<MergeResult>  // after sign-in, §12.5.4
  get subtotal(): Money
  get itemCount(): number
}

19.3 Data fetching

ConcernRule
Query keysStructured arrays: ['products', {subcategory, sort, page}]. Built by a keys factory per feature so no two call sites can disagree.
Stale timesCategories and settings 30 min · products 5 min · cart 0 (always fresh) · orders 1 min · admin lists 0.
InvalidationEvery mutation declares the key prefixes it invalidates. A mutation with no invalidation list fails review.
Optimistic updatesCart quantity, wishlist toggle, admin active toggles and reordering. Always with a rollback path.
PrefetchProduct detail is prefetched on card hover or focus after a 150 ms delay; the next catalogue page is prefetched when the current one is 80% scrolled.
RetryTwice with exponential backoff for GET; never for POST, PATCH or DELETE, which is what idempotency keys are for instead.
Error handlingA single API client maps error codes to typed errors; 401 triggers one queued refresh and a replay; 403 and 5xx surface through the error boundary.

19.4 Forms and validation

One Zod schema per form, imported from packages/contracts — the same object the server validates against. Mode is onTouched then onChange once a field has errored. Server-side field errors from a 422 are mapped back onto form fields by name so they appear in the same place as client-side ones. Submit is disabled while pending and while the form is pristine. Every form guards against navigation with unsaved changes.

19.5 Error boundaries

Three levels: a root boundary rendering the 500 page with the request id; a route-level boundary so one failing route does not blank the shell; and section-level boundaries around each independently loaded home-page rail (§7.2.2), so one failed request degrades one section. Boundaries report to the error tracker with the request id, the route and a redacted breadcrumb trail.

19.6 Code splitting and performance

  • Route-level lazy loading for every route; the admin console is a single separate chunk group that a customer never downloads.
  • Heavy libraries are loaded only where used: the rich-text editor, the chart library, FilePond and Leaflet are all dynamic imports.
  • Initial storefront bundle budget: 180 KB gzipped of JavaScript. The reference platform ships a 2 MB vendor bundle on first paint; the largest single contributor is a date library that has a 20× smaller replacement.
  • Images lazy below the fold, eager for the banner and the product hero, always with explicit dimensions.
  • Fonts self-hosted, preloaded, subset, font-display: swap.
  • react-window for any list beyond 200 rows — the admin product table at scale.

19.7 Accessibility

WCAG 2.1 AA is the target and is testable. Landmark regions on every page; one h1 per page with no skipped levels; a visible skip link; focus never lost or trapped except deliberately in modals and off-canvas panels; a visible 2 px focus ring everywhere; all interactive elements reachable and operable by keyboard; 44 px minimum touch targets; live regions for cart changes, search result counts and form errors; no information conveyed by colour alone — every status pill carries text; all images have meaningful alt text or are explicitly decorative; forms use real labels, never placeholders as labels. eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y runs in CI and axe runs against every Storybook story.

19.8 Internationalisation

react-i18next, English shipped, Swahili scaffolded. All copy resolved through t() from day one — retrofitting this into a finished application costs many times what it costs now. Interpolation for names and counts; ICU plurals; dates and money through Intl with the Africa/Dar_es_Salaam zone and the configured currency. The payment instruction blocks ship in both languages immediately (§7.8.1), because that is where a mistranslation costs an order.

Part TwentyBackend Architecture

20.1 Folder structure

apps/api/src/ ├── server.ts http server, socket gateway, graceful shutdown ├── app.ts express app, middleware pipeline (§4.3) ├── config/ zod-validated environment, fails fast at boot ├── modules/ one folder per bounded context │ ├── auth/ catalogue/ cart/ orders/ payments/ │ ├── invoices/ customers/ shipping/ content/ campaigns/ │ ├── media/ search/ analytics/ settings/ audit/ │ └── each: routes.ts · controller.ts · service.ts · repository.ts │ · schemas.ts · events.ts · __tests__/ ├── middleware/ auth, rbac, validate, rateLimit, requestId, error ├── infra/ │ ├── db/ prisma client, transaction helper │ ├── cache/ redis │ ├── queue/ bullmq queues and workers │ ├── storage/ storage adapters (§15.3) │ ├── mail/ sms/ provider adapters │ ├── payments/ payment provider adapters (§13.3) │ └── realtime/ socket.io gateway ├── domain/ pure logic: state machines, pricing, SKU, stock ├── jobs/ scheduled job definitions └── lib/ logger, errors, money, phone, slug, ids

20.2 Layering

Strictly one direction: routes → controller → service → repository → Prisma. Controllers know HTTP and nothing about business rules. Services own invariants and transaction boundaries and know nothing about HTTP. Repositories know Prisma and nothing about rules. domain/ is pure and has no imports from any other layer, which is what makes the order state machine, the pricing resolver and the SKU allocator testable without a database. Violations are caught by an import-boundary lint rule.

20.3 Transactions

OperationMust be atomic across
Place orderOrder · items · address · reservations on every variant · stock movements · order event · cart clear · idempotency record
Verify paymentPayment · order payment status and lifecycle · stock deduction · movements · order event · notification enqueue
Cancel orderOrder status · reservation release · movements · order event
Create productProduct · attributes · variants · images · SKU allocation · counter updates on subcategory and category
Delete categoryPrecondition check · soft delete · counter updates · audit record
Approve reviewReview status · product review_count and average_rating · audit record

Rules: lock rows in a consistent order — always ascending by primary key — to avoid deadlock; keep transactions short and never perform network I/O inside one; enqueue side effects rather than executing them, so a failed email never rolls back a paid order; and set an explicit 5-second timeout with a retry for deadlock victims.

20.4 Error taxonomy

AppError (base: code, httpStatus, message, details?, isOperational)
├── ValidationError        422   from the Zod middleware
├── UnauthenticatedError   401
├── ForbiddenError         403
├── NotFoundError          404
├── GoneError              410
├── ConflictError          409
│   ├── PriceChangedError
│   ├── InsufficientStockError
│   └── InvalidStateTransitionError
├── RateLimitError         429
└── ExternalServiceError   502   provider unreachable or malformed

Anything else is non-operational: logged with a full stack, reported to the
error tracker, and returned to the client as a generic 500 with a request id.

20.5 Logging and observability

Structured JSON via pino. Every line carries requestId, userId where known, route, method, status and duration. A serializer redacts password, token, authorization, refreshToken, paymentNumber and any key matching /secret|key|hash/i. Levels: error for failures needing attention, warn for handled anomalies such as a rejected webhook signature, info for business events (order placed, payment verified, campaign sent), debug off in production. OpenTelemetry traces span HTTP, database and queue work, so a slow checkout can be attributed to a specific query.

20.6 Caching

CachedTTLInvalidated by
Category and subcategory tree30 minAny category or subcategory write
Public settings30 minSettings write
Shipping methods30 minShipping write
Home page rails5 minProduct or banner write
Product detail5 minWrite to that product or its variants
Analytics aggregates5 minTime only
Regions and districts24 hManual

Cart, orders, customers, payments and anything user-specific are never cached. Cache keys are namespaced with a schema version so a deployment that changes a response shape does not serve stale structures.

20.7 Configuration

One config/index.ts parses process.env through a Zod schema at boot and exports a frozen, typed object. A missing or malformed variable stops the process with a message naming the variable — never a runtime undefined three hours later. No other file may read process.env, enforced by lint. The full variable list is Appendix D.

Part Twenty-OneQuality: Testing, Performance, SEO

21.1 Test strategy

LevelTargetScope
Unit — domain95% of domain/Order state machine (every legal and illegal transition), pricing and discount resolution, SKU allocation, stock arithmetic, phone canonicalisation, slug generation, money formatting. Pure functions, no database, milliseconds to run.
Unit — components80%Every component in every state: loading, empty, error, populated. Plus an axe assertion per story.
Integration — APIEvery endpointSupertest against a real MySQL schema in Docker. Each endpoint tested for success, validation failure, unauthenticated, unauthorised, and not-found.
Integration — dataKey invariantsConcurrent add-to-cart on the last unit; double webhook delivery; duplicate idempotency key; cascade and restrict behaviour; the financial invariant of §13.6.
End-to-end7 journeysPlaywright, Chromium and mobile Chrome: guest purchase with Lipa Namba · signed-in purchase with saved address · delegated payment · admin creates a product with variants · admin verifies a payment and ships · search to purchase · registration, verification and password reset.
VisualAll storiesStorybook snapshots at three widths, diffed in CI.
LoadBefore launchk6: 200 concurrent browsers on the catalogue; 20 orders per minute sustained; the search endpoint at 100 requests per second.
SecurityEach releaseDependency audit, an automated ZAP baseline scan, and a manual authorisation matrix test asserting that a customer token is rejected by every admin route.

Test data comes from typed factories, never from fixtures copied between tests. Each test creates only what it asserts on. The database is truncated between tests, not re-migrated.

21.2 Performance budgets

MetricBudgetMeasured
Largest Contentful Paint, mobile 3G< 2.5 sLighthouse CI on every pull request
Interaction to Next Paint< 200 msField data
Cumulative Layout Shift< 0.1Lighthouse CI
Initial JS, gzipped< 180 KBBundle analysis, CI fails on regression

API p95, order creation< 800 msServer metrics

Slow query threshold> 100 msMySQL slow log with alerting

21.3 SEO

The catalogue is the acquisition channel, so the client-rendered decision (D3) must be paid for deliberately. Public routes — home, catalogue, every product, every article, every content page — are prerendered at build time and revalidated on content change, producing static HTML that crawlers and slow devices receive immediately while React hydrates over it. Beyond that: unique title and meta description per page; canonical URLs; Open Graph and Twitter cards with the product image; JSON-LD for Product, Offer, BreadcrumbList, Review, Organisation and FAQPage; a generated sitemap.xml segmented by type and refreshed nightly; a robots.txt excluding /admin, /account, /checkout, /pay and /invoice; 301s from retired slugs; and a PWA manifest with offline support for previously viewed pages.

Part Twenty-TwoDeployment and Operations

22.1 Topology

Production (single VPS is adequate to ~50k monthly sessions)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Nginx  :443  TLS (Let's Encrypt, auto-renew)            │
│    /            → SPA static build, immutable cache      │
│    /api         → 127.0.0.1:4000                         │
│    /socket.io   → 127.0.0.1:4000 (upgrade)               │
│    /media       → /var/lib/techstar/media (exec off)     │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  PM2: api ×2 (cluster)  ·  worker ×1  ·  scheduler ×1    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  MySQL 8   (local socket, 2 GB buffer pool)              │
│  Redis 7   (maxmemory 512 MB, allkeys-lru for cache DB,  │
│             noeviction for the queue DB — never evict    │
│             a job)                                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Scaling path, in order: move MySQL to its own host → add a read replica for
analytics → move media to S3 + CDN → run the API behind a load balancer.

22.2 CI/CD

on pull request        typecheck · lint · unit · integration · build
                       bundle-size check · Lighthouse CI · axe
                       npm audit (fail on high/critical)
on merge to main       build → staging → migrate → smoke → e2e
on tag v*              build → manual approval → production
                         ├─ backup database
                         ├─ prisma migrate deploy
                         ├─ pm2 reload (zero downtime)
                         ├─ smoke tests
                         └─ rollback on failure

The production build strips all non-production configuration — no development hostnames, no localhost ports (fixing R10), no source maps served publicly, though they are uploaded to the error tracker. Secrets come from the deployment environment and never from the repository.

22.3 Migrations

Prisma Migrate, forward-only, reviewed in the pull request that needs them. Rules: additive first — add a column nullable, backfill in a job, then enforce NOT NULL in a later release; never drop a column in the same release that stops writing to it; every destructive migration is preceded by a verified backup; and migrations run automatically on deploy with an advisory lock so two instances cannot race.

22.4 Backups and disaster recovery

ItemPolicy
Databasemysqldump --single-transaction nightly, plus binary logs for point-in-time recovery. Encrypted, off-site, 30 daily / 12 monthly / 7 yearly.
MediaNightly incremental sync to off-site object storage.
Restore drillQuarterly, mandatory. Restore to a scratch host and verify order counts and totals. An untested backup is not a backup.
RPO / RTO24 hours / 4 hours for a full rebuild from backup; 15 minutes / 1 hour with binary logs available.

22.5 Monitoring and runbooks

Uptime checks on /, /api/v1/health and /api/v1/health/deep (which verifies MySQL, Redis and storage). Alerts on: 5xx rate above 1% for 5 minutes; p95 latency above 1 second; queue depth above 1,000; failed webhook count above zero; disk above 80%; MySQL connections above 80% of maximum; SMS balance below the threshold; a failed nightly backup; and any financial-invariant violation. Written runbooks cover payment provider outage, SMS provider outage, database restore, stuck queue, high traffic event, and a suspected security incident — each naming who to call and what to say to customers.

22.6 Local development on XAMPP

The workstation already runs XAMPP, and its MySQL is a perfectly good development database. Start MySQL from the XAMPP control panel, create the techstar schema with utf8mb4, point DATABASE_URL at mysql://root@127.0.0.1:3306/techstar, and run migrations and the seed. phpMyAdmin at localhost/phpmyadmin is then available for inspection. Apache is not needed — Vite serves the front end and Express serves the API. Redis is the one thing XAMPP does not provide: run it under Docker, or under WSL, or use the in-memory queue driver, which is supported for development only.

Part Twenty-ThreeDelivery Plan

23.1 Phases

PhaseOutcomeContents
0FoundationsRepository, workspaces, tooling, CI. Prisma schema and migrations for all 50 tables. Seed: taxonomy, regions, districts, roles, permissions, settings. Design tokens and the primitive component set with Storybook. Auth end to end. Exit: a developer can clone, install, migrate, seed and sign in.
1CatalogueCategories, subcategories, products with variants, images and media pipeline. Admin CRUD for all of it. Storefront home, catalogue, product detail, search. Exit: the full reference catalogue shape is enterable and browsable.
2CommerceCart, wishlist, checkout, shipping methods, order creation with revalidation and reservation, order state machine, manual Lipa Namba, cash on delivery, admin order queue and payment verification, transactional email and SMS. Exit: a real order can be placed, paid and shipped.
3Accounts & contentProfile, addresses, order history, reviews, banners, articles, comments, services, partners, FAQs, content pages, moderation queues, invoices, delegated payment. Exit: feature parity with the reference platform.
4Operations & growthDashboard analytics, search analytics, campaigns, contacts, staff and roles, audit log, exports, aggregator payment adapter, prerendering and SEO, PWA. Exit: the business can be run and measured from the console.
5Hardening & launchLoad testing, security review, accessibility audit, restore drill, runbooks, staff training, content population, sender-ID registration, soft launch, launch. Exit: live.

23.2 Estimate

Ranges for one full-stack engineer working steadily, and for a team of three (two full-stack, one front-end-leaning). They assume the design system is built once in Phase 0 and reused, which is where most of the compression comes from.

PhaseSoloTeam of threeCritical path
02–3 weeks1–1.5 weeksSchema decisions; everything downstream depends on them
14–5 weeks2–2.5 weeksThe variant matrix; it is the hardest single screen
25–6 weeks2.5–3 weeksOrder state machine, stock concurrency, payment reconciliation
34–5 weeks2–2.5 weeksBreadth rather than depth; highly parallelisable
44–5 weeks2–2.5 weeksCampaigns and the aggregator integration, both with external dependencies
52–3 weeks1.5–2 weeksSender-ID registration and content population are not engineering work and must start in Phase 3
Total21–27 weeks11.5–14 weeks

23.3 Acceptance criteria

A phase is complete only when all of the following hold, not when the screens look finished.

  • Every endpoint in scope has integration tests covering success, validation, unauthenticated, unauthorised and not-found.
  • Every screen in scope has its loading, empty, error and populated states implemented and covered by a Storybook story.
  • Domain logic in scope is at 95% unit coverage.
  • Lighthouse and axe pass at the budgets in §21.2 with no new violations.
  • No high or critical dependency advisory.
  • Every admin action in scope writes an audit record.
  • The relevant end-to-end journeys pass on Chromium and mobile Chrome.
  • Any deviation from this specification is written down, with its reason, in a decision record.

23.4 Risk register

RiskImpactMitigation
SMS sender-ID registration takes weeksHighStart in Phase 3, not Phase 5. It blocks launch and cannot be accelerated. Have an email-only fallback path ready.
Manual reconciliation does not scale past ~50 orders/dayHighAggregator adapter designed in Phase 2, implemented in Phase 4. Watch the payment-lag metric; it is the trigger.
Catalogue population is slower than expectedHighCSV import in Phase 1, not later. Product entry time is a tracked metric (§3.6).
Payment provider onboarding is slow or is refusedMediumLaunch does not depend on it. Approach two providers in parallel.
Mobile money USSD menus changeMediumInstructions are editable settings, not code (§14.16). Re-verify quarterly.
Legal copy not ready at launchMediumAssign in Phase 0. Privacy and Terms need legal review, which has its own lead time.
Product photography inconsistentMediumAgree a shooting standard — white background, square, 1200 px minimum — before Phase 1 bulk entry, not after.
Scope creep into marketplace or multi-currencyMedium§3.5 states the non-goals. Changes go through a written decision record.
Single-server topology becomes a bottleneckLowThe scaling path in §22.1 is ordered and requires no re-architecture.

Appendix ACatalogue Taxonomy

The recommended launch taxonomy for a STEM and electronics retailer: 17 categories and 102 subcategories, each with its positional SKU code per §12.1. This is a structural classification of the electronics component trade, not proprietary content — it is the right starting shape, and TechStar should add, rename and reorder freely once real stock is known. Codes are assigned so that a subcategory code always begins with its parent category code. Gaps in the sequence are intentional and reserved.

CodeCategorySubsSubcategories

01Hardware Development Tools & Platforms40100 Compatible Arduino® boards & accessories · 0101 Programmers and debuggers · 0102 Development kits · 0111 Raspberry Pi boards & accessories
02Integrated Circuits90200 4000 Series · 0201 7400 Series · 0202 OpAmps · 0203 A/D & D/A Converters · 0204 Memory chips · 0205 Timers · 0206 Drivers · 0207 Programmable devices · 0208 Special function

13Security & Surveillance21300 CCTV Cameras · 1301 Access control systems
14Industrial Control & Automation11400 Programmable Logic Controllers
16Heating, Cooling & Air Quality41600 Humidifiers · 1601 Dehumidifiers · 1604 Heat sinks · 1605 Heat shrink tubing & heat sleeves
17Electronics Projects11700 Automatic Irrigation Pump

A.1 Notes on applying this taxonomy

  • Depth is fixed at two levels by the SKU scheme and by the mega-menu design (§9.3.8). Resist a third level; split a subcategory instead.
  • Reserve codes as you go. Leaving gaps — using 00, 10, 20 for the first three subcategories — lets related items be inserted later without renumbering, and SKUs are immutable once assigned.
  • Empty subcategories are hidden from storefront navigation (§6.4.2) but remain fully manageable in the console, so the tree can be built out ahead of stock arriving.
  • Name for the buyer, not the engineer. "Passive devices" is correct and opaque; consider "Resistors, capacitors & inductors" for the STEM half of the audience, with the technical term as a tag.
  • Seed order matters. Categories must be created before subcategories, and both before products, because SKU allocation reads the parent (Appendix F).
